# Seri 9 - View Migration

## 1. Tujuan Seri Ini

Seri ini membahas cara mengelola **PostgreSQL View** dalam database migration untuk microservice berbasis:

```text id="w6q82z"
Jersey / JAX-RS
MyBatis
PostgreSQL
Flyway / Liquibase
Camunda
CI/CD
Kubernetes
```

View sering dianggap hanya sebagai “query shortcut”. Dalam production system, itu terlalu dangkal. View bisa menjadi:

- kontrak baca untuk MyBatis mapper;
- boundary akses data;
- abstraction layer untuk query kompleks;
- source untuk reporting;
- dependency untuk materialized view;
- dependency untuk function/procedure;
- bagian dari compatibility strategy saat schema berubah.

PostgreSQL `CREATE VIEW` membuat view dari query `SELECT` atau `VALUES`, dan `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` dapat mengganti definisi view yang sudah ada, tetapi ada batasan penting: kolom baru dapat ditambahkan di akhir list, sedangkan nama, urutan, dan tipe kolom yang sudah ada harus sama. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createview.html))

---

## 2. Mental Model

```text id="bzepiw"
View Migration Mental Model
────────────────────────────────────────────

Base Tables
  │
  ├─ enforcement_case
  ├─ case_status_reference
  └─ case_assignment
       │
       ▼
PostgreSQL View
  │
  ├─ v_case_summary
  ├─ v_case_queue
  └─ v_case_assignment_workload
       │
       ▼
Consumers
  │
  ├─ MyBatis Mapper
  ├─ Jersey API
  ├─ Reporting query
  ├─ Materialized view
  ├─ Procedure/function
  └─ Camunda worker/batch job
```

Core rule:

```text id="riep58"
Jika view dikonsumsi oleh aplikasi, view adalah contract.
Bukan sekadar implementation detail.
```

---

## 3. Kapan View Berguna?

| Use Case | View Cocok? | Catatan |
|---|---:|---|
| Query join berulang | Ya | Mengurangi duplikasi SQL di mapper/report. |
| Read model sederhana | Ya | Cocok untuk summary/projection. |
| Backward compatibility saat rename column | Ya | Bisa expose nama lama dan baru sementara. |
| Row filtering untuk akses terbatas | Ya, hati-hati | Pertimbangkan `security_barrier` / `security_invoker`. |
| Heavy aggregation | Kadang | Kalau mahal dan sering, materialized view mungkin lebih cocok. |
| Business logic kompleks | Hati-hati | Jangan menyembunyikan logic kritikal tanpa test. |
| Write API | Biasanya tidak | Updatable view ada, tapi perlu governance ketat. |

---

## 4. Basic View

```sql id="48zbug"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    c.status,
    sr.label AS status_label,
    c.priority,
    c.created_at,
    c.updated_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
JOIN case_app.case_status_reference sr
    ON sr.code = c.status;
```

Praktik baik:

```text id="iuj90x"
- selalu schema-qualified;
- selalu explicit column list;
- hindari SELECT *;
- alias kolom harus stabil;
- output view dianggap contract;
- dependency harus diketahui.
```

PostgreSQL documentation memberi peringatan bahwa nama dan tipe kolom view harus dibuat secara eksplisit; contoh `SELECT 'Hello World'` tanpa alias dianggap bad form karena nama kolom default bisa menjadi `?column?`, dan `SELECT *` pada view hanya mengambil kolom yang ada saat view dibuat, bukan otomatis menambahkan kolom baru di table pada masa depan. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createview.html))

---

## 5. View sebagai Contract untuk MyBatis

Mapper:

```xml id="1pxslp"
<select id="findCaseSummaryById" resultMap="CaseSummaryResultMap">
    SELECT
        id,
        case_number,
        status,
        status_label,
        priority,
        created_at,
        updated_at
    FROM case_app.v_case_summary
    WHERE id = #{id}
</select>
```

Jika view berubah menjadi:

```sql id="4wrh6x"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.reference_number,
    c.status,
    sr.label AS status_label,
    c.priority,
    c.created_at,
    c.updated_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
JOIN case_app.case_status_reference sr
    ON sr.code = c.status;
```

Mapper lama akan rusak karena `case_number` hilang.

### Rule

```text id="nb2s5s"
Jangan hapus atau rename kolom view yang dipakai mapper
dalam release yang sama dengan app change.
```

Gunakan additive change atau versioned view.

---

## 6. `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` - Safe dan Tidak Safe

### Safe: Additive Column at End

```sql id="nl0h48"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    c.status,
    sr.label AS status_label,
    c.priority,
    c.created_at,
    c.updated_at,
    c.risk_score
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
JOIN case_app.case_status_reference sr
    ON sr.code = c.status;
```

Ini aman relatif jika kolom baru ditambahkan di akhir, karena consumer lama masih bisa membaca kolom lama.

### Unsafe: Rename Existing Column

```sql id="xj0ok7"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.reference_number,
    c.status
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c;
```

Ini breaking jika sebelumnya view mengekspos `case_number`.

### Unsafe: Change Type Existing Column

Misalnya sebelumnya:

```text id="f6wldd"
risk_score INTEGER
```

lalu menjadi:

```text id="bmc8sr"
risk_score NUMERIC(5,2)
```

Consumer Java/MyBatis bisa terdampak mapping type.

### Practical Interpretation

Karena `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` mensyaratkan existing columns tetap punya nama, urutan, dan tipe yang sama, strategi evolusi aman biasanya adalah **menambahkan kolom baru**, bukan mengganti kolom lama di tempat. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createview.html))

---

## 7. Flyway Strategy for View

View biasanya cocok sebagai **repeatable migration**.

```text id="g2o4tm"
src/main/resources/db/migration/repeatable/
  R__020_case_view_case_summary.sql
```

Isi:

```sql id="z6jyj3"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    c.status,
    sr.label AS status_label,
    c.priority,
    c.created_at,
    c.updated_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
JOIN case_app.case_status_reference sr
    ON sr.code = c.status;
```

### Kenapa Repeatable?

Karena view adalah **definitional object**. Saat definisi berubah, kita ingin file view menjadi source of truth.

### Naming dengan Dependency Order

```text id="srgn07"
R__010_case_fn_calculate_sla.sql
R__020_case_view_case_summary.sql
R__030_case_view_case_queue.sql
R__040_case_matview_daily_metrics.sql
```

Jika view memanggil function, function harus dibuat dulu.

---

## 8. Liquibase Strategy for View

Gunakan wrapper changeset + `sqlFile` + `runOnChange`.

```yaml id="j2mkdo"
databaseChangeLog:
  - changeSet:
      id: 501-case-view-case-summary
      author: case-service-team
      runOnChange: true
      labels: view,case-read-model
      changes:
        - sqlFile:
            path: src/main/resources/db/changelog/500-views/sql/v_case_summary.sql
            splitStatements: false
            stripComments: false
      rollback:
        - sql:
            sql: |
              DROP VIEW IF EXISTS case_app.v_case_summary;
```

SQL file:

```sql id="stpbyx"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    c.status,
    sr.label AS status_label,
    c.priority,
    c.created_at,
    c.updated_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
JOIN case_app.case_status_reference sr
    ON sr.code = c.status;
```

`CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` mengganti defining `SELECT` rule dan view options/check option, tetapi ownership, permissions, dan non-SELECT rules tetap tidak berubah; ini berarti grant/owner tidak otomatis reset hanya karena definisi view diganti. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createview.html))

---

## 9. Versioned View vs Repeatable View

| Strategy | Cocok Untuk | Kelebihan | Risiko |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Repeatable view** | Definisi view yang stabil namanya. | File selalu mencerminkan definisi terbaru. | Breaking change bisa terselip jika review lemah. |
| **Versioned view** | Perubahan kontrak besar. | Konsumen lama dan baru bisa coexist. | Banyak object versi lama perlu cleanup. |
| **Additive same view** | Tambah kolom baru tanpa hapus lama. | Simple dan backward-compatible. | View bisa membesar dan perlu contract cleanup. |
| **New view v2** | Rename, type change, semantic change. | Aman untuk consumer lama. | Butuh migration consumer bertahap. |

### Rule

```text id="wd3hkh"
Small additive change:
  repeatable same view

Breaking contract change:
  create v2 view or additive compatibility first
```

---

## 10. Pattern: Add Column to View

### Requirement

Tambahkan `risk_score` ke case summary.

### Base Table Migration

```sql id="daw9px"
ALTER TABLE case_app.enforcement_case
ADD COLUMN risk_score INTEGER;
```

### View Migration

```sql id="a4ygme"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    c.status,
    sr.label AS status_label,
    c.priority,
    c.created_at,
    c.updated_at,
    c.risk_score
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
JOIN case_app.case_status_reference sr
    ON sr.code = c.status;
```

### MyBatis Update

```xml id="6434mp"
<result property="riskScore" column="risk_score"/>
```

### Safety

Old mapper remains safe because existing columns remain present.

---

## 11. Pattern: Rename Column via View Compatibility

### Requirement

Rename base table column:

```text id="2bnwg9"
case_number → reference_number
```

### Expand Table

```sql id="udwowr"
ALTER TABLE case_app.enforcement_case
ADD COLUMN reference_number TEXT;
```

### Compatibility View

```sql id="g87xo8"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    c.reference_number,
    c.status,
    sr.label AS status_label,
    c.priority,
    c.created_at,
    c.updated_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
JOIN case_app.case_status_reference sr
    ON sr.code = c.status;
```

Consumer lama tetap pakai `case_number`.

Consumer baru mulai pakai `reference_number`.

### Contract Later

Setelah semua consumer pindah:

```sql id="b5zmzx"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.reference_number,
    c.status,
    sr.label AS status_label,
    c.priority,
    c.created_at,
    c.updated_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
JOIN case_app.case_status_reference sr
    ON sr.code = c.status;
```

Tetapi ini harus dilakukan setelah mapper/reporting/API lama sudah tidak menggunakan `case_number`.

---

## 12. Pattern: Create `v2` View for Breaking Change

### Existing View

```sql id="zye6at"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    c.status,
    c.priority
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c;
```

### New Contract

```sql id="18b44i"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary_v2 AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.reference_number,
    c.status,
    c.priority,
    c.risk_score
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c;
```

### Consumer Migration

```text id="wgoc7u"
Old mapper:
  SELECT ... FROM case_app.v_case_summary

New mapper:
  SELECT ... FROM case_app.v_case_summary_v2
```

### Cleanup

Drop old view only after:

- old app not running;
- old mapper removed;
- report/dashboard migrated;
- stored procedures no longer reference old view;
- Camunda workers/batch jobs checked.

---

## 13. Pattern: View as Backward Compatibility Layer

View can preserve old column names while table evolves.

### Base Table New Shape

```sql id="cdfvbg"
case_app.enforcement_case (
    id UUID,
    reference_number TEXT,
    status TEXT
)
```

### Compatibility View

```sql id="57ak9v"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_legacy_case AS
SELECT
    id,
    reference_number AS case_number,
    status
FROM case_app.enforcement_case;
```

This can support old reporting or old mapper temporarily.

### Warning

Compatibility views should have explicit sunset plan.

```text id="px8v6f"
temporary compatibility without cleanup
becomes permanent technical debt.
```

---

## 14. Avoid `SELECT *` in Views

Bad:

```sql id="00q6lz"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT *
FROM case_app.enforcement_case;
```

Problems:

- output contract unclear;
- column order unclear;
- consumer coupling hidden;
- new table columns do not automatically become part of existing view;
- MyBatis mapping can become fragile.

PostgreSQL documentation explicitly notes that a view created with `SELECT *` contains the columns present at creation time; columns added later to the base table do not automatically become part of the view. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createview.html))

Better:

```sql id="3arrhg"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    c.status,
    c.created_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c;
```

---

## 15. View Column Naming Rules

Always alias expressions.

Bad:

```sql id="cfe5h3"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_metrics AS
SELECT
    status,
    count(*)
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
GROUP BY status;
```

Better:

```sql id="w79em7"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_metrics AS
SELECT
    status,
    COUNT(*) AS case_count
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
GROUP BY status;
```

Reason:

```text id="vfdd42"
Expression names are API-like output names.
Do not let PostgreSQL invent them.
```

---

## 16. Dependency Ordering

Recommended order:

```text id="3pga0l"
1. Base schema
2. Base tables
3. Reference data
4. Functions used by view
5. Views
6. Views depending on other views
7. Materialized views depending on views
8. Grants
9. Tests / validation
```

Example:

```text id="1d02bp"
R__010_case_fn_format_status.sql
R__020_case_view_case_summary.sql
R__030_case_view_case_queue.sql
R__040_case_matview_case_metrics.sql
```

If a view depends on a function, the function must exist before the view is created.

---

## 17. Detect View Dependencies

### Views Depending on Table

```sql id="l4gisa"
SELECT
    dependent_ns.nspname AS dependent_schema,
    dependent_view.relname AS dependent_view,
    source_ns.nspname AS source_schema,
    source_table.relname AS source_table
FROM pg_depend d
JOIN pg_rewrite r
    ON r.oid = d.objid
JOIN pg_class dependent_view
    ON dependent_view.oid = r.ev_class
JOIN pg_class source_table
    ON source_table.oid = d.refobjid
JOIN pg_namespace dependent_ns
    ON dependent_ns.oid = dependent_view.relnamespace
JOIN pg_namespace source_ns
    ON source_ns.oid = source_table.relnamespace
WHERE source_ns.nspname = 'case_app'
  AND source_table.relname = 'enforcement_case';
```

PostgreSQL system catalog `pg_depend` records dependency relationships between database objects, and that information is used by `DROP` commands to decide what must be dropped with `CASCADE` or blocked with `RESTRICT`. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/catalog-pg-depend.html))

### View Definition Search

```sql id="huc0li"
SELECT
    schemaname,
    viewname,
    definition
FROM pg_views
WHERE schemaname = 'case_app'
  AND definition ILIKE '%case_number%';
```

Use both dependency query and text search. Dependency query tells object relationships; text search helps detect specific columns/expressions.

---

## 18. `DROP VIEW`: Avoid CASCADE by Default

Dangerous:

```sql id="xek4fm"
DROP VIEW case_app.v_case_summary CASCADE;
```

`CASCADE` automatically drops objects that depend on the view, including dependent views and objects depending on those objects; `RESTRICT` refuses the drop if dependent objects exist and is the default behavior. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-dropview.html))

Safer:

```sql id="1gf9fg"
DROP VIEW IF EXISTS case_app.v_case_summary RESTRICT;
```

### Rule

```text id="73ojdf"
Do not use CASCADE in production view migration
unless every dependent object is explicitly listed and approved.
```

---

## 19. Grants and Ownership

Views often serve as access boundary.

```sql id="x4v0t7"
GRANT SELECT ON case_app.v_case_summary TO case_app_runtime;
```

If runtime user should access view but not base table, be clear about permission model.

By default, access to underlying base relations referenced in a view is determined by the view owner’s permissions; with `security_invoker=true`, access to underlying base relations is checked using the privileges of the user executing the query. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createview.html))

### Ownership Pattern

```text id="3xm1yp"
case_app_migration owns view
case_app_runtime gets SELECT on view
case_app_runtime may or may not get SELECT on base table
depending on security model
```

---

## 20. `security_invoker`

Example:

```sql id="o4k6uv"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary
WITH (security_invoker = true)
AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    c.status
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c;
```

Use when you want underlying table permissions and row-level security to behave as if the caller accessed the base tables directly.

PostgreSQL documentation states that if `security_invoker` is `true`, base relation permissions are checked against the user executing the query, and row-level security policies and permissions of the invoking user are used when underlying relations have RLS enabled. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createview.html))

### Practical Consequence

| Mode | Base Table Permission Checked As |
|---|---|
| Default view | View owner |
| `security_invoker=true` | Querying user |

This matters for RLS and least privilege design.

---

## 21. `security_barrier`

Example:

```sql id="s5ngde"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_public_case
WITH (security_barrier = true)
AS
SELECT
    id,
    case_number,
    status
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
WHERE is_public = true;
```

Use when the view is intended to enforce security filtering and you need to reduce risk of user-supplied functions/operators observing rows that should have been filtered.

PostgreSQL documentation says `security_barrier` should be applied when a view needs to provide row-level security, but it can perform far worse than normal views because faster plans may be rejected if they could compromise security. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/rules-privileges.html))

### Practical Rule

```text id="h2a9nw"
Do not enable security_barrier for performance-neutral reasons.
Use it when the view is part of a security boundary.
```

---

## 22. Alter View Options

PostgreSQL `ALTER VIEW` can rename a view, move it to another schema, change owner, rename columns, set/reset view options, and alter defaults for updatable view columns; supported view options include `check_option`, `security_barrier`, and `security_invoker`. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-alterview.html))

Example:

```sql id="7mbz05"
ALTER VIEW case_app.v_case_summary
SET (security_invoker = true);
```

Example rename:

```sql id="4kufj2"
ALTER VIEW case_app.v_case_summary
RENAME TO v_case_summary_legacy;
```

### Caution

Renaming a view is breaking for consumers using the old view name.

Prefer:

```text id="r0oa70"
1. Create new view
2. Migrate consumers
3. Drop old view later
```

---

## 23. Updatable Views

PostgreSQL supports automatically updatable simple views under specific conditions, such as having exactly one item in the `FROM` list and not using top-level `WITH`, `DISTINCT`, `GROUP BY`, `HAVING`, `LIMIT`, `OFFSET`, set operations, aggregates, window functions, or set-returning functions in the select list. More complex views are read-only unless implemented with `INSTEAD OF` triggers or rules. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createview.html))

### Practical Recommendation

For this stack:

```text id="ikgvmt"
Use views primarily as read contracts.
Avoid using updatable views for core write paths
unless there is a strong governance reason.
```

Why?

- MyBatis already gives explicit SQL write control;
- updatable view behavior can confuse ownership;
- `INSTEAD OF` triggers can hide write behavior;
- debugging write issues becomes harder.

---

## 24. `WITH CHECK OPTION`

For updatable views, `WITH CHECK OPTION` can prevent inserted/updated rows from violating the view’s `WHERE` condition. PostgreSQL supports `LOCAL` and `CASCADED`; if neither is specified, `CASCADED` is assumed. It is only supported for automatically updatable views and not recursive views. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createview.html))

Example:

```sql id="wjj807"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_open_case AS
SELECT
    id,
    case_number,
    status
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
WHERE status = 'OPEN'
WITH CASCADED CHECK OPTION;
```

### Practical Use

Useful when:

- view is intentionally updatable;
- you want writes through the view to remain visible through the view;
- you have strong reason to use view as write boundary.

Otherwise, keep writes explicit in MyBatis mapper.

---

## 25. View and Row-Level Security

If PostgreSQL RLS is enabled on base tables, view behavior must be reviewed carefully.

Default view execution can use the view owner’s permissions/policies for underlying tables. With `security_invoker=true`, PostgreSQL applies the invoking user’s permissions and RLS behavior as though base tables were accessed directly. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createview.html))

### Review Questions

| Question | Why |
|---|---|
| Does base table use RLS? | View may bypass intended user-level filtering if owner policy applies. |
| Should caller need base table privileges? | If yes, consider `security_invoker`. |
| Is view used as security boundary? | Consider `security_barrier`. |
| Are functions called by view safe? | Function privileges and `SECURITY DEFINER` matter. |
| Does runtime role only need view access? | Grant strategy must match security model. |

---

## 26. Function Calls Inside View

If a view calls functions, users need permission to call those functions. PostgreSQL docs state that functions in a view execute according to the function’s own `SECURITY INVOKER` or `SECURITY DEFINER` behavior, and this should not be confused with view `security_invoker`. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createview.html))

Example:

```sql id="erjff1"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days(c.created_at) AS age_days
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c;
```

Checklist:

```text id="d29246"
- function exists before view
- runtime role can execute function
- function volatility is correct
- SECURITY DEFINER functions are reviewed
- function does not leak hidden data
```

---

## 27. Performance Considerations

View does not automatically make query faster. It is usually an abstraction over a query.

### Query Using View

```sql id="0flqo0"
EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)
SELECT *
FROM case_app.v_case_summary
WHERE status = 'OPEN'
ORDER BY created_at DESC
LIMIT 50;
```

### Base Index Needed

```sql id="cjtsm1"
CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY idx_enforcement_case_status_created_at
ON case_app.enforcement_case (status, created_at DESC);
```

### Rule

```text id="kxpcbt"
Optimize base tables and query plan.
Do not assume view is a performance feature.
```

If query is heavy aggregation and read frequently, consider materialized view, which is next series.

---

## 28. View Testing Strategy

### Test 1 - View Exists

```sql id="1gal34"
SELECT EXISTS (
    SELECT 1
    FROM information_schema.views
    WHERE table_schema = 'case_app'
      AND table_name = 'v_case_summary'
);
```

### Test 2 - Columns Match Contract

```sql id="dc9oxs"
SELECT
    column_name,
    data_type,
    ordinal_position
FROM information_schema.columns
WHERE table_schema = 'case_app'
  AND table_name = 'v_case_summary'
ORDER BY ordinal_position;
```

Expected:

```text id="3ww7s6"
1 id
2 case_number
3 status
4 status_label
5 priority
6 created_at
7 updated_at
```

### Test 3 - Mapper Query Runs

```xml id="pfxy2y"
<select id="findCaseSummaryById" resultMap="CaseSummaryResultMap">
    SELECT
        id,
        case_number,
        status,
        status_label,
        priority,
        created_at,
        updated_at
    FROM case_app.v_case_summary
    WHERE id = #{id}
</select>
```

Integration test must run against migrated PostgreSQL, not mock DB only.

### Test 4 - No `SELECT *`

CI grep:

```bash id="j6n2xg"
grep -RniE "CREATE( OR REPLACE)? VIEW.*|SELECT \*" src/main/resources/db
```

Better build a SQL linter if the repository is large.

---

## 29. View Migration Review Checklist

| Question | Why |
|---|---|
| Is this view consumed by MyBatis? | Mapper contract risk. |
| Is this view consumed by API/reporting? | Output contract risk. |
| Are existing columns preserved? | `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` compatibility. |
| Are new columns appended? | Safer additive change. |
| Does the view use `SELECT *`? | Avoid hidden contract drift. |
| Does it depend on functions? | Function order/privilege/security. |
| Does it depend on another view? | Ordering and cascade risk. |
| Does it need grants? | Runtime access. |
| Is RLS involved? | `security_invoker` review. |
| Is view used as security boundary? | `security_barrier` review. |
| Does it need a v2 view? | Breaking changes should not replace in place. |
| Does it affect materialized view? | Refresh/index dependency. |
| Is performance tested? | View can hide expensive query. |

---

## 30. CI/CD Gates for View Changes

```text id="7tsqde"
Pull Request
  ├─ Detect changed view SQL
  ├─ Run migration on fresh PostgreSQL
  ├─ Validate view creation
  ├─ Check output column contract
  ├─ Run MyBatis mapper tests
  ├─ Run API integration tests
  ├─ Run EXPLAIN for critical views
  ├─ Detect SELECT *
  ├─ Detect DROP VIEW CASCADE
  └─ Require owner approval for breaking view changes
```

### Dangerous Pattern Detector

```bash id="a2xg4h"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

ROOT="${1:-src/main/resources/db}"

if grep -RniE "DROP VIEW .*CASCADE" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "DROP VIEW CASCADE detected. Requires explicit DB dependency review."
  exit 1
fi

if grep -RniE "CREATE( OR REPLACE)? VIEW" "$ROOT" | grep -i "SELECT \*"; then
  echo "SELECT * in view detected. Use explicit column list."
  exit 1
fi
```

---

## 31. Ownership Model

| View Type | Owner | Reviewer |
|---|---|---|
| API read model view | API/domain team | Tech lead + DB reviewer |
| Reporting view | Reporting/data owner | Data/DB reviewer |
| Security-filtered view | Security/platform owner | Security reviewer |
| Compatibility view | Migration owner | Tech lead |
| View used by materialized view | Reporting/domain owner | DB reviewer |
| View used by Camunda worker | Workflow/domain owner | Camunda/process reviewer |

### Rule

```text id="ci1i8h"
No orphan views.
Every view must have an owner and known consumers.
```

---

## 32. Documentation Template for View

```md id="yuj0qr"
# View: case_app.v_case_summary

## Purpose
Read model for case summary API and MyBatis mapper.

## Owner
case-service-team

## Consumers
- CaseMapper.findCaseSummaryById
- CaseResource GET /cases/{id}/summary
- case daily report

## Base Objects
- case_app.enforcement_case
- case_app.case_status_reference

## Contract Columns
| Column | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | Case ID |
| case_number | text | Stable case number |
| status | text | Case status code |
| status_label | text | Human-readable status |
| priority | text | Case priority |
| created_at | timestamptz | Creation timestamp |
| updated_at | timestamptz | Last update timestamp |

## Security
Runtime role has SELECT on view.
Base table access: explain whether granted or not.

## Change Policy
Additive changes are allowed.
Breaking changes require v2 view or migration plan.
```

---

## 33. Safe Evolution Examples

### Example A - Add Non-Breaking Column

```text id="kpbwb3"
Status: safe if appended and nullable/optional
Strategy: repeatable view update
```

```sql id="dyxacu"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    c.status,
    sr.label AS status_label,
    c.priority,
    c.created_at,
    c.updated_at,
    c.risk_score
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
JOIN case_app.case_status_reference sr
    ON sr.code = c.status;
```

---

### Example B - Rename Output Column

```text id="8xgjvy"
Status: breaking
Strategy: expose both columns first
```

```sql id="bivb8h"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    c.reference_number,
    c.status
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c;
```

Later, after consumers migrate, remove `case_number`.

---

### Example C - Major Semantic Change

```text id="z3ih3f"
Status: breaking
Strategy: create v2 view
```

```sql id="g0kjm5"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary_v2 AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.reference_number,
    c.status,
    c.risk_score,
    case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days(c.created_at) AS age_days
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c;
```

---

## 34. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Why Dangerous | Better |
|---|---|---|
| `SELECT *` in view | Contract unclear and columns do not auto-refresh as expected. | Explicit column list. |
| Rename view column in place | Breaks MyBatis/report consumers. | Add new column or v2 view. |
| Change existing column type | Breaks Java mapping. | Add new column/view v2. |
| `DROP VIEW CASCADE` | Can drop dependent objects. | Use RESTRICT and explicit dependency cleanup. |
| View uses function not granted to runtime | Runtime query fails. | Grant execute or adjust ownership/security. |
| View used as security boundary without review | Data leakage risk. | Review `security_invoker` / `security_barrier` / RLS. |
| Complex view with no performance test | Slow endpoint hidden behind abstraction. | EXPLAIN and base index review. |
| Compatibility view with no sunset | Permanent debt. | Document cleanup release. |
| View and mapper changed without integration test | Runtime failure. | Test against migrated PostgreSQL. |

---

## 35. Practical Recommendation for Your Stack

Untuk stack **Jersey/JAX-RS + MyBatis + PostgreSQL**, saya rekomendasikan:

```text id="nbb50n"
1. Treat every app-consumed view as a read contract.
2. Use repeatable migration for normal view definition.
3. Use v2 view for breaking changes.
4. Never use SELECT * in view.
5. Avoid DROP VIEW CASCADE in production.
6. Keep view output columns stable.
7. Add new columns at the end where possible.
8. Test MyBatis mapper against migrated DB.
9. Review RLS/security_invoker/security_barrier if view is access boundary.
10. Document owner, consumers, base objects, and contract columns.
```

---

## 36. Ringkasan Seri 9

| Area | Kesimpulan |
|---|---|
| View | Query object yang bisa menjadi contract internal. |
| `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` | Cocok untuk additive/compatible changes, bukan breaking contract. |
| Existing columns | Nama, urutan, dan tipe harus dijaga. |
| `SELECT *` | Hindari; output view harus eksplisit. |
| MyBatis | View column adalah mapper contract. |
| Breaking change | Gunakan additive compatibility atau `v2` view. |
| Flyway | View cocok sebagai repeatable migration. |
| Liquibase | Gunakan `sqlFile` + `runOnChange`. |
| Dependency | Urutkan function → view → dependent view/materialized view. |
| Drop view | Hindari `CASCADE`; default `RESTRICT` lebih aman. |
| Security | Review `security_invoker`, `security_barrier`, RLS, grants, function privileges. |
| Performance | View bukan performance optimization otomatis. |
| Testing | Test columns, mapper, API, dependency, and query plan. |

---

Rekomendasi materi selanjutnya:

**Seri 10 - Materialized View Migration**

Fokusnya: `CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW`, `WITH DATA` vs `WITH NO DATA`, refresh strategy, `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY`, unique index requirement, stale data contract, scheduling refresh, dependency ordering, grants, dan safe evolution tanpa membuat API/reporting membaca data kosong atau stale tanpa sadar.

**Sources:**

- [PostgreSQL: Documentation: 18: CREATE VIEW](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createview.html)

7/13/2026, 7:31:51 PM

Saya lanjut ke **Seri 10 dari 18**: Materialized View Migration. Fokusnya bukan hanya syntax, tetapi kontrak freshness, refresh, index, dependency, dan risiko saat dipakai oleh API/reporting.

Kita lanjut ke **Seri 10 dari 18**.

# Seri 10 - Materialized View Migration

## 1. Tujuan Seri Ini

Seri ini membahas cara mengelola **PostgreSQL Materialized View** dalam database migration untuk microservice berbasis:

```text id="jd1z12"
Jersey / JAX-RS
MyBatis
PostgreSQL
Flyway / Liquibase
Camunda
CI/CD
Kubernetes
```

Materialized view berbeda dari view biasa.

View biasa:

```text id="81n3vu"
Query dievaluasi saat dibaca.
```

Materialized view:

```text id="unfze1"
Hasil query disimpan secara fisik dan harus di-refresh.
```

PostgreSQL menjelaskan bahwa materialized view memakai rule system seperti view, tetapi menyimpan hasil query dalam bentuk table-like; datanya bisa di-refresh dengan `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW`. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/rules-materializedviews.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Core rule:

```text id="1j1w2v"
Materialized view is not only schema.
It is schema + data freshness + refresh operation + index strategy.
```

---

## 2. Mental Model

```text id="x4lyyn"
Materialized View Mental Model
────────────────────────────────────────────

Base Tables
  │
  ├─ enforcement_case
  ├─ case_status_reference
  └─ case_assignment
       │
       ▼
Materialized View Definition
  │
  └─ mv_case_daily_metrics
       │
       ├─ Stored result data
       ├─ Indexes
       ├─ Refresh process
       ├─ Freshness SLA
       └─ Grants / ownership
              │
              ▼
Consumers
  ├─ MyBatis Mapper
  ├─ Jersey API
  ├─ Dashboard/report
  ├─ Batch job
  └─ Camunda worker / escalation query
```

Materialized view tidak otomatis berubah ketika base table berubah. Ia harus di-refresh secara eksplisit.

---

## 3. Kapan Materialized View Cocok?

| Use Case | Cocok? | Catatan |
|---|---:|---|
| Heavy aggregation yang sering dibaca | Ya | Misalnya dashboard counts, SLA metrics, aging buckets. |
| Query join kompleks yang mahal | Ya | Jika data boleh stale. |
| Reporting snapshot | Ya | Jika freshness jelas. |
| API read model yang butuh latency rendah | Kadang | Hanya jika stale data diterima. |
| Real-time transactional read | Tidak cocok | Gunakan table/query/index biasa. |
| Authorization decision real-time | Biasanya tidak | Stale permission berbahaya. |
| Workflow critical transition | Hati-hati | Stale data bisa salah memicu escalation. |

### Rule

```text id="l4dldl"
Use materialized view when read performance matters
and stale data is explicitly acceptable.
```

---

## 4. Basic Syntax

```sql id="jcbek1"
CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics AS
SELECT
    status,
    COUNT(*) AS case_count,
    max(updated_at) AS latest_update_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
GROUP BY status;
```

`CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW` mendefinisikan materialized view dari query; query dijalankan dan hasilnya mengisi materialized view saat command dijalankan, kecuali memakai `WITH NO DATA`. Materialized view kemudian bisa di-refresh dengan `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW`. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-creatematerializedview.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 5. `WITH DATA` vs `WITH NO DATA`

### `WITH DATA`

```sql id="ljfix2"
CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics AS
SELECT
    status,
    COUNT(*) AS case_count
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
GROUP BY status
WITH DATA;
```

Artinya:

```text id="dxxunc"
Definition dibuat dan query langsung dijalankan untuk mengisi data.
```

Cocok jika:

- base table kecil;
- query ringan;
- environment local/test;
- migration window aman.

Risiko di production:

- migration bisa lama;
- lock/IO tinggi;
- deployment job timeout;
- refresh cost terjadi saat migration.

---

### `WITH NO DATA`

```sql id="5ymboo"
CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics AS
SELECT
    status,
    COUNT(*) AS case_count
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
GROUP BY status
WITH NO DATA;
```

Artinya:

```text id="8u7wyg"
Definition dibuat, tetapi materialized view belum populated.
```

Materialized view yang dibuat `WITH NO DATA` tidak langsung bisa dipakai seperti data siap baca; perlu `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW` sebelum consumer mengandalkannya.

### Practical Rule

```text id="cd9xra"
Production default:
  CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW ... WITH NO DATA

Then:
  create indexes/grants
  refresh in controlled job/window
  only then enable consumers
```

---

## 6. Basic Refresh

```sql id="35hhkd"
REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;
```

`REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW` mengganti isi materialized view sepenuhnya. Jika memakai `WITH DATA`, view dibiarkan dalam state scannable; jika memakai `WITH NO DATA`, view ditandai tidak bisa di-scan. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-refreshmaterializedview.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

### Warning

Refresh biasa dapat membuat reader terblokir atau tidak mendapatkan behavior non-blocking yang diharapkan. Untuk production reader aktif, pertimbangkan `CONCURRENTLY`.

---

## 7. Concurrent Refresh

```sql id="hqfevq"
REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;
```

PostgreSQL membatasi `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY`: materialized view harus sudah populated, dan harus memiliki setidaknya satu **UNIQUE index** yang hanya memakai nama kolom dan mencakup semua row; index tersebut tidak boleh expression index atau partial index dengan `WHERE`. Selain itu, hanya satu refresh yang boleh berjalan untuk satu materialized view pada satu waktu. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-refreshmaterializedview.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

### Implikasi

Agar bisa refresh concurrently:

```sql id="opgsoh"
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_mv_case_status_metrics_status
ON case_app.mv_case_status_metrics (status);
```

Lalu:

```sql id="vn3v88"
REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;
```

---

## 8. Unique Index Requirement

### Valid Unique Index

```sql id="lvod7x"
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_mv_case_status_metrics_status
ON case_app.mv_case_status_metrics (status);
```

Valid karena:

- unique;
- hanya memakai column name;
- mencakup semua row;
- tidak partial;
- bukan expression index.

### Tidak Valid untuk Concurrent Refresh

Expression index:

```sql id="jafc92"
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_mv_case_status_metrics_lower_status
ON case_app.mv_case_status_metrics (lower(status));
```

Partial index:

```sql id="ctvveb"
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_mv_case_status_metrics_active
ON case_app.mv_case_status_metrics (status)
WHERE case_count > 0;
```

Keduanya tidak memenuhi syarat `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY` karena PostgreSQL mensyaratkan unique index yang hanya memakai nama kolom dan mencakup semua row. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-refreshmaterializedview.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 9. Migration Strategy: Definition, Index, Refresh

Jangan campur semuanya sembarangan.

### Recommended Breakdown

```text id="nsszki"
1. Create materialized view definition WITH NO DATA
2. Create required indexes
3. Grant access
4. Initial refresh
5. Enable consumer
6. Schedule recurring refresh
```

### Why Separate?

| Step | Reason |
|---|---|
| Definition | DDL object lifecycle. |
| Index | Needed for read performance and concurrent refresh. |
| Grant | Runtime access. |
| Initial refresh | Operational data load. |
| Consumer enablement | Avoid reading empty/unscannable view. |
| Recurring refresh | Freshness guarantee. |

---

## 10. Flyway Structure

```text id="sin0y7"
src/main/resources/db/migration/
├─ versioned/
│  ├─ V001100__case_create_mv_case_status_metrics.sql
│  ├─ V001101__case_create_mv_case_status_metrics_indexes.sql
│  ├─ V001102__case_grant_mv_case_status_metrics.sql
│  └─ V001103__case_refresh_mv_case_status_metrics_initial.sql
│
└─ repeatable/
   └─ R__040_case_mv_case_status_metrics.sql
```

Ada dua pendekatan:

### Approach A - Versioned Definition

```text id="vw81re"
V001100__create_mv_case_status_metrics.sql
```

Cocok jika materialized view jarang berubah dan contract-nya sensitif.

### Approach B - Repeatable Definition

```text id="ua8q2o"
R__040_case_mv_case_status_metrics.sql
```

Cocok jika definisi diperlakukan sebagai source-of-truth file dan perubahan selalu direview ketat.

Untuk materialized view yang dikonsumsi API/reporting, saya cenderung memilih:

```text id="02mo6s"
Breaking change:
  create mv_v2

Small compatible internal definition change:
  repeatable definition can be okay
```

---

## 11. Flyway Example

### Definition

```sql id="gy40dd"
-- R__040_case_mv_case_status_metrics.sql

CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW IF NOT EXISTS case_app.mv_case_status_metrics AS
SELECT
    c.status,
    COUNT(*) AS case_count,
    max(c.updated_at) AS latest_update_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
GROUP BY c.status
WITH NO DATA;
```

### Index

```sql id="5gshmm"
-- V001101__case_mv_case_status_metrics_indexes.sql

CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_mv_case_status_metrics_status
ON case_app.mv_case_status_metrics (status);

CREATE INDEX idx_mv_case_status_metrics_case_count
ON case_app.mv_case_status_metrics (case_count DESC);
```

### Initial Refresh

```sql id="x9vc0k"
-- V001103__case_refresh_mv_case_status_metrics_initial.sql

REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;
```

### Grant

```sql id="1r924o"
-- V001102__case_grant_mv_case_status_metrics.sql

GRANT SELECT ON case_app.mv_case_status_metrics TO case_app_runtime;
```

---

## 12. Liquibase Structure

```text id="4b40m0"
src/main/resources/db/changelog/600-materialized-views/
├─ 601-mv-case-status-metrics.yaml
├─ 602-mv-case-status-metrics-indexes.yaml
├─ 603-mv-case-status-metrics-grants.yaml
├─ 604-mv-case-status-metrics-initial-refresh.yaml
└─ sql/
   └─ mv_case_status_metrics.sql
```

### Definition Wrapper

```yaml id="l5y12k"
databaseChangeLog:
  - changeSet:
      id: 601-case-mv-status-metrics-definition
      author: case-service-team
      labels: materialized-view,case-reporting
      changes:
        - sqlFile:
            path: src/main/resources/db/changelog/600-materialized-views/sql/mv_case_status_metrics.sql
            splitStatements: false
            stripComments: false
      rollback:
        - sql:
            sql: |
              DROP MATERIALIZED VIEW IF EXISTS case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;
```

### SQL File

```sql id="6f62ju"
CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics AS
SELECT
    c.status,
    COUNT(*) AS case_count,
    max(c.updated_at) AS latest_update_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
GROUP BY c.status
WITH NO DATA;
```

### Index Changeset

```yaml id="p2rgpb"
databaseChangeLog:
  - changeSet:
      id: 602-case-mv-status-metrics-indexes
      author: case-service-team
      labels: materialized-view,index
      changes:
        - sql:
            sql: |
              CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_mv_case_status_metrics_status
              ON case_app.mv_case_status_metrics (status);

              CREATE INDEX idx_mv_case_status_metrics_case_count
              ON case_app.mv_case_status_metrics (case_count DESC);
      rollback:
        - sql:
            sql: |
              DROP INDEX IF EXISTS case_app.idx_mv_case_status_metrics_case_count;
              DROP INDEX IF EXISTS case_app.uq_mv_case_status_metrics_status;
```

---

## 13. Initial Refresh as Migration: Pros and Cons

### Option A - Refresh inside migration

```sql id="ft01vq"
REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;
```

| Pros | Cons |
|---|---|
| Data ready after migration. | Migration duration can be long. |
| Simple lifecycle. | Heavy query can block/timeout deploy. |
| Good for small data. | Less observable than dedicated job. |

### Option B - Refresh via operational job

```text id="r8xzwd"
Migration:
  create matview WITH NO DATA
  create indexes
  grant select

Job:
  refresh matview
  validate row count
  mark ready
```

| Pros | Cons |
|---|---|
| Better observability. | More operational moving parts. |
| Can retry separately. | Consumer enablement must wait. |
| Safer for large data. | Needs runbook/scheduler. |

### Recommendation

```text id="gkktnv"
Small materialized view:
  refresh in migration can be acceptable.

Large / API-critical materialized view:
  refresh in dedicated controlled job.
```

---

## 14. Readiness Marker Pattern

Jika consumer tidak boleh membaca materialized view sebelum refresh pertama selesai, tambahkan readiness metadata.

```sql id="zlx60z"
CREATE TABLE case_app.read_model_refresh_state (
    read_model_name      TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    last_refresh_started_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    last_refresh_completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    last_refresh_status  TEXT NOT NULL,
    row_count            BIGINT,
    error_message        TEXT,
    updated_at           TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

After refresh:

```sql id="hzfku3"
INSERT INTO case_app.read_model_refresh_state (
    read_model_name,
    last_refresh_completed_at,
    last_refresh_status,
    row_count
)
SELECT
    'mv_case_status_metrics',
    now(),
    'SUCCESS',
    COUNT(*)
FROM case_app.mv_case_status_metrics
ON CONFLICT (read_model_name) DO UPDATE
SET
    last_refresh_completed_at = EXCLUDED.last_refresh_completed_at,
    last_refresh_status = EXCLUDED.last_refresh_status,
    row_count = EXCLUDED.row_count,
    updated_at = now();
```

### API Guard

Jersey service can check:

```text id="8558e5"
If read_model_refresh_state.last_refresh_status != SUCCESS:
    return 503 / fallback / use live query
```

---

## 15. Stale Data Contract

Materialized view harus punya freshness SLA.

| Freshness Type | Meaning | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Real-time | Not appropriate for matview. | Use base table/query. |
| Near-real-time | Refresh every 1-5 min. | Operational dashboard. |
| Periodic | Refresh every 15-60 min. | Reporting summary. |
| Daily snapshot | Refresh daily. | Management report. |
| Manual refresh | Refresh on demand. | Admin report. |

### API Response Should Be Honest

```json id="26qz17"
{
  "status": "OPEN",
  "caseCount": 1234,
  "lastRefreshedAt": "2026-07-13T02:00:00Z",
  "freshness": "REFRESHED_EVERY_15_MINUTES"
}
```

If the API hides staleness, consumers may make incorrect decisions.

---

## 16. Scheduling Refresh

Options:

| Refresh Mechanism | Fit |
|---|---|
| Kubernetes CronJob | Good for periodic refresh. |
| Application admin endpoint | Good for controlled manual refresh, but secure it. |
| Batch worker | Good if refresh has orchestration/metrics. |
| Database scheduler extension | Possible if platform supports it. |
| CI/CD job | Good for initial release refresh only. |
| Camunda job | Possible if refresh is business workflow-driven. |

### Kubernetes CronJob Shape

```yaml id="pbyxlk"
apiVersion: batch/v1
kind: CronJob
metadata:
  name: case-status-metrics-refresh
spec:
  schedule: "*/15 * * * *"
  jobTemplate:
    spec:
      template:
        spec:
          restartPolicy: OnFailure
          containers:
            - name: refresh
              image: case-service-migration:1.0.0
              envFrom:
                - secretRef:
                    name: case-service-db
              command:
                - "psql"
              args:
                - "$(JDBC_COMPATIBLE_PSQL_ARGS)"
                - "-c"
                - "REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;"
```

In real setup, avoid vague env like `JDBC_COMPATIBLE_PSQL_ARGS`; use explicit `PGHOST`, `PGPORT`, `PGDATABASE`, `PGUSER`, `PGPASSWORD` or a secure wrapper.

---

## 17. Refresh Function / Procedure

Daripada CronJob menjalankan SQL langsung, buat procedure yang mengelola audit state.

```sql id="d23fa2"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_refresh_case_status_metrics()
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    INSERT INTO case_app.read_model_refresh_state (
        read_model_name,
        last_refresh_started_at,
        last_refresh_status,
        updated_at
    )
    VALUES (
        'mv_case_status_metrics',
        now(),
        'RUNNING',
        now()
    )
    ON CONFLICT (read_model_name) DO UPDATE
    SET
        last_refresh_started_at = EXCLUDED.last_refresh_started_at,
        last_refresh_status = 'RUNNING',
        error_message = NULL,
        updated_at = now();

    REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;

    INSERT INTO case_app.read_model_refresh_state (
        read_model_name,
        last_refresh_completed_at,
        last_refresh_status,
        row_count,
        updated_at
    )
    SELECT
        'mv_case_status_metrics',
        now(),
        'SUCCESS',
        COUNT(*),
        now()
    FROM case_app.mv_case_status_metrics
    ON CONFLICT (read_model_name) DO UPDATE
    SET
        last_refresh_completed_at = EXCLUDED.last_refresh_completed_at,
        last_refresh_status = 'SUCCESS',
        row_count = EXCLUDED.row_count,
        updated_at = now();

EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS THEN
    INSERT INTO case_app.read_model_refresh_state (
        read_model_name,
        last_refresh_status,
        error_message,
        updated_at
    )
    VALUES (
        'mv_case_status_metrics',
        'FAILED',
        SQLERRM,
        now()
    )
    ON CONFLICT (read_model_name) DO UPDATE
    SET
        last_refresh_status = 'FAILED',
        error_message = EXCLUDED.error_message,
        updated_at = now();

    RAISE;
END;
$$;
```

Call:

```sql id="sfyfi5"
CALL case_app.proc_refresh_case_status_metrics();
```

---

## 18. Privilege Model

PostgreSQL 18 docs mention that the `MAINTAIN` privilege allows operations including `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW`, among other maintenance operations. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/ddl-priv.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Practical role split:

```text id="kuo54s"
case_app_runtime:
  SELECT on materialized view

case_app_refresh:
  MAINTAIN / required refresh privilege
  EXECUTE on refresh procedure

case_app_migration:
  CREATE / ALTER / DROP materialized view and indexes
```

### Example

```sql id="8lkgv4"
GRANT SELECT ON case_app.mv_case_status_metrics TO case_app_runtime;

GRANT EXECUTE ON PROCEDURE case_app.proc_refresh_case_status_metrics()
TO case_app_refresh;
```

Depending on PostgreSQL version and privilege model in your environment, validate whether refresh is granted via ownership, `MAINTAIN`, or controlled procedure ownership.

---

## 19. Materialized View and MyBatis

Mapper:

```xml id="ij1cxk"
<select id="findCaseStatusMetrics" resultType="CaseStatusMetricDto">
    SELECT
        status,
        case_count,
        latest_update_at
    FROM case_app.mv_case_status_metrics
    ORDER BY case_count DESC
</select>
```

### Contract Columns

```text id="6t536y"
status
case_count
latest_update_at
```

If you change `case_count` to `total_cases`, mapper breaks.

### Safer Evolution

Add new column first:

```sql id="7gudor"
CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics_v2 AS
SELECT
    status,
    COUNT(*) AS case_count,
    COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE priority = 'HIGH') AS high_priority_case_count,
    max(updated_at) AS latest_update_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
GROUP BY status
WITH NO DATA;
```

Switch mapper to v2 later.

---

## 20. Materialized View and Jersey API

If API reads from materialized view, expose freshness.

Bad API:

```json id="8rwja0"
{
  "openCases": 120
}
```

Better API:

```json id="3wdfqq"
{
  "openCases": 120,
  "source": "MATERIALIZED_VIEW",
  "lastRefreshedAt": "2026-07-13T02:00:00Z"
}
```

### Service Behavior

| Refresh State | API Behavior |
|---|---|
| Success and fresh | Return cached/materialized result. |
| Success but stale | Return with stale warning or fallback. |
| Running | Return previous result or fallback. |
| Failed | Return fallback or 503 depending criticality. |
| Never refreshed | Do not return misleading empty result. |

---

## 21. Materialized View and Camunda

Materialized view can be dangerous if used for workflow decisions.

Example risky use:

```text id="18u97k"
Escalate case if metric from materialized view says overdue_count > 0.
```

If materialized view is stale, escalation can be late or wrong.

### Safer Use

| Use Case | Matview Acceptable? |
|---|---:|
| Dashboard counts | Yes |
| Non-critical reporting | Yes |
| Batch planning | Maybe |
| Escalation trigger | Usually use live query or clearly stale-aware query |
| Authorization | No |
| Financial/regulatory deadline calculation | Usually no unless freshness SLA is strict and monitored |

---

## 22. Dependency Ordering

Recommended order:

```text id="r2lyx3"
1. Base tables
2. Reference data
3. Functions used by materialized view query
4. Normal views used by materialized view
5. Materialized view definition WITH NO DATA
6. Unique index for concurrent refresh
7. Other read indexes
8. Grants
9. Initial refresh
10. Readiness marker
11. Consumer app deployment
12. Refresh schedule
```

### Example File Order

```text id="to5dkk"
V001001__case_tables.sql
V001020__case_reference_seed.sql
R__010_case_fn_bucket_priority.sql
R__020_case_view_case_summary.sql
V001100__case_mv_case_status_metrics_create.sql
V001101__case_mv_case_status_metrics_unique_index.sql
V001102__case_mv_case_status_metrics_grants.sql
V001103__case_mv_case_status_metrics_initial_refresh.sql
```

---

## 23. Avoid Drop/Recreate In-Place for Critical Matview

Bad:

```sql id="io5g89"
DROP MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;

CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics AS
SELECT ...
WITH DATA;
```

Problems:

- readers can fail during drop/recreate;
- grants may be lost;
- indexes are lost;
- refresh state resets;
- API may see empty/missing object;
- dependent objects may break.

### Safer: Create v2

```sql id="vea1xa"
CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics_v2 AS
SELECT
    status,
    priority,
    COUNT(*) AS case_count,
    max(updated_at) AS latest_update_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
GROUP BY status, priority
WITH NO DATA;
```

Create indexes:

```sql id="1qe63b"
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_mv_case_status_metrics_v2
ON case_app.mv_case_status_metrics_v2 (status, priority);
```

Refresh:

```sql id="7ck5eb"
REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics_v2;
```

Switch consumer:

```text id="n8tt77"
old mapper → mv_case_status_metrics
new mapper → mv_case_status_metrics_v2
```

Drop old later.

---

## 24. Changing Definition Safely

### Compatible Additive Change

Add a new derived metric while preserving existing columns:

```sql id="81va9f"
CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics_v2 AS
SELECT
    status,
    COUNT(*) AS case_count,
    max(updated_at) AS latest_update_at,
    COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE priority = 'HIGH') AS high_priority_case_count
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
GROUP BY status
WITH NO DATA;
```

Even if additive, I still prefer v2 for materialized view if:

- API/reporting heavily depends on it;
- refresh cost is high;
- index strategy changes;
- freshness contract changes.

### Breaking Change

Examples:

- remove column;
- rename column;
- change granularity from `status` to `status + priority`;
- change metric definition;
- change timestamp basis;
- change filter semantics.

Use v2.

---

## 25. Materialized View Naming Convention

| Pattern | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `mv_case_status_metrics` | Current stable materialized view. |
| `mv_case_status_metrics_v2` | New contract during migration. |
| `mv_case_status_metrics_legacy` | Temporary old contract, if renamed. |
| `mv_case_status_metrics_daily` | Freshness/grain encoded. |
| `mv_case_assignment_workload` | Domain-oriented read model. |

### Rule

```text id="wf45jd"
Name should communicate:
  domain + metric/read model + grain/freshness if important.
```

---

## 26. Index Strategy

Materialized view needs indexes just like table.

### Query

```sql id="9ogpsk"
SELECT
    status,
    case_count
FROM case_app.mv_case_status_metrics
ORDER BY case_count DESC;
```

Index:

```sql id="z06p7h"
CREATE INDEX idx_mv_case_status_metrics_case_count
ON case_app.mv_case_status_metrics (case_count DESC);
```

### Concurrent Refresh Requirement

If using:

```sql id="37muf6"
REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;
```

Need unique index:

```sql id="8ggy3l"
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_mv_case_status_metrics_status
ON case_app.mv_case_status_metrics (status);
```

### Index Review Checklist

| Question | Why |
|---|---|
| What query consumes the matview? | Determines read index. |
| Is concurrent refresh required? | Determines unique index requirement. |
| Does unique index represent actual row identity? | Avoid refresh conflicts. |
| Are columns nullable? | Null uniqueness semantics matter. |
| Is index partial/expression? | Not valid for concurrent refresh requirement. |
| Does refresh maintain large indexes? | Refresh cost increases. |

---

## 27. Refresh Performance

Refresh completely replaces materialized view contents. This is not incremental refresh by default.

### Performance Risks

| Risk | Explanation |
|---|---|
| Heavy base query | High CPU/IO. |
| Large matview result | Large storage and index maintenance. |
| Frequent refresh | Repeated cost. |
| Concurrent refresh | Less blocking for readers but still expensive. |
| Multiple matviews refreshing | Load spike. |
| Replication lag | Refresh writes can generate WAL. |

### Mitigation

```text id="djpu5r"
- schedule off-peak if possible
- stagger refresh jobs
- use appropriate indexes on base tables
- reduce result grain
- consider partitioned summary tables if needed
- monitor duration and row count
```

---

## 28. Materialized View vs Summary Table

| Option | Pros | Cons |
|---|---|---|
| Materialized view | Simple definition, managed by PostgreSQL. | Full refresh model, limited incremental control. |
| Summary table | Full control over incremental updates. | More code and data correctness burden. |
| View | Always fresh. | Query may be expensive. |
| Cache/Redis | Fast, flexible. | Consistency and invalidation complexity. |

### Choose Materialized View When

```text id="zrx5js"
- query is expensive but refresh can be periodic;
- result size is manageable;
- staleness is acceptable;
- full refresh cost is acceptable;
- definition should remain SQL-centric.
```

### Choose Summary Table When

```text id="5801wz"
- incremental update is required;
- refresh is too expensive;
- strict freshness needed;
- complex workflow controls data updates.
```

---

## 29. Refresh Locking and Concurrency

Practical interpretation:

| Refresh Type | Reader Impact | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW` | Can block readers / replace contents directly. | No unique index required. |
| `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY` | Allows reads during refresh. | Populated matview + qualifying unique index. |

PostgreSQL docs state `CONCURRENTLY` cannot be used together with `WITH NO DATA`, can only be used when the materialized view is already populated, and only one refresh at a time may run against a materialized view. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-refreshmaterializedview.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 30. Initial Population Pattern

Because concurrent refresh requires the matview already populated, the initial flow is:

```text id="kbe4sd"
1. CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW ... WITH NO DATA
2. CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ...
3. REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW ...
4. Subsequent refresh:
     REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY ...
```

Initial refresh cannot be concurrent if materialized view is not populated.

---

## 31. Deployment Pattern for New Materialized View

```text id="rif0s5"
Release 1:
  Create matview WITH NO DATA
  Create indexes
  Grant select
  Initial refresh
  Mark ready

Release 2:
  Deploy app that reads matview
  Include fallback/readiness check

Release 3:
  Schedule recurring refresh
  Monitor freshness

Release 4:
  If replacing old matview, migrate consumers

Release 5:
  Drop old matview after no consumers
```

For critical API, do not deploy app reading the matview before initial refresh/readiness is done.

---

## 32. Fallback Strategy

If matview stale or refresh failed:

### Option A - Return Stale with Metadata

```json id="fu3t28"
{
  "data": [
    {
      "status": "OPEN",
      "caseCount": 120
    }
  ],
  "freshness": {
    "source": "MATERIALIZED_VIEW",
    "lastRefreshedAt": "2026-07-13T02:00:00Z",
    "stale": true
  }
}
```

### Option B - Fallback to Live Query

```text id="8rwrpq"
if matview freshness > threshold:
    run live query
else:
    use matview
```

### Option C - Fail Closed

```text id="e81al2"
if matview is stale:
    return 503
```

Use for critical data where stale answer is worse than no answer.

---

## 33. MyBatis Mapper with Freshness Metadata

```xml id="9nb562"
<select id="findCaseStatusMetrics" resultMap="CaseStatusMetricResultMap">
    SELECT
        m.status,
        m.case_count,
        m.latest_update_at,
        s.last_refresh_completed_at
    FROM case_app.mv_case_status_metrics m
    CROSS JOIN case_app.read_model_refresh_state s
    WHERE s.read_model_name = 'mv_case_status_metrics'
      AND s.last_refresh_status = 'SUCCESS'
    ORDER BY m.case_count DESC
</select>
```

This lets service layer evaluate freshness.

---

## 34. Refresh State Query

```sql id="2hukj5"
SELECT
    read_model_name,
    last_refresh_started_at,
    last_refresh_completed_at,
    now() - last_refresh_completed_at AS age,
    last_refresh_status,
    row_count,
    error_message
FROM case_app.read_model_refresh_state
WHERE read_model_name = 'mv_case_status_metrics';
```

Useful for:

- health endpoint;
- dashboard;
- deployment verification;
- incident triage.

---

## 35. Health Check Design

Do not make Kubernetes readiness depend on every reporting materialized view unless the service cannot function without it.

| Health Type | Use |
|---|---|
| Liveness | App process alive; should not depend on matview freshness. |
| Readiness | Core dependency ready; only include matview if critical. |
| Business health | Expose matview freshness separately. |
| Alerting | Alert if refresh failure/freshness SLA breached. |

Example health payload:

```json id="9nvtwh"
{
  "database": "UP",
  "materializedViews": {
    "mv_case_status_metrics": {
      "status": "STALE",
      "lastRefreshedAt": "2026-07-13T02:00:00Z",
      "ageMinutes": 32,
      "slaMinutes": 15
    }
  }
}
```

---

## 36. Grants and Recreate Pitfall

If you drop and recreate materialized view:

```sql id="equc6l"
DROP MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;
CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics AS ...
```

You may need to recreate:

- indexes;
- grants;
- comments;
- ownership;
- dependency links;
- refresh state;
- monitoring configuration.

Use v2 migration when possible.

---

## 37. Comments for Documentation

```sql id="h8j7sh"
COMMENT ON MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics IS
'Aggregated case count by status. Refreshed every 15 minutes. Source for case dashboard.';

COMMENT ON COLUMN case_app.mv_case_status_metrics.case_count IS
'Number of cases in the status as of last materialized view refresh.';
```

This helps DB introspection and onboarding.

---

## 38. Ownership Documentation

```md id="nhmu6b"
# Materialized View: case_app.mv_case_status_metrics

## Purpose
Dashboard read model for case count by status.

## Owner
case-service-team

## Consumers
- CaseMetricsMapper.findStatusMetrics
- GET /case-metrics/status
- Operations dashboard

## Base Objects
- case_app.enforcement_case
- case_app.case_status_reference

## Refresh
- Type: periodic
- Schedule: every 15 minutes
- Method: REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY
- Required unique index: uq_mv_case_status_metrics_status

## Freshness Contract
Data may be stale up to 15 minutes.
API exposes lastRefreshedAt.

## Failure Behavior
If stale > 30 minutes, API returns stale warning.
If stale > 2 hours, alert operations.

## Change Policy
Breaking shape changes require v2 materialized view.
```

---

## 39. Testing Strategy

### Test 1 - Definition Exists

```sql id="uuyevy"
SELECT matviewname
FROM pg_matviews
WHERE schemaname = 'case_app'
  AND matviewname = 'mv_case_status_metrics';
```

### Test 2 - Column Contract

```sql id="yml3wx"
SELECT
    column_name,
    data_type,
    ordinal_position
FROM information_schema.columns
WHERE table_schema = 'case_app'
  AND table_name = 'mv_case_status_metrics'
ORDER BY ordinal_position;
```

### Test 3 - Initial Refresh Works

```sql id="5lwr4y"
REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;
```

### Test 4 - Concurrent Refresh Works

```sql id="v50gis"
REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;
```

This should be tested after initial population and unique index creation.

### Test 5 - Mapper Query Works

Run MyBatis integration test against real migrated PostgreSQL.

### Test 6 - Freshness State Works

```sql id="fzf65p"
SELECT *
FROM case_app.read_model_refresh_state
WHERE read_model_name = 'mv_case_status_metrics';
```

---

## 40. CI/CD Gates

```text id="ln0ehp"
Pull Request
  ├─ Detect materialized view change
  ├─ Run migration on fresh PostgreSQL
  ├─ Validate matview exists
  ├─ Validate unique index if concurrent refresh is used
  ├─ Run initial refresh
  ├─ Run concurrent refresh if configured
  ├─ Run MyBatis tests
  ├─ Validate freshness metadata
  ├─ Run EXPLAIN for consumer queries
  └─ Require owner approval for breaking matview shape change
```

### Dangerous Pattern Detector

```bash id="bua0fp"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

ROOT="${1:-src/main/resources/db}"

if grep -RniE "DROP MATERIALIZED VIEW .*CASCADE" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "DROP MATERIALIZED VIEW CASCADE detected. Requires explicit dependency review."
  exit 1
fi

if grep -RniE "REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY" "$ROOT" \
   && ! grep -RniE "CREATE UNIQUE INDEX .*ON .*mv_" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "Concurrent materialized view refresh detected without obvious unique index."
  exit 1
fi
```

This is crude and should be complemented with real SQL inspection.

---

## 41. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Why Dangerous | Better |
|---|---|---|
| Treat matview like normal view | Ignores freshness and refresh cost. | Define freshness contract. |
| Create `WITH DATA` on huge query in migration | Deploy timeout/load spike. | Use `WITH NO DATA` + controlled refresh. |
| API reads matview before initial refresh | Empty/unavailable misleading data. | Readiness marker. |
| Use concurrent refresh without qualifying unique index | Refresh fails. | Add proper unique index. |
| Drop/recreate critical matview in place | Reader failure, grants/index loss. | Create v2, refresh, switch consumers. |
| No stale-data metadata | Consumers assume real-time. | Expose `lastRefreshedAt`. |
| Use matview for authorization | Stale access decisions. | Use live source of truth. |
| Refresh too frequently | DB load and WAL pressure. | Tune schedule and monitor duration. |
| No refresh monitoring | Silent stale data. | Refresh state + alerting. |
| No mapper test | Runtime query failure. | Integration test against PostgreSQL. |

---

## 42. Practical Example: Case Dashboard Metrics

### Requirement

Dashboard needs:

```text id="xiak4x"
- count by status
- count by status + priority
- latest update timestamp
- refreshed every 15 minutes
```

### Materialized View v1

```sql id="4yb2gg"
CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_dashboard_metrics AS
SELECT
    c.status,
    c.priority,
    COUNT(*) AS case_count,
    max(c.updated_at) AS latest_update_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
GROUP BY c.status, c.priority
WITH NO DATA;
```

### Unique Index for Concurrent Refresh

```sql id="9vxwhn"
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_mv_case_dashboard_metrics
ON case_app.mv_case_dashboard_metrics (status, priority);
```

### Read Index

```sql id="3n5p7d"
CREATE INDEX idx_mv_case_dashboard_metrics_case_count
ON case_app.mv_case_dashboard_metrics (case_count DESC);
```

### Initial Refresh

```sql id="u2meb2"
REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_dashboard_metrics;
```

### Subsequent Refresh

```sql id="t0raio"
REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY case_app.mv_case_dashboard_metrics;
```

### Mapper

```xml id="48s30k"
<select id="findDashboardMetrics" resultType="CaseDashboardMetricDto">
    SELECT
        status,
        priority,
        case_count,
        latest_update_at
    FROM case_app.mv_case_dashboard_metrics
    ORDER BY status, priority
</select>
```

---

## 43. Decision Tree

```text id="34e2jd"
Do you need fresh real-time data?
  ├─ Yes → do not use materialized view
  └─ No
      │
      ├─ Is query expensive and read frequently?
      │   ├─ Yes → materialized view candidate
      │   └─ No → use normal view/query/index
      │
      ├─ Can full refresh finish within SLA?
      │   ├─ Yes → materialized view okay
      │   └─ No → consider summary table/incremental pipeline
      │
      ├─ Can readers tolerate stale data?
      │   ├─ Yes → expose freshness metadata
      │   └─ No → fallback/live query required
      │
      └─ Is contract likely to change?
          ├─ Yes → use v2 migration pattern
          └─ No → stable matview definition
```

---

## 44. Production Review Checklist

| Question | Required Answer |
|---|---|
| Why materialized view instead of normal view/index? | Performance justification. |
| What is freshness SLA? | Minutes/hours/daily/manual. |
| Who consumes it? | Mapper/API/reporting list. |
| Is stale data acceptable? | Explicit yes/no. |
| Is initial refresh done before consumer deploy? | Must be planned. |
| Is concurrent refresh required? | If yes, unique index exists. |
| Is unique index valid for concurrent refresh? | Column-only, full-row coverage. |
| What is refresh schedule? | Cron/job/procedure. |
| What happens on refresh failure? | Fallback/alert/503/stale warning. |
| Are grants correct? | Runtime and refresh roles. |
| Are indexes recreated if replaced? | Must be explicit. |
| Is breaking change handled with v2? | Required for contract change. |
| Are MyBatis tests updated? | Required. |
| Is API freshness exposed? | Recommended. |

---

## 45. Ringkasan Seri 10

| Area | Kesimpulan |
|---|---|
| Materialized view | View-like query result stored physically. |
| Main difference from view | Needs explicit refresh. |
| `WITH DATA` | Populates during creation; risky for large production query. |
| `WITH NO DATA` | Safer definition-first pattern. |
| Refresh | Replaces materialized view contents. |
| Concurrent refresh | Requires populated matview and qualifying unique index. |
| Unique index | Must be column-only and cover all rows for concurrent refresh. |
| API usage | Must expose or honor freshness contract. |
| MyBatis | Matview columns are mapper contract. |
| Camunda | Avoid stale matview for critical workflow decisions. |
| Migration strategy | Separate definition, index, grant, refresh, consumer enablement. |
| Breaking change | Prefer v2 materialized view. |
| Monitoring | Track last refresh, status, row count, error. |
| Production safety | Test initial refresh, concurrent refresh, mapper query, freshness behavior. |
