# Seri 11 - PL/pgSQL Function Migration

## 1. Tujuan Seri Ini

Seri ini membahas bagaimana mengelola **PostgreSQL function / PL/pgSQL function** dalam database migration untuk microservice berbasis:

```text id="k12nhw"
Jersey / JAX-RS
MyBatis
PostgreSQL
Flyway / Liquibase
Camunda
CI/CD
Kubernetes
```

Function di PostgreSQL bisa menjadi bagian penting dari sistem:

- dipanggil oleh MyBatis query;
- dipakai oleh view/materialized view;
- dipakai trigger function;
- dipakai procedure;
- dipakai untuk validation/normalization;
- dipakai untuk read projection;
- dipakai untuk auditing;
- dipakai untuk permission/security logic.

Karena itu function migration harus diperlakukan sebagai **database code lifecycle**, bukan sekadar SQL helper.

PostgreSQL `CREATE FUNCTION` mendukung banyak opsi seperti bahasa function, return type, volatility (`VOLATILE`, `STABLE`, `IMMUTABLE`), `SECURITY INVOKER`/`SECURITY DEFINER`, `LEAKPROOF`, parallel safety, cost, rows, support function, dan `SET` configuration saat function dipanggil. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createfunction.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 2. Mental Model

```text id="db5y01"
PL/pgSQL Function Migration Mental Model
────────────────────────────────────────────

Function Definition
  │
  ├─ name
  ├─ schema
  ├─ input argument types
  ├─ return type
  ├─ language
  ├─ volatility
  ├─ security mode
  ├─ privileges / grants
  └─ body
       │
       ▼
Dependencies
  ├─ MyBatis mapper
  ├─ View
  ├─ Materialized view
  ├─ Trigger function / trigger
  ├─ Procedure
  ├─ Constraint / index expression
  ├─ API service logic
  └─ Camunda worker / batch job
```

Core rule:

```text id="c3p7fq"
Function signature is a contract.
Function body is implementation.
Function volatility/security are runtime behavior.
```

---

## 3. Basic PL/pgSQL Function

```sql id="8sm9ci"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days(
    p_created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
)
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
STABLE
AS $$
BEGIN
    IF p_created_at IS NULL THEN
        RETURN NULL;
    END IF;

    RETURN FLOOR(EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now() - p_created_at)) / 86400)::INTEGER;
END;
$$;
```

### Important Parts

| Part | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days` | Schema-qualified function name. |
| `p_created_at TIMESTAMPTZ` | Input argument and type. |
| `RETURNS INTEGER` | Return contract. |
| `LANGUAGE plpgsql` | Function language. |
| `STABLE` | Planner/runtime volatility hint. |
| Function body | Implementation. |

PostgreSQL function volatility has three categories: `VOLATILE`, `STABLE`, and `IMMUTABLE`; `VOLATILE` is the default if unspecified. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/xfunc-volatility.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 4. Function Signature Is the Real Identity

PostgreSQL functions are resolved by name and input argument types.

These are different overloads:

```sql id="jq38qc"
case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla(p_case_id UUID)

case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla(p_case_id UUID, p_timezone TEXT)
```

### Signature Components

```text id="8gxph6"
schema
function name
input argument types
```

Return type is part of the contract for callers, but function lookup is primarily driven by name and argument types.

### Practical Rule

```text id="pfeyiv"
Changing function body with same signature:
  usually repeatable migration

Changing function argument types / return type:
  breaking change, use versioning or explicit migration plan
```

---

## 5. `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION`: What It Can and Cannot Do

`CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` is useful for updating the body or attributes of an existing function, but it is not a magic compatibility layer.

### Usually Okay

```sql id="7qy9lu"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days(
    p_created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
)
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
STABLE
AS $$
BEGIN
    RETURN GREATEST(
        FLOOR(EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now() - p_created_at)) / 86400)::INTEGER,
        0
    );
END;
$$;
```

Same signature, same return type.

### Risky / Breaking

```sql id="kijfeh"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days(
    p_created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
)
RETURNS NUMERIC
LANGUAGE plpgsql
STABLE
AS $$
BEGIN
    RETURN EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now() - p_created_at)) / 86400;
END;
$$;
```

Changing return type can break callers and is not safe to treat as ordinary body replacement. PostgreSQL does not allow all aspects of an existing function to be changed in place; for many identity-level changes, the safer pattern is to create a new function or drop/recreate with dependency review. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createfunction.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 6. Versioned vs Repeatable Function Migration

## 6.1 Repeatable Function Migration

Best for:

```text id="9f01n6"
Same name
Same arguments
Same return type
Body changes
Internal logic changes
Volatility/security unchanged or consciously reviewed
```

Flyway:

```text id="e7s7fl"
R__010_case_fn_calculate_case_age_days.sql
```

Liquibase:

```yaml id="epqkhr"
databaseChangeLog:
  - changeSet:
      id: 701-case-fn-calculate-case-age-days
      author: case-service-team
      runOnChange: true
      labels: function,plpgsql
      changes:
        - sqlFile:
            path: src/main/resources/db/changelog/700-functions/sql/fn_calculate_case_age_days.sql
            splitStatements: false
            stripComments: false
```

---

## 6.2 Versioned Function Migration

Best for:

```text id="84o9dk"
New function
Function signature change
Return type change
Privilege/security change
Dependency-breaking semantic change
Dropping old function
```

Example:

```text id="nww3a6"
V001200__case_create_fn_calculate_case_sla_v2.sql
V001230__case_drop_fn_calculate_case_sla_v1.sql
```

### Practical Rule

```text id="e7sz8g"
Repeatable for implementation changes.
Versioned for contract changes.
```

---

## 7. Function Versioning Pattern

### Existing Function

```sql id="9q8gee"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla(
    p_case_id UUID
)
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
STABLE
AS $$
BEGIN
    RETURN 0;
END;
$$;
```

### New Requirement

Need timezone-aware calculation:

```sql id="p3m576"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla_v2(
    p_case_id UUID,
    p_timezone TEXT
)
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
STABLE
AS $$
BEGIN
    RETURN 0;
END;
$$;
```

### Migration Plan

```text id="yhnb86"
Release 1:
  Create fn_calculate_case_sla_v2

Release 2:
  Update view / MyBatis / service logic to use v2

Release 3:
  Verify no callers use v1

Release 4:
  Drop v1 after dependency search
```

### Why Not Replace In Place?

Because existing callers may still call:

```sql id="k0ck53"
case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla(case_id)
```

During rolling deployment, both old and new app versions may exist.

---

## 8. Volatility: `VOLATILE`, `STABLE`, `IMMUTABLE`

Function volatility is not decoration. It tells PostgreSQL what assumptions the planner can make.

| Volatility | Meaning | Example Fit |
|---|---|---|
| `VOLATILE` | Result can change even within a single table scan; functions with side effects must be volatile. | `random()`, sequence updates, writes, external effects. |
| `STABLE` | Result does not change within a single statement, but can change between statements. | Lookup based on DB state, `now()`-based computation in same statement. |
| `IMMUTABLE` | Same inputs always produce same output forever. | Pure deterministic math/string normalization independent of DB/session/time. |

PostgreSQL documentation states every function has a volatility classification, and `VOLATILE` is the default when `CREATE FUNCTION` does not specify one. It also states that side-effecting functions must be `VOLATILE` so calls are not optimized away. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/xfunc-volatility.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

### Example: Good `IMMUTABLE`

```sql id="eoeqt8"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_normalize_code(
    p_code TEXT
)
RETURNS TEXT
LANGUAGE sql
IMMUTABLE
AS $$
    SELECT upper(trim(p_code));
$$;
```

### Example: Not `IMMUTABLE`

```sql id="43jhsm"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_today_age_days(
    p_created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
)
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
STABLE
AS $$
BEGIN
    RETURN FLOOR(EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now() - p_created_at)) / 86400)::INTEGER;
END;
$$;
```

Uses current time, so not immutable.

---

## 9. Volatility Mistakes

| Mistake | Why Dangerous |
|---|---|
| Marking DB lookup function as `IMMUTABLE` | Planner may cache/optimize based on false assumption. |
| Marking time-dependent function as `IMMUTABLE` | Results can become wrong. |
| Leaving pure function as `VOLATILE` | Prevents useful optimization. |
| Side-effect function marked `STABLE` | Planner may optimize in unsafe ways. |
| Changing volatility silently | Query plans and index eligibility may change. |

### Rule

```text id="xceqek"
Volatility is part of function behavior contract.
Review it like code, not syntax.
```

---

## 10. Functions in Indexes and Constraints

If a function is used in an expression index:

```sql id="m0phvd"
CREATE INDEX idx_case_normalized_number
ON case_app.enforcement_case (
    case_app.fn_normalize_code(case_number)
);
```

The function must be safe for index semantics. In practice, expression index functions should be deterministic; `IMMUTABLE` functions are generally expected for index expressions.

### Migration Risk

If you change `fn_normalize_code`, the index may become semantically stale.

Safe pattern:

```text id="p1aubh"
1. Create fn_normalize_code_v2
2. Create new index concurrently using v2
3. Switch queries to v2
4. Drop old index
5. Drop old function
```

---

## 11. `SECURITY INVOKER` vs `SECURITY DEFINER`

### `SECURITY INVOKER`

Default. Function runs with privileges of the caller.

```sql id="tnjwkq"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_public_case_count()
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE sql
SECURITY INVOKER
STABLE
AS $$
    SELECT COUNT(*)::INTEGER
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case;
$$;
```

### `SECURITY DEFINER`

Function runs with privileges of the function owner.

```sql id="rd4fuh"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_admin_case_count()
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE sql
SECURITY DEFINER
STABLE
AS $$
    SELECT COUNT(*)::INTEGER
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case;
$$;
```

PostgreSQL docs define `SECURITY INVOKER` as executing with the calling user’s privileges and `SECURITY DEFINER` as executing with the owner’s privileges; invoker is the default. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createfunction.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 12. `SECURITY DEFINER` Search Path Risk

`SECURITY DEFINER` functions are high-risk because callers can execute logic with owner privileges.

PostgreSQL documentation recommends writing `SECURITY DEFINER` functions safely by setting a secure `search_path` that excludes schemas writable by untrusted users, and placing trusted schemas before `pg_temp`. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createfunction.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

### Safer Pattern

```sql id="rrah38"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_admin_case_count()
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE sql
SECURITY DEFINER
SET search_path = case_app, pg_temp
STABLE
AS $$
    SELECT COUNT(*)::INTEGER
    FROM enforcement_case;
$$;
```

Even better: still schema-qualify objects in body:

```sql id="wfwwro"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_admin_case_count()
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE sql
SECURITY DEFINER
SET search_path = case_app, pg_temp
STABLE
AS $$
    SELECT COUNT(*)::INTEGER
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case;
$$;
```

### Review Questions

| Question | Why |
|---|---|
| Why is `SECURITY DEFINER` needed? | Avoid privilege escalation by default. |
| Who owns the function? | Owner privileges are inherited. |
| Is `search_path` locked down? | Prevent object hijacking. |
| Are all objects schema-qualified? | Reduce ambiguity. |
| Who can execute it? | Grants must be explicit. |
| Can untrusted users create objects in schemas on path? | Security risk. |

---

## 13. Function Grants

By default, privilege strategy should be explicit.

```sql id="y6krhf"
REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION case_app.fn_admin_case_count()
FROM PUBLIC;

GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION case_app.fn_admin_case_count()
TO case_app_runtime;
```

PostgreSQL has function execution privileges; if a function is security-sensitive, restrict `EXECUTE` and avoid leaving broad access unintentionally. The `CREATE FUNCTION` docs explicitly warn that `SECURITY DEFINER` functions should be handled carefully and show revoking `PUBLIC` and granting selected users. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createfunction.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

### Practical Role Pattern

```text id="xxjfqn"
case_app_migration:
  owns/creates functions

case_app_runtime:
  EXECUTE only on safe functions needed by app

case_app_reporting:
  EXECUTE on reporting-safe functions

public:
  no execute on sensitive functions
```

---

## 14. Function Dependency Types

| Consumer | Example Risk |
|---|---|
| MyBatis mapper | Query fails if function removed/signature changed. |
| View | View recreation fails if function missing. |
| Materialized view | Refresh fails if function changes/removes output behavior. |
| Trigger function | Trigger fails on write. |
| Procedure | Procedure fails at runtime. |
| Expression index | Index semantics can become invalid. |
| CHECK constraint | Constraint behavior changes. |
| API service | Endpoint returns different semantics. |
| Camunda worker | Job execution or routing changes. |

### Dependency Search

```sql id="nwf9ia"
SELECT
    n.nspname AS schema_name,
    p.proname AS function_name,
    pg_get_functiondef(p.oid) AS function_definition
FROM pg_proc p
JOIN pg_namespace n
    ON n.oid = p.pronamespace
WHERE n.nspname = 'case_app'
  AND pg_get_functiondef(p.oid) ILIKE '%fn_calculate_case_sla%';
```

---

## 15. Function Used by View

Function:

```sql id="sjm9to"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days(
    p_created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
)
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
STABLE
AS $$
BEGIN
    RETURN FLOOR(EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now() - p_created_at)) / 86400)::INTEGER;
END;
$$;
```

View:

```sql id="ssowu7"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days(c.created_at) AS age_days
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c;
```

### Migration Order

```text id="t53jrs"
1. Create/update function
2. Create/update view
3. Test view query
4. Test MyBatis mapper
```

---

## 16. Function Used by MyBatis

Mapper:

```xml id="a5vhe1"
<select id="findCaseAge" resultType="int">
    SELECT case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days(created_at)
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case
    WHERE id = #{id}
</select>
```

If function return type changes from `INTEGER` to `NUMERIC`, Java mapping can break.

### Safe Strategy

Create new function:

```sql id="uy5hd8"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days_v2(
    p_created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
)
RETURNS NUMERIC
LANGUAGE plpgsql
STABLE
AS $$
BEGIN
    RETURN EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now() - p_created_at)) / 86400;
END;
$$;
```

Then update mapper:

```xml id="yvx8ud"
<select id="findCaseAge" resultType="java.math.BigDecimal">
    SELECT case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days_v2(created_at)
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case
    WHERE id = #{id}
</select>
```

Drop v1 later.

---

## 17. Function Used by Trigger

Trigger functions are special: they return `trigger` and are called by triggers.

```sql id="bfbry2"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_set_updated_at()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    NEW.updated_at := now();
    RETURN NEW;
END;
$$;
```

PostgreSQL PL/pgSQL trigger functions are created with `CREATE FUNCTION`, have no declared arguments, and return `trigger` for data-change triggers or `event_trigger` for event triggers; PostgreSQL automatically defines special `TG_` variables in trigger functions. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/plpgsql-trigger.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

### Migration Strategy

```text id="qyba5i"
Trigger function body:
  repeatable migration

Trigger binding:
  versioned migration
```

Because:

```text id="arf7m9"
function body = implementation
trigger binding = structural attachment to table event
```

---

## 18. Function Body Style

### Prefer Explicit Guard

```sql id="78qd1y"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days(
    p_created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
)
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
STABLE
AS $$
BEGIN
    IF p_created_at IS NULL THEN
        RETURN NULL;
    END IF;

    RETURN FLOOR(EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now() - p_created_at)) / 86400)::INTEGER;
END;
$$;
```

### Avoid Hidden Assumptions

Bad:

```sql id="x31a0y"
RETURN FLOOR(EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (now() - p_created_at)) / 86400)::INTEGER;
```

If `p_created_at` is null, result is null, which may be okay, but make it intentional.

### Rule

```text id="htybcl"
Function should make null behavior explicit.
```

---

## 19. Null Handling and Strictness

PostgreSQL functions can be declared `CALLED ON NULL INPUT`, `RETURNS NULL ON NULL INPUT`, or `STRICT`; `STRICT` is equivalent to `RETURNS NULL ON NULL INPUT`. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createfunction.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Example:

```sql id="azv5jr"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_normalize_code(
    p_code TEXT
)
RETURNS TEXT
LANGUAGE sql
IMMUTABLE
STRICT
AS $$
    SELECT upper(trim(p_code));
$$;
```

If input is null, PostgreSQL returns null without executing the function body.

### When to Use `STRICT`

Good for pure transformations where any null input means null output.

Avoid when function has custom null behavior, for example defaulting null to `'UNKNOWN'`.

---

## 20. SQL Function vs PL/pgSQL Function

### SQL Function

```sql id="ruhtv4"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_normalize_code(
    p_code TEXT
)
RETURNS TEXT
LANGUAGE sql
IMMUTABLE
STRICT
AS $$
    SELECT upper(trim(p_code));
$$;
```

Use when function is simple expression/query.

### PL/pgSQL Function

```sql id="o8h438"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla(
    p_case_id UUID
)
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
STABLE
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_priority TEXT;
BEGIN
    SELECT priority
    INTO v_priority
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case
    WHERE id = p_case_id;

    IF v_priority = 'HIGH' THEN
        RETURN 2;
    END IF;

    RETURN 5;
END;
$$;
```

Use when you need:

- variables;
- branching;
- exception handling;
- procedural control;
- multiple statements.

---

## 21. Avoid Too Much Business Logic in DB Function

Function is useful, but do not blindly move application logic into DB.

Good DB function candidates:

| Candidate | Why |
|---|---|
| Deterministic normalization | Close to data and reusable. |
| Query projection helper | Avoid repeated SQL expressions. |
| Trigger helper | Required by trigger. |
| Validation invariant | Shared by DB-side object. |
| Read-model calculation | Useful in views/materialized views. |

Risky DB function candidates:

| Candidate | Risk |
|---|---|
| Complex workflow decision | Harder to version with Camunda. |
| External service orchestration | DB should not call external service normally. |
| Authorization policy | Stale/hidden policy risk if not governed. |
| Large batch processing | Harder observability unless carefully designed. |
| Frequently changing business policy | Migration overhead and rollout complexity. |

### Rule

```text id="uwtoxp"
Put logic in PL/pgSQL when database locality, reuse, integrity, or performance justify it.
Do not use functions as a dumping ground for hidden business logic.
```

---

## 22. Exception Handling

PL/pgSQL supports exception blocks.

Example:

```sql id="w3mml4"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_parse_priority(
    p_priority TEXT
)
RETURNS TEXT
LANGUAGE plpgsql
IMMUTABLE
AS $$
BEGIN
    IF p_priority IS NULL THEN
        RETURN 'MEDIUM';
    END IF;

    IF upper(p_priority) NOT IN ('LOW', 'MEDIUM', 'HIGH', 'CRITICAL') THEN
        RAISE EXCEPTION 'Invalid priority: %', p_priority
            USING ERRCODE = '22023';
    END IF;

    RETURN upper(p_priority);
END;
$$;
```

### Practical Guidance

| Practice | Reason |
|---|---|
| Use clear error messages | Easier debugging from Java/MyBatis. |
| Use SQLSTATE when appropriate | Better exception classification. |
| Avoid swallowing exceptions silently | Data corruption risk. |
| Avoid broad `WHEN OTHERS` unless re-raising/logging | Hidden failures. |

---

## 23. Function Returning Table

Example:

```sql id="pgudfv"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_find_cases_by_status(
    p_status TEXT
)
RETURNS TABLE (
    id UUID,
    case_number TEXT,
    status TEXT,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ
)
LANGUAGE sql
STABLE
AS $$
    SELECT
        c.id,
        c.case_number,
        c.status,
        c.created_at
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
    WHERE c.status = p_status;
$$;
```

### Risk

This is essentially a query contract. Changing output columns breaks consumers.

For MyBatis, a view is often simpler and more transparent than table-returning function unless parameters are needed.

---

## 24. Function Grants for Table-Returning Function

```sql id="ykq5k8"
REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION case_app.fn_find_cases_by_status(TEXT)
FROM PUBLIC;

GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION case_app.fn_find_cases_by_status(TEXT)
TO case_app_runtime;
```

If it reads sensitive tables under `SECURITY DEFINER`, review carefully.

---

## 25. Function Comments

```sql id="1mxl60"
COMMENT ON FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days(TIMESTAMPTZ) IS
'Calculates whole number of days between created_at and current transaction timestamp. Used by v_case_summary and CaseMapper.';
```

Function comments are useful for onboarding, catalog introspection, and dependency documentation.

---

## 26. Drop Function Safely

Dangerous:

```sql id="1npzef"
DROP FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla(UUID) CASCADE;
```

`CASCADE` can drop dependent views, triggers, constraints, indexes, or other objects.

Safer:

```sql id="o67b6v"
DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla(UUID) RESTRICT;
```

### Pre-Drop Checklist

```text id="iwdxd7"
- search MyBatis mapper
- search Java code
- search views
- search materialized views
- search procedures
- search trigger functions
- search expression indexes
- search constraints
- search Camunda worker/process queries
```

---

## 27. Detect Function Dependencies

### Find function definition

```sql id="h09jum"
SELECT
    n.nspname AS schema_name,
    p.proname AS function_name,
    pg_get_function_arguments(p.oid) AS arguments,
    pg_get_function_result(p.oid) AS result_type,
    pg_get_functiondef(p.oid) AS definition
FROM pg_proc p
JOIN pg_namespace n
    ON n.oid = p.pronamespace
WHERE n.nspname = 'case_app'
  AND p.proname = 'fn_calculate_case_sla';
```

### Find DB object dependencies

```sql id="ku7nna"
SELECT
    dependent_ns.nspname AS dependent_schema,
    dependent_class.relname AS dependent_object,
    dependent_class.relkind AS dependent_kind
FROM pg_depend d
JOIN pg_proc p
    ON p.oid = d.refobjid
JOIN pg_namespace fn_ns
    ON fn_ns.oid = p.pronamespace
LEFT JOIN pg_rewrite r
    ON r.oid = d.objid
LEFT JOIN pg_class dependent_class
    ON dependent_class.oid = r.ev_class
LEFT JOIN pg_namespace dependent_ns
    ON dependent_ns.oid = dependent_class.relnamespace
WHERE fn_ns.nspname = 'case_app'
  AND p.proname = 'fn_calculate_case_sla';
```

This catches some DB dependencies, especially view rewrite dependencies. Still also text-search SQL code.

---

## 28. Repository Search

```bash id="o3a6ud"
grep -R "fn_calculate_case_sla" \
  src/main/java \
  src/main/resources/mybatis \
  src/main/resources/db \
  src/main/resources/**/*.bpmn \
  src/main/resources/**/*.dmn \
  --line-number
```

Search paths depend on repository layout.

---

## 29. Flyway Function Layout

```text id="zma7hq"
src/main/resources/db/migration/
├─ versioned/
│  ├─ V001200__case_create_fn_calculate_case_sla_v2.sql
│  ├─ V001230__case_drop_fn_calculate_case_sla_v1.sql
│  └─ V001240__case_grant_fn_calculate_case_sla_v2.sql
│
└─ repeatable/
   ├─ R__010_case_fn_normalize_code.sql
   ├─ R__020_case_fn_calculate_case_age_days.sql
   ├─ R__030_case_fn_calculate_case_sla.sql
   └─ R__040_case_trg_fn_set_updated_at.sql
```

### Naming Rule

```text id="jmm6od"
R__<order>_<domain>_fn_<name>.sql
R__<order>_<domain>_trg_fn_<name>.sql
```

Trigger function uses `trg_fn` prefix to signal it returns trigger and is not called directly by application.

---

## 30. Liquibase Function Layout

```text id="78msln"
src/main/resources/db/changelog/700-functions/
├─ 701-fn-normalize-code.yaml
├─ 702-fn-calculate-case-age-days.yaml
├─ 703-fn-calculate-case-sla.yaml
└─ sql/
   ├─ fn_normalize_code.sql
   ├─ fn_calculate_case_age_days.sql
   └─ fn_calculate_case_sla.sql
```

Wrapper:

```yaml id="653761"
databaseChangeLog:
  - changeSet:
      id: 702-case-fn-calculate-case-age-days
      author: case-service-team
      runOnChange: true
      labels: function,plpgsql,case-domain
      changes:
        - sqlFile:
            path: src/main/resources/db/changelog/700-functions/sql/fn_calculate_case_age_days.sql
            splitStatements: false
            stripComments: false
      rollback:
        - sql:
            sql: |
              DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days(TIMESTAMPTZ);
```

---

## 31. Ordering with Views / Materialized Views / Triggers

Recommended order:

```text id="jzykft"
1. Tables
2. Reference data
3. Base helper functions
4. Trigger functions
5. Views
6. Materialized views
7. Procedures
8. Trigger bindings
9. Grants
10. Consumer app deploy
```

Nuance:

- trigger function must exist before trigger binding;
- view function must exist before view creation;
- materialized view function must exist before matview definition/refresh;
- grants may need to happen after function creation/replacement.

---

## 32. Testing Functions

### Test Function Directly

```sql id="p5eik6"
SELECT case_app.fn_normalize_code(' open ') AS normalized_code;
```

Expected:

```text id="ejzyev"
OPEN
```

### Test Null Behavior

```sql id="6wcp04"
SELECT case_app.fn_normalize_code(NULL) AS normalized_code;
```

Expected depends on `STRICT` or custom logic.

### Test Boundary Cases

```sql id="npu3j7"
SELECT case_app.fn_parse_priority('INVALID');
```

Expected exception.

### Test View/Mapper Consumer

```text id="x1068n"
function test alone is not enough.
Also test the views/mappers/procedures that call it.
```

---

## 33. MyBatis Integration Test Example

Mapper:

```xml id="nh7vwi"
<select id="calculateCaseAgeDays" resultType="int">
    SELECT case_app.fn_calculate_case_age_days(created_at)
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case
    WHERE id = #{id}
</select>
```

Test flow:

```text id="ah0yrl"
1. Start PostgreSQL
2. Run migration
3. Insert test case row
4. Call mapper
5. Assert returned value
```

This catches:

- function missing;
- grant missing;
- return type mismatch;
- SQL syntax issue;
- mapper result mapping mismatch.

---

## 34. Function and API Contract

If Jersey API exposes function output:

```json id="e6h3ud"
{
  "caseAgeDays": 12
}
```

Changing function semantics changes API behavior.

Example semantic change:

```text id="q2udwk"
old: calendar days
new: business days
```

Even if function signature remains same, this is breaking for consumers if the API contract promised calendar days.

### Rule

```text id="u81zzp"
Function body change can still be a contract change
if consumers depend on semantics.
```

Use release notes, tests, and possibly new function name:

```text id="tjcbpo"
fn_calculate_case_age_calendar_days
fn_calculate_case_age_business_days
```

---

## 35. Function and Camunda

If a Camunda worker or decision flow depends on function result, be careful.

Example:

```text id="z3jn33"
worker queries fn_calculate_escalation_days(case_id)
if result > threshold → create escalation task
```

Changing function logic can change workflow behavior for active process instances.

Checklist:

```text id="w1k0qd"
- Is function used by worker?
- Is result stored as process variable?
- Is function result used in BPMN/DMN condition?
- Does old process version expect old semantics?
- Does change need process definition migration?
```

---

## 36. Security Review Checklist

For every function:

| Question | Required Review |
|---|---|
| Is function `SECURITY DEFINER`? | Security review required. |
| Is `search_path` set? | Must be secure. |
| Are object references schema-qualified? | Recommended. |
| Is `EXECUTE` granted to PUBLIC? | Usually no for sensitive functions. |
| Does function access sensitive data? | Privilege review. |
| Does function perform writes? | Must be `VOLATILE`; review side effects. |
| Does function use dynamic SQL? | SQL injection review. |
| Does function call other functions? | Dependency/security review. |
| Is function used in RLS/security policy? | High-risk review. |

---

## 37. Dynamic SQL Warning

Example risky function:

```sql id="0c4en0"
EXECUTE 'SELECT count(*) FROM ' || p_table_name;
```

Better:

```sql id="ngnx43"
EXECUTE format(
    'SELECT count(*) FROM %I.%I',
    p_schema_name,
    p_table_name
);
```

Dynamic SQL inside `SECURITY DEFINER` functions is especially sensitive because untrusted input plus elevated privileges can create SQL injection or privilege escalation risk.

This series will cover dynamic SQL in more depth in later PL/pgSQL-specific material, but for function migration review, flag all `EXECUTE` usage.

---

## 38. Function Review Checklist

| Question | Why |
|---|---|
| Is signature unchanged? | Determines repeatable vs versioned migration. |
| Is return type unchanged? | Java/MyBatis mapping risk. |
| Is volatility correct? | Query planner and correctness. |
| Is null behavior explicit? | Avoid surprise output. |
| Is security mode appropriate? | Privilege risk. |
| Is `search_path` safe for definer function? | Prevent hijacking. |
| Are grants explicit? | Runtime access. |
| Does it write data? | Side effect and transaction risk. |
| Does it use dynamic SQL? | SQL injection risk. |
| Does it call other functions? | Dependency ordering. |
| Is it used by view/materialized view? | Refresh/query risk. |
| Is it used by trigger? | Write-path risk. |
| Is it used by MyBatis? | Mapper compatibility. |
| Does semantic meaning change? | API/workflow contract. |
| Are tests updated? | Prevent runtime failure. |

---

## 39. CI/CD Gates for Function Changes

```text id="y0hg6l"
Pull Request
  ├─ Detect function SQL changes
  ├─ Run migration on fresh PostgreSQL
  ├─ Validate function exists
  ├─ Validate grants
  ├─ Run direct SQL function tests
  ├─ Run dependent view/matview tests
  ├─ Run MyBatis integration tests
  ├─ Detect SECURITY DEFINER
  ├─ Detect dynamic SQL EXECUTE
  ├─ Detect DROP FUNCTION CASCADE
  └─ Require DB/security reviewer for risky changes
```

### Dangerous Pattern Detector

```bash id="tpwmj7"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

ROOT="${1:-src/main/resources/db}"

if grep -RniE "DROP FUNCTION .*CASCADE" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "DROP FUNCTION CASCADE detected. Requires explicit dependency review."
  exit 1
fi

if grep -RniE "SECURITY DEFINER" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "SECURITY DEFINER detected. Requires security review."
  exit 1
fi

if grep -RniE "EXECUTE\s+['\"]|EXECUTE\s+format" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "Dynamic SQL detected. Requires SQL injection review."
  exit 1
fi
```

This script is intentionally conservative.

---

## 40. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Why Dangerous | Better |
|---|---|---|
| Function without schema qualification | `search_path` ambiguity. | Use `case_app.fn_name`. |
| No volatility declared | Defaults to `VOLATILE`, may hurt performance. | Declare correct volatility. |
| Marking non-deterministic logic `IMMUTABLE` | Wrong query plans/results. | Use `STABLE`/`VOLATILE`. |
| `SECURITY DEFINER` without secure `search_path` | Privilege escalation risk. | Set secure path and review grants. |
| Granting sensitive function to `PUBLIC` | Broad access. | Revoke and grant explicitly. |
| Changing return type in place | MyBatis/API break. | Create v2 function. |
| Changing semantics silently | Business/API contract drift. | Version function or release note/test. |
| Dropping function with `CASCADE` | Dependent object loss. | Use `RESTRICT` and dependency cleanup. |
| Dynamic SQL without quoting | SQL injection risk. | Use `format('%I', identifier)` / bind values. |
| Putting complex workflow logic in DB function | Harder rollout with Camunda. | Keep workflow logic visible or versioned. |
| No direct function tests | Runtime failure found late. | SQL + mapper integration tests. |

---

## 41. Practical Example: Function Lifecycle

### Requirement

Calculate SLA days based on priority.

### v1 Function

```sql id="y1exnq"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla_days(
    p_priority TEXT
)
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
IMMUTABLE
STRICT
AS $$
BEGIN
    CASE upper(p_priority)
        WHEN 'CRITICAL' THEN RETURN 1;
        WHEN 'HIGH' THEN RETURN 2;
        WHEN 'MEDIUM' THEN RETURN 5;
        WHEN 'LOW' THEN RETURN 10;
        ELSE
            RAISE EXCEPTION 'Unsupported priority: %', p_priority
                USING ERRCODE = '22023';
    END CASE;
END;
$$;
```

### Grant

```sql id="d4tm2e"
REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla_days(TEXT)
FROM PUBLIC;

GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla_days(TEXT)
TO case_app_runtime;
```

### View Usage

```sql id="gwqk55"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    c.priority,
    case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla_days(c.priority) AS sla_days
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c;
```

### Test

```sql id="ug4hir"
SELECT case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla_days('HIGH') AS sla_days;
```

Expected:

```text id="npk9qz"
2
```

### Change Requirement

SLA now depends on case type too.

### v2 Function

```sql id="wynfcx"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla_days_v2(
    p_priority TEXT,
    p_case_type TEXT
)
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
IMMUTABLE
STRICT
AS $$
BEGIN
    IF upper(p_case_type) = 'URGENT_INVESTIGATION' THEN
        RETURN 1;
    END IF;

    CASE upper(p_priority)
        WHEN 'CRITICAL' THEN RETURN 1;
        WHEN 'HIGH' THEN RETURN 2;
        WHEN 'MEDIUM' THEN RETURN 5;
        WHEN 'LOW' THEN RETURN 10;
        ELSE
            RAISE EXCEPTION 'Unsupported priority: %', p_priority
                USING ERRCODE = '22023';
    END CASE;
END;
$$;
```

### Migration Flow

```text id="fi5nmg"
1. Create v2 function
2. Update view to expose sla_days_v2 or replace in v2 view
3. Update mapper/service
4. Verify no v1 callers remain
5. Drop v1 in later contract release
```

---

## 42. Function Documentation Template

```md id="n51sqk"
# Function: case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla_days(TEXT)

## Purpose
Calculates SLA day threshold from case priority.

## Owner
case-service-team

## Consumers
- case_app.v_case_summary
- CaseMapper.findCaseSummary
- GET /cases/{id}/summary

## Signature
fn_calculate_case_sla_days(p_priority TEXT) RETURNS INTEGER

## Volatility
IMMUTABLE

## Security
SECURITY INVOKER
EXECUTE granted to case_app_runtime

## Null Behavior
STRICT; null input returns null.

## Error Behavior
Raises SQLSTATE 22023 for unsupported priority.

## Change Policy
Body changes require direct SQL tests and mapper tests.
Signature or return type changes require v2 function.
```

---

## 43. Decision Tree

```text id="eog6s7"
Are you changing only function body?
  ├─ Yes
  │   ├─ Same signature and return type?
  │   │   ├─ Yes → repeatable migration
  │   │   └─ No → versioned migration / v2 function
  │
  └─ No
      ├─ Changing arguments?
      │   └─ Create v2 function
      ├─ Changing return type?
      │   └─ Create v2 function
      ├─ Changing volatility?
      │   └─ DB review; check indexes/views/query plans
      ├─ Changing SECURITY DEFINER?
      │   └─ Security review
      ├─ Dropping function?
      │   └─ Dependency search + contract release
      └─ Changing semantics?
          └─ Treat as API/business contract change
```

---

## 44. Production Review Checklist

| Area | Must Be True |
|---|---|
| Signature | Compatible or versioned. |
| Return type | Compatible with callers. |
| Volatility | Correct and reviewed. |
| Security | Definer functions reviewed and search path secured. |
| Grants | Explicit and least privilege. |
| Dependencies | Views/matviews/triggers/procedures/mappers checked. |
| MyBatis | Mapper tests pass. |
| API | Exposed semantics reviewed. |
| Camunda | Workflow impact checked. |
| Dynamic SQL | Quoted/parameterized and reviewed. |
| Rollback | Old function retained until consumers migrate. |
| Drop | No `CASCADE` without explicit dependency approval. |
| Observability | Errors are visible in app logs/DB logs. |

---

## 45. Ringkasan Seri 11

| Area | Kesimpulan |
|---|---|
| Function | Database code with contract and runtime behavior. |
| Signature | Name + argument types are critical; treat as contract. |
| Repeatable migration | Good for same-signature body changes. |
| Versioned migration | Use for new function, signature change, return type change, drop. |
| Function versioning | Use `_v2` or new descriptive function for breaking changes. |
| Volatility | `VOLATILE`, `STABLE`, `IMMUTABLE` affect correctness and planning. |
| Security | `SECURITY DEFINER` is powerful and risky; secure `search_path`. |
| Grants | Revoke broad access for sensitive functions; grant explicitly. |
| MyBatis | Function return type and semantics affect mapper/API. |
| View/materialized view | Function order and dependency must be managed. |
| Trigger | Trigger function body repeatable, trigger binding versioned. |
| Dynamic SQL | High-risk, especially with definer functions. |
| Drop | Avoid `CASCADE`; dependency search first. |
| Testing | Direct SQL tests + dependent mapper/view tests. |

---

# Seri 12 - Stored Procedure Migration

## 1. Tujuan Seri Ini

Seri ini membahas bagaimana mengelola **PostgreSQL stored procedure** dalam database migration untuk microservice berbasis:

```text id="e64xel"
Jersey / JAX-RS
MyBatis
PostgreSQL
Flyway / Liquibase
Camunda
CI/CD
Kubernetes
```

Stored procedure berbeda dari function. Dalam PostgreSQL, procedure dipanggil dengan `CALL`, bukan `SELECT`, dan procedure dapat dipakai untuk operasi server-side yang bersifat **operational**, seperti refresh materialized view, batch repair, maintenance task, atau proses administrasi. PostgreSQL `CALL` mengeksekusi procedure dan dapat mengembalikan result row jika procedure punya output parameter. ([postgresql.org](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-call.html))

Perbedaan paling penting: procedure dapat melakukan transaction control dalam kondisi tertentu, tetapi ada batasan. PostgreSQL PL/pgSQL documentation menunjukkan procedure dapat memakai `COMMIT` dan `ROLLBACK`, tetapi transaction control hanya mungkin pada pemanggilan `CALL`/`DO` tertentu tanpa intervening command. ([postgresql.org](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/plpgsql-transactions.html))

---

## 2. Mental Model

```text id="ajd6kl"
Stored Procedure Migration Mental Model
────────────────────────────────────────────

Procedure
  │
  ├─ name + argument types
  ├─ operational intent
  ├─ transaction behavior
  ├─ privilege model
  ├─ side effects
  ├─ observability
  └─ runbook
       │
       ▼
Consumers
  ├─ migration job
  ├─ Kubernetes CronJob
  ├─ admin operation
  ├─ MyBatis CALL
  ├─ Jersey admin endpoint
  ├─ batch worker
  └─ Camunda service task / worker
```

Core rule:

```text id="9vzyf3"
Function is usually expression/query-oriented.
Procedure is usually operation-oriented.
```

---

## 3. Function vs Procedure

| Aspect | Function | Procedure |
|---|---|---|
| Invocation | `SELECT fn(...)` | `CALL proc(...)` |
| Return | `RETURNS ...` | No `RETURNS`; can use output/INOUT parameters. |
| Typical use | Compute value, transform data, query helper. | Operational task, maintenance, batch action. |
| Transaction control | Cannot directly commit/rollback independently in normal function execution. | Can use transaction control in allowed contexts. |
| Use in SQL expression | Yes | No. |
| Use in view | Yes | No. |
| Use in index expression | Yes, if suitable | No. |
| Use in trigger | Trigger uses function, not procedure. |
| MyBatis use | `SELECT fn(...)` | `{ call proc(...) }` / `CALL proc(...)` |
| Migration strategy | Repeatable for body, versioned for signature. | Repeatable for body, versioned for operational contract/signature. |

PostgreSQL procedure syntax is defined by `CREATE PROCEDURE`, and procedure can be written in languages such as SQL, C, internal, or user-defined procedural languages like PL/pgSQL. ([postgresql.org](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createprocedure.html))

---

## 4. Basic Procedure

```sql id="svghrq"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_refresh_case_status_metrics()
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;
END;
$$;
```

Call:

```sql id="3t5uyv"
CALL case_app.proc_refresh_case_status_metrics();
```

This is useful when the refresh should be exposed as a controlled operation, not repeated as raw SQL across scripts, jobs, and admin tools.

---

## 5. Procedure as Operational Boundary

Procedure is good when you want one named operation:

```text id="g8jc40"
refresh metrics
repair data
recalculate SLA
archive old rows
rebuild derived state
mark stuck workflow items
backfill missing reference values
```

Bad use:

```text id="4otueg"
hide critical business workflow inside DB procedure
without tests, ownership, logs, or release process
```

### Rule

```text id="pl5qzg"
If a procedure changes business state,
it needs the same review level as application code.
```

---

## 6. Procedure Signature as Contract

Example:

```sql id="t7l5cf"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_recalculate_case_sla(
    p_case_id UUID
)
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    -- recalculation logic
END;
$$;
```

The caller depends on:

```text id="5q4qs0"
procedure name
schema
input argument types
INOUT/OUT behavior
side effects
transaction behavior
error behavior
```

Changing any of these can break:

- MyBatis call;
- admin endpoint;
- batch worker;
- Kubernetes CronJob;
- Camunda worker;
- manual runbook.

---

## 7. `CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE`

Body changes with same signature are usually safe for repeatable migration:

```sql id="anp9y5"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_refresh_case_status_metrics()
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;
END;
$$;
```

But changing operational semantics can still be breaking.

Example semantic change:

```text id="v67mt8"
old:
  refresh materialized view only

new:
  refresh materialized view and update dashboard cache state
```

Even if signature remains same, it changes side effects.

### Rule

```text id="cv7vfe"
Same signature does not automatically mean same operational contract.
```

---

## 8. Versioned vs Repeatable Procedure Migration

### Repeatable Procedure Migration

Use when:

```text id="kdjo7i"
same name
same arguments
same privilege model
same operational intent
body implementation refined
```

Flyway:

```text id="x3l6l5"
R__080_case_proc_refresh_case_metrics.sql
```

Liquibase:

```yaml id="onjmmn"
databaseChangeLog:
  - changeSet:
      id: 801-case-proc-refresh-case-metrics
      author: case-service-team
      runOnChange: true
      labels: procedure,reporting
      changes:
        - sqlFile:
            path: src/main/resources/db/changelog/800-procedures/sql/proc_refresh_case_metrics.sql
            splitStatements: false
            stripComments: false
```

### Versioned Procedure Migration

Use when:

```text id="eu5fq9"
new procedure
signature change
privilege/security change
transaction behavior change
major side effect change
drop old procedure
```

Example:

```text id="0uk07f"
V001300__case_create_proc_recalculate_sla_v2.sql
V001330__case_drop_proc_recalculate_sla_v1.sql
```

---

## 9. Procedure Versioning Pattern

### Existing

```sql id="ly3quw"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_recalculate_case_sla(
    p_case_id UUID
)
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    -- old logic
END;
$$;
```

### New Requirement

Need recalculation mode:

```sql id="wtoqxi"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_recalculate_case_sla_v2(
    p_case_id UUID,
    p_mode TEXT
)
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    -- new logic
END;
$$;
```

### Rollout

```text id="22yzqt"
Release 1:
  create proc_recalculate_case_sla_v2

Release 2:
  update MyBatis/admin job/Camunda worker to call v2

Release 3:
  monitor usage and errors

Release 4:
  drop v1 after dependency search and no usage
```

---

## 10. Transaction Control in Procedures

PL/pgSQL procedures can use transaction commands in certain contexts.

Example from PostgreSQL docs conceptually:

```sql id="s4kwc0"
CREATE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_transaction_example()
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    INSERT INTO case_app.operation_log(message)
    VALUES ('step 1');

    COMMIT;

    INSERT INTO case_app.operation_log(message)
    VALUES ('step 2');

    COMMIT;
END;
$$;
```

```sql id="wb99ys"
CALL case_app.proc_transaction_example();
```

PostgreSQL PL/pgSQL docs explain that transaction commands are only possible in `CALL` or `DO` invocations from the top level, or nested `CALL`/`DO` invocations without any intervening command; if a `SELECT` occurs between calls, transaction control inside the later procedure is not allowed. ([postgresql.org](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/plpgsql-transactions.html))

### Practical Meaning for Java

If Java code already starts a transaction and then calls a procedure that tries to `COMMIT`, you can hit invalid transaction behavior.

For Java/Jersey/MyBatis:

```text id="t8th0o"
Do not casually put COMMIT/ROLLBACK inside procedure
called from application-managed transaction.
```

---

## 11. Procedure Transaction Modes

| Procedure Type | Transaction Control Inside? | Best Caller |
|---|---:|---|
| Pure operational procedure without `COMMIT` | No | App transaction, job, migration. |
| Procedure that does batch commits | Yes, if allowed context | Dedicated job / direct `CALL`, not app-managed transaction. |
| Security definer procedure | No transaction control allowed if `SECURITY DEFINER`. | Controlled admin call. |
| Procedure with `SET` clause | Cannot execute transaction control statements. | Use for environment/session config, not internal commit. |

PostgreSQL `CREATE PROCEDURE` documentation states that a `SECURITY DEFINER` procedure cannot execute transaction control statements, and if a `SET` clause is attached to a procedure, that procedure cannot execute transaction control statements. ([postgresql.org](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createprocedure.html))

---

## 12. Transaction Control Anti-Pattern

Bad procedure for app call:

```sql id="wdqlnm"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_close_case(
    p_case_id UUID
)
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
    SET status = 'CLOSED'
    WHERE id = p_case_id;

    COMMIT;
END;
$$;
```

If called from service transaction:

```java id="e2w8i6"
transactionManager.inTransaction(() -> {
    caseMapper.closeCase(caseId);
    auditMapper.writeAudit(caseId);
});
```

The procedure’s internal commit fights the application’s transaction boundary.

Better:

```sql id="58jwq3"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_close_case(
    p_case_id UUID
)
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
    SET status = 'CLOSED'
    WHERE id = p_case_id;
END;
$$;
```

Let application or job own transaction boundary.

---

## 13. Procedure with INOUT Parameter

Procedures can use INOUT parameters to return values.

```sql id="6153ge"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_refresh_case_status_metrics(
    INOUT p_refreshed_rows BIGINT
)
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;

    SELECT COUNT(*)
    INTO p_refreshed_rows
    FROM case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;
END;
$$;
```

Call:

```sql id="myn71h"
CALL case_app.proc_refresh_case_status_metrics(NULL);
```

If procedure has output parameters, `CALL` returns a result row containing those values. ([postgresql.org](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-call.html))

### Practical Caution

For Java/MyBatis, output parameters need explicit mapping. Simpler often:

```text id="0z736f"
Procedure writes result to operation log table.
App reads log/status table.
```

---

## 14. Procedure Operation Log Pattern

Create operation log:

```sql id="6sfrb3"
CREATE TABLE case_app.operation_log (
    operation_id      UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    operation_name    TEXT NOT NULL,
    status            TEXT NOT NULL,
    started_at        TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    completed_at      TIMESTAMPTZ,
    affected_rows     BIGINT,
    error_message     TEXT,
    requested_by      TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT current_user
);
```

Procedure:

```sql id="g0p751"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_refresh_case_status_metrics(
    p_operation_id UUID
)
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_row_count BIGINT;
BEGIN
    INSERT INTO case_app.operation_log (
        operation_id,
        operation_name,
        status
    )
    VALUES (
        p_operation_id,
        'refresh_case_status_metrics',
        'RUNNING'
    );

    REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;

    SELECT COUNT(*)
    INTO v_row_count
    FROM case_app.mv_case_status_metrics;

    UPDATE case_app.operation_log
    SET
        status = 'SUCCESS',
        completed_at = now(),
        affected_rows = v_row_count
    WHERE operation_id = p_operation_id;

EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS THEN
    UPDATE case_app.operation_log
    SET
        status = 'FAILED',
        completed_at = now(),
        error_message = SQLERRM
    WHERE operation_id = p_operation_id;

    RAISE;
END;
$$;
```

This makes procedure execution observable.

---

## 15. Procedure for Materialized View Refresh

This is one of the best use cases.

```sql id="nfi2ct"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_refresh_case_dashboard_metrics()
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY case_app.mv_case_dashboard_metrics;

    INSERT INTO case_app.read_model_refresh_state (
        read_model_name,
        last_refresh_completed_at,
        last_refresh_status,
        updated_at
    )
    VALUES (
        'mv_case_dashboard_metrics',
        now(),
        'SUCCESS',
        now()
    )
    ON CONFLICT (read_model_name) DO UPDATE
    SET
        last_refresh_completed_at = EXCLUDED.last_refresh_completed_at,
        last_refresh_status = 'SUCCESS',
        updated_at = now();
END;
$$;
```

### Why Procedure Helps

| Without Procedure | With Procedure |
|---|---|
| CronJob embeds raw refresh SQL. | CronJob calls named operation. |
| Audit scattered. | Audit centralized. |
| Error handling duplicated. | Error handling centralized. |
| Refresh state inconsistent. | Refresh state standard. |

---

## 16. Procedure for Backfill

Procedure can coordinate chunked backfill, but be careful with transaction design.

### Safer No-Internal-Commit Procedure

```sql id="ssab1w"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_backfill_case_risk_score_batch(
    p_limit INTEGER
)
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    WITH batch AS (
        SELECT id
        FROM case_app.enforcement_case
        WHERE risk_score IS NULL
        ORDER BY id
        LIMIT p_limit
    )
    UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case c
    SET risk_score = 0
    FROM batch
    WHERE c.id = batch.id;
END;
$$;
```

Caller controls loop and commit per batch:

```text id="1ae0by"
job loop:
  CALL proc_backfill_case_risk_score_batch(1000)
  COMMIT
  sleep
  repeat until no rows left
```

### Why Better

- Java/Kubernetes job can observe progress;
- retry logic is outside DB;
- transaction boundary clear;
- easier to stop/resume.

---

## 17. Procedure with Internal Commit: Use Sparingly

Example:

```sql id="hnp9pr"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_backfill_with_internal_commits()
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_count INTEGER;
BEGIN
    LOOP
        WITH batch AS (
            SELECT id
            FROM case_app.enforcement_case
            WHERE risk_score IS NULL
            ORDER BY id
            LIMIT 1000
        )
        UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case c
        SET risk_score = 0
        FROM batch
        WHERE c.id = batch.id;

        GET DIAGNOSTICS v_count = ROW_COUNT;

        COMMIT;

        EXIT WHEN v_count = 0;
    END LOOP;
END;
$$;
```

Use only when:

- called as top-level `CALL`;
- not inside app transaction;
- not `SECURITY DEFINER`;
- no `SET` clause attached;
- runbook explicitly says how to call;
- tested in staging.

---

## 18. MyBatis Calling Procedure

### Simple CALL

```xml id="0aex3c"
<update id="refreshCaseStatusMetrics">
    CALL case_app.proc_refresh_case_status_metrics()
</update>
```

### With Parameter

```xml id="j6h8n0"
<update id="backfillRiskScoreBatch">
    CALL case_app.proc_backfill_case_risk_score_batch(#{limit})
</update>
```

### With INOUT/OUT

MyBatis can support callable statements, but mapping is more complex. For critical operations, prefer:

```text id="xvd1lf"
CALL procedure with operation_id
procedure writes operation_log
application reads operation_log
```

This is easier to observe, retry, and audit.

---

## 19. Jersey Admin Endpoint Calling Procedure

Example endpoint:

```text id="7gg4ux"
POST /admin/read-models/case-dashboard-metrics/refresh
```

Service flow:

```text id="yyt62g"
1. Check admin permission
2. Generate operation_id
3. Call procedure with operation_id
4. Return operation_id
5. Client polls operation status
```

Do not expose arbitrary procedure execution over HTTP.

### Security Rule

```text id="1zdvet"
Admin endpoint should call one specific approved procedure.
Never accept procedure name as request input.
```

---

## 20. Procedure and Camunda

Procedure can be called from a worker/service task for operational side effects, but use caution.

Good:

```text id="ba31p1"
Camunda job triggers nightly recalculation procedure
with explicit operation id and audit state.
```

Risky:

```text id="lpw31f"
BPMN path depends on a procedure with hidden transaction control,
unclear retry semantics, and non-idempotent writes.
```

### Camunda Worker Checklist

| Question | Why |
|---|---|
| Is procedure idempotent? | Camunda jobs may retry. |
| Does procedure partially commit? | Retry behavior changes. |
| Does it write operation log? | Need audit and recovery. |
| What exception is thrown to worker? | Determines incident/retry. |
| Can it run concurrently? | Avoid duplicate side effects. |
| Is it tied to process version? | Active instances may expect old behavior. |

---

## 21. Idempotency

Procedure that may be retried must be idempotent or have operation key.

Bad:

```sql id="3xbxk6"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_create_escalation_task(
    p_case_id UUID
)
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    INSERT INTO case_app.case_task (case_id, task_type)
    VALUES (p_case_id, 'ESCALATION');
END;
$$;
```

Retry creates duplicate task.

Better:

```sql id="15pnbc"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_create_escalation_task(
    p_case_id UUID
)
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    INSERT INTO case_app.case_task (case_id, task_type)
    VALUES (p_case_id, 'ESCALATION')
    ON CONFLICT (case_id, task_type) DO NOTHING;
END;
$$;
```

Even better: include operation id and audit state.

---

## 22. Concurrency Control

Procedure that mutates shared state should consider locking.

Example:

```sql id="kcweiz"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_assign_case(
    p_case_id UUID,
    p_assignee_id UUID
)
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_current_status TEXT;
BEGIN
    SELECT status
    INTO v_current_status
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case
    WHERE id = p_case_id
    FOR UPDATE;

    IF v_current_status = 'CLOSED' THEN
        RAISE EXCEPTION 'Cannot assign closed case %', p_case_id
            USING ERRCODE = '22023';
    END IF;

    UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
    SET assignee_id = p_assignee_id,
        updated_at = now()
    WHERE id = p_case_id;
END;
$$;
```

### Notes

`FOR UPDATE` protects row against concurrent modification in same transaction. But it can also create blocking behavior. Application/service timeout and transaction timeout must be aligned.

---

## 23. Procedure Error Handling

```sql id="kx6obe"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_close_case(
    p_case_id UUID
)
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_status TEXT;
BEGIN
    SELECT status
    INTO v_status
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case
    WHERE id = p_case_id
    FOR UPDATE;

    IF NOT FOUND THEN
        RAISE EXCEPTION 'Case not found: %', p_case_id
            USING ERRCODE = 'P0002';
    END IF;

    IF v_status = 'CLOSED' THEN
        RETURN;
    END IF;

    UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
    SET status = 'CLOSED',
        updated_at = now()
    WHERE id = p_case_id;

EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS THEN
    -- Optional audit can be added here if operation id exists.
    RAISE;
END;
$$;
```

### Rule

```text id="my47f0"
Do not swallow errors unless the procedure records an explicit compensating state.
```

---

## 24. Procedure Security

Like functions, procedures can be `SECURITY INVOKER` or `SECURITY DEFINER`; invoker is default, definer runs with owner privileges. ([postgresql.org](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createprocedure.html))

### Default

```sql id="y0sq8x"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_refresh_case_metrics()
LANGUAGE plpgsql
SECURITY INVOKER
AS $$
BEGIN
    REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY case_app.mv_case_dashboard_metrics;
END;
$$;
```

### Definer

```sql id="31zldc"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_admin_repair_case_state(
    p_case_id UUID
)
LANGUAGE plpgsql
SECURITY DEFINER
SET search_path = case_app, pg_temp
AS $$
BEGIN
    -- privileged repair
END;
$$;
```

### Security Review Required

If `SECURITY DEFINER`:

```text id="a5wnqe"
- set safe search_path
- schema-qualify objects
- revoke public execute
- grant only approved role
- validate dynamic SQL
- document operational purpose
```

---

## 25. Grants

```sql id="ssay5a"
REVOKE ALL ON PROCEDURE case_app.proc_admin_repair_case_state(UUID)
FROM PUBLIC;

GRANT EXECUTE ON PROCEDURE case_app.proc_admin_repair_case_state(UUID)
TO case_app_admin;
```

Runtime application should not automatically get execute access to every procedure.

### Recommended Role Split

| Role | Access |
|---|---|
| `case_app_runtime` | Execute only app-required safe procedures. |
| `case_app_admin` | Execute admin/repair procedures. |
| `case_app_refresh` | Execute refresh procedures. |
| `case_app_migration` | Create/replace/drop procedures. |
| `PUBLIC` | Usually no execute for sensitive procedures. |

---

## 26. Procedure with `SET` Clause

A procedure can have `SET` clauses for configuration during execution, but PostgreSQL docs state that if a `SET` clause is attached to a procedure, that procedure cannot execute transaction control statements like `COMMIT` and `ROLLBACK`. ([postgresql.org](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createprocedure.html))

Example:

```sql id="dk14xv"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_refresh_case_metrics()
LANGUAGE plpgsql
SET lock_timeout = '5s'
SET statement_timeout = '10min'
AS $$
BEGIN
    REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY case_app.mv_case_dashboard_metrics;
END;
$$;
```

Good for:

- lock timeout;
- statement timeout;
- controlled search path;
- other local execution parameters.

Not compatible with internal transaction control.

---

## 27. Procedure Comments

```sql id="0nv9s1"
COMMENT ON PROCEDURE case_app.proc_refresh_case_metrics() IS
'Refreshes case dashboard materialized view and updates refresh state. Called by Kubernetes CronJob every 15 minutes.';
```

Use comments for onboarding and catalog introspection.

---

## 28. Drop Procedure Safely

Dangerous:

```sql id="3sp9ee"
DROP PROCEDURE case_app.proc_refresh_case_metrics() CASCADE;
```

Safer:

```sql id="41xhg4"
DROP PROCEDURE IF EXISTS case_app.proc_refresh_case_metrics() RESTRICT;
```

### Pre-Drop Checklist

```text id="2pthp3"
- search MyBatis XML
- search Java code
- search Kubernetes CronJob
- search GitHub Actions
- search shell scripts/runbooks
- search Camunda worker/process code
- search docs
- check DB grants/dependencies
```

---

## 29. Dependency Search

### PostgreSQL Catalog

```sql id="ul4482"
SELECT
    n.nspname AS schema_name,
    p.proname AS procedure_name,
    pg_get_function_arguments(p.oid) AS arguments,
    pg_get_functiondef(p.oid) AS definition
FROM pg_proc p
JOIN pg_namespace n
    ON n.oid = p.pronamespace
WHERE n.nspname = 'case_app'
  AND p.prokind = 'p';
```

In PostgreSQL catalog, procedures are represented in `pg_proc`; `prokind = 'p'` identifies procedures.

### Repository

```bash id="8li1un"
grep -R "proc_refresh_case_metrics" \
  src/main/java \
  src/main/resources \
  deploy \
  charts \
  .github \
  docs \
  --line-number
```

---

## 30. Flyway Procedure Layout

```text id="uu6wqh"
src/main/resources/db/migration/
├─ versioned/
│  ├─ V001300__case_create_proc_recalculate_sla_v2.sql
│  ├─ V001310__case_grant_proc_recalculate_sla_v2.sql
│  └─ V001340__case_drop_proc_recalculate_sla_v1.sql
│
└─ repeatable/
   ├─ R__080_case_proc_refresh_case_metrics.sql
   ├─ R__081_case_proc_backfill_risk_score_batch.sql
   └─ R__082_case_proc_repair_case_state.sql
```

### Naming Rule

```text id="355zj0"
R__<order>_<domain>_proc_<operation>.sql
```

Procedure names should sound like operations:

```text id="u6o9rh"
proc_refresh_case_metrics
proc_backfill_risk_score_batch
proc_recalculate_case_sla
proc_repair_case_state
```

---

## 31. Liquibase Procedure Layout

```text id="00bqmy"
src/main/resources/db/changelog/800-procedures/
├─ 801-proc-refresh-case-metrics.yaml
├─ 802-proc-backfill-risk-score-batch.yaml
├─ 803-proc-repair-case-state.yaml
└─ sql/
   ├─ proc_refresh_case_metrics.sql
   ├─ proc_backfill_risk_score_batch.sql
   └─ proc_repair_case_state.sql
```

Wrapper:

```yaml id="63lodk"
databaseChangeLog:
  - changeSet:
      id: 801-case-proc-refresh-case-metrics
      author: case-service-team
      runOnChange: true
      labels: procedure,reporting,refresh
      changes:
        - sqlFile:
            path: src/main/resources/db/changelog/800-procedures/sql/proc_refresh_case_metrics.sql
            splitStatements: false
            stripComments: false
      rollback:
        - sql:
            sql: |
              DROP PROCEDURE IF EXISTS case_app.proc_refresh_case_metrics();
```

## 32. Procedure Ordering

Recommended order:

```text id="oacpma"
1. Tables used by procedure
2. Reference data used by procedure
3. Functions called by procedure
4. Materialized views refreshed by procedure
5. Procedure definition
6. Grants
7. Scheduler/CronJob/admin endpoint
8. Tests and runbook
```

Example:

```text id="hqf7js"
V001001__case_tables.sql
R__010_case_fn_calculate_sla.sql
V001100__case_mv_case_metrics.sql
R__080_case_proc_refresh_case_metrics.sql
V001150__case_grant_proc_refresh_case_metrics.sql
```

---

## 33. Procedure Testing Strategy

### Test 1 - Procedure Exists

```sql id="vmp2rq"
SELECT
    n.nspname,
    p.proname,
    pg_get_function_arguments(p.oid)
FROM pg_proc p
JOIN pg_namespace n
    ON n.oid = p.pronamespace
WHERE n.nspname = 'case_app'
  AND p.prokind = 'p'
  AND p.proname = 'proc_refresh_case_metrics';
```

### Test 2 - CALL Works

```sql id="1yzz6v"
CALL case_app.proc_refresh_case_metrics();
```

### Test 3 - Side Effect Verified

```sql id="jskcib"
SELECT *
FROM case_app.read_model_refresh_state
WHERE read_model_name = 'mv_case_dashboard_metrics';
```

### Test 4 - Permission

Run as runtime/refresh role:

```sql id="nf9yjg"
CALL case_app.proc_refresh_case_metrics();
```

Confirm unauthorized role cannot execute sensitive procedure.

### Test 5 - Idempotency

Call twice:

```sql id="6av2xt"
CALL case_app.proc_refresh_case_metrics();
CALL case_app.proc_refresh_case_metrics();
```

Expected: no duplicate or corrupt state.

---

## 34. MyBatis Test Flow

```text id="hje7qw"
1. Start PostgreSQL
2. Run migration
3. Insert seed/test data
4. Call mapper method that invokes procedure
5. Assert side effect
6. Assert operation log / output state
```

Mapper:

```xml id="v9l3hr"
<update id="refreshCaseMetrics">
    CALL case_app.proc_refresh_case_metrics()
</update>
```

Test should catch:

- procedure missing;
- bad grants;
- transaction conflict;
- procedure exception;
- side effect mismatch.

---

## 35. Transaction Test

If procedure contains `COMMIT` or `ROLLBACK`, explicitly test how it is called.

### Top-Level Call Test

```sql id="rhjkxc"
CALL case_app.proc_backfill_with_internal_commits();
```

### Inside Transaction Test

```sql id="yppvh5"
BEGIN;
CALL case_app.proc_backfill_with_internal_commits();
COMMIT;
```

Expected: if internal transaction control is incompatible with context, test should fail in non-prod before production.

### Java Test

Test with application transaction boundary:

```text id="2eedic"
service transaction opens
  -> mapper CALL procedure
  -> verify whether procedure can execute
```

If procedure uses internal commit, do not call from application-managed transaction.

---

## 36. Procedure Observability

Procedure execution should be visible.

Minimum:

```text id="mk4qqw"
- operation_id
- procedure name
- start time
- end time
- status
- affected rows
- error message
- caller/user
```

Table:

```sql id="u6na46"
CREATE TABLE case_app.procedure_execution_log (
    execution_id    UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    procedure_name  TEXT NOT NULL,
    status          TEXT NOT NULL,
    started_at      TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    completed_at    TIMESTAMPTZ,
    affected_rows   BIGINT,
    error_message   TEXT,
    executed_by     TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT current_user
);
```

---

## 37. Procedure Runbook Template

```md id="z0gb2w"
# Procedure Runbook: case_app.proc_refresh_case_metrics()

## Purpose
Refreshes materialized view for case dashboard metrics.

## Caller
Kubernetes CronJob: case-metrics-refresh

## Required Role
case_app_refresh

## Frequency
Every 15 minutes

## Command
CALL case_app.proc_refresh_case_metrics();

## Expected Side Effects
- Refreshes case_app.mv_case_dashboard_metrics
- Updates case_app.read_model_refresh_state

## Success Criteria
- read_model_refresh_state.last_refresh_status = SUCCESS
- last_refresh_completed_at within 15 minutes
- API /case-metrics returns fresh data

## Failure Handling
- Check procedure_execution_log
- Check PostgreSQL error
- Retry once after 5 minutes
- If repeated failure, disable dashboard freshness guarantee and alert ops

## Rollback
No rollback required for refresh-only operation.
If procedure definition is bad, deploy corrected procedure migration.
```

---

## 38. CI/CD Gates for Procedure Changes

```text id="e20gbj"
Pull Request
  ├─ Detect procedure SQL changes
  ├─ Run migration on fresh PostgreSQL
  ├─ Validate procedure exists
  ├─ Validate grants
  ├─ Run CALL tests
  ├─ Verify side effects
  ├─ Test idempotency if retryable
  ├─ Test transaction behavior if COMMIT/ROLLBACK present
  ├─ Detect SECURITY DEFINER
  ├─ Detect dynamic SQL
  ├─ Detect DROP PROCEDURE CASCADE
  └─ Require DB/security/ops reviewer for risky changes
```

### Dangerous Pattern Detector

```bash id="evq6sm"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

ROOT="${1:-src/main/resources/db}"

if grep -RniE "DROP PROCEDURE .*CASCADE" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "DROP PROCEDURE CASCADE detected. Requires explicit dependency review."
  exit 1
fi

if grep -RniE "SECURITY DEFINER" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "SECURITY DEFINER procedure detected. Requires security review."
  exit 1
fi

if grep -RniE "\b(COMMIT|ROLLBACK)\b" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "Transaction control inside procedure detected. Requires transaction behavior review."
  exit 1
fi

if grep -RniE "EXECUTE\s+['\"]|EXECUTE\s+format" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "Dynamic SQL detected. Requires SQL injection review."
  exit 1
fi
```

---

## 39. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Why Dangerous | Better |
|---|---|---|
| Procedure hides business workflow | Hard to version/test with app/Camunda. | Keep workflow visible or heavily governed. |
| Internal `COMMIT` called from app transaction | Transaction conflict. | Let app/job own transaction boundary. |
| `SECURITY DEFINER` without secure search path | Privilege escalation risk. | Set safe search path and review grants. |
| Procedure has no operation log | Failure invisible. | Use execution log/readiness state. |
| Non-idempotent procedure called by retrying job | Duplicate side effects. | Use operation key/unique constraints. |
| Procedure called by public admin endpoint | Security incident risk. | Restrict endpoint and procedure name. |
| Dropping procedure with `CASCADE` | Hidden dependency loss. | Use `RESTRICT` and dependency search. |
| No MyBatis CALL test | Runtime failure. | Integration test against PostgreSQL. |
| Changing procedure signature in place | Caller break. | Create v2 procedure. |
| Using procedure where function/view is enough | Over-operationalizes query logic. | Use function/view for query expressions. |

---

## 40. Practical Example: Refresh Dashboard Metrics

### Materialized View

```sql id="pcqbpx"
CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW case_app.mv_case_dashboard_metrics AS
SELECT
    status,
    priority,
    COUNT(*) AS case_count,
    max(updated_at) AS latest_update_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
GROUP BY status, priority
WITH NO DATA;
```

### Unique Index

```sql id="v45dlk"
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_mv_case_dashboard_metrics
ON case_app.mv_case_dashboard_metrics (status, priority);
```

### Refresh State

```sql id="rgl7fk"
CREATE TABLE case_app.read_model_refresh_state (
    read_model_name TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    last_refresh_started_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    last_refresh_completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    last_refresh_status TEXT NOT NULL,
    row_count BIGINT,
    error_message TEXT,
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

### Procedure

```sql id="hj75z1"
CREATE OR REPLACE PROCEDURE case_app.proc_refresh_case_dashboard_metrics()
LANGUAGE plpgsql
SET lock_timeout = '5s'
SET statement_timeout = '10min'
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_row_count BIGINT;
BEGIN
    INSERT INTO case_app.read_model_refresh_state (
        read_model_name,
        last_refresh_started_at,
        last_refresh_status,
        updated_at
    )
    VALUES (
        'mv_case_dashboard_metrics',
        now(),
        'RUNNING',
        now()
    )
    ON CONFLICT (read_model_name) DO UPDATE
    SET
        last_refresh_started_at = EXCLUDED.last_refresh_started_at,
        last_refresh_status = 'RUNNING',
        error_message = NULL,
        updated_at = now();

    REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY case_app.mv_case_dashboard_metrics;

    SELECT COUNT(*)
    INTO v_row_count
    FROM case_app.mv_case_dashboard_metrics;

    UPDATE case_app.read_model_refresh_state
    SET
        last_refresh_completed_at = now(),
        last_refresh_status = 'SUCCESS',
        row_count = v_row_count,
        updated_at = now()
    WHERE read_model_name = 'mv_case_dashboard_metrics';

EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS THEN
    UPDATE case_app.read_model_refresh_state
    SET
        last_refresh_status = 'FAILED',
        error_message = SQLERRM,
        updated_at = now()
    WHERE read_model_name = 'mv_case_dashboard_metrics';

    RAISE;
END;
$$;
```

### Grant

```sql id="0qmbd9"
REVOKE ALL ON PROCEDURE case_app.proc_refresh_case_dashboard_metrics()
FROM PUBLIC;

GRANT EXECUTE ON PROCEDURE case_app.proc_refresh_case_dashboard_metrics()
TO case_app_refresh;
```

### Call

```sql id="d0a22k"
CALL case_app.proc_refresh_case_dashboard_metrics();
```

---

## 41. Decision Tree

```text id="okowbx"
Do you need a SQL expression in SELECT/view/index?
  ├─ Yes → use function, not procedure
  └─ No
      │
      ├─ Is this a named operation with side effects?
      │   ├─ Yes → procedure candidate
      │   └─ No → maybe plain SQL/job is enough
      │
      ├─ Does it need internal COMMIT/ROLLBACK?
      │   ├─ Yes → procedure, but only top-level CALL/job context
      │   └─ No → procedure or app-managed transaction
      │
      ├─ Will application call it?
      │   ├─ Yes → avoid internal transaction control
      │   └─ No → runbook-controlled procedure possible
      │
      ├─ Is it retryable?
      │   ├─ Yes → make idempotent
      │   └─ No → operation key and manual review
      │
      └─ Is it security-sensitive?
          ├─ Yes → SECURITY DEFINER review + explicit grants
          └─ No → SECURITY INVOKER default
```

---

## 42. Production Review Checklist

| Area | Required |
|---|---|
| Purpose | Procedure has clear operational purpose. |
| Caller | Known caller: job/app/admin/Camunda. |
| Signature | Stable or versioned. |
| Transaction | Internal commit/rollback reviewed. |
| App compatibility | Not called inside incompatible transaction. |
| Idempotency | Safe for retries or operation-keyed. |
| Side effects | Documented and tested. |
| Security | Definer/search path/grants reviewed. |
| Dynamic SQL | Quoted/parameterized and reviewed. |
| Observability | Operation log or refresh state exists. |
| MyBatis | CALL integration test exists. |
| Camunda | Retry and process version compatibility reviewed. |
| Runbook | Exists for manual/operational procedure. |
| Drop | No `CASCADE`; dependency search done. |

---

## 43. Ringkasan Seri 12

| Area | Kesimpulan |
|---|---|
| Procedure | Named operation, not SQL expression. |
| Invocation | Called with `CALL`. |
| Function vs procedure | Function for values/query expressions; procedure for operations/side effects. |
| Migration strategy | Repeatable for body, versioned for signature/contract/security changes. |
| Transaction control | Possible only in allowed contexts; risky with app-managed transactions. |
| `SECURITY DEFINER` | Cannot execute transaction control; requires security review. |
| `SET` clause | Useful for timeout/search path, but disables transaction control inside procedure. |
| MyBatis | Can call procedure, but OUT/INOUT mapping can be complex. |
| Observability | Procedures with side effects should log execution state. |
| Idempotency | Required for retrying jobs/Camunda workers. |
| Materialized view refresh | Excellent use case for procedure. |
| Backfill | Use batch procedure carefully; caller-owned commit often safer. |
| Drop | Avoid `CASCADE`; search dependencies and runbooks. |
| Production readiness | Requires runbook, grants, tests, and transaction behavior validation. |
