# Seri 13 - Trigger & Trigger Function Migration

## 1. Tujuan Seri Ini

Seri ini membahas cara mengelola **PostgreSQL trigger** dan **trigger function** dalam database migration untuk stack:

```text id="v4o9p1"
Jersey / JAX-RS
MyBatis
PostgreSQL
PL/pgSQL
Flyway / Liquibase
Camunda
CI/CD
Kubernetes
```

Trigger adalah mekanisme yang membuat database otomatis menjalankan function saat event tertentu terjadi pada table/view, seperti `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, atau `TRUNCATE`. PostgreSQL mendukung trigger `BEFORE`, `AFTER`, dan `INSTEAD OF`, serta level `FOR EACH ROW` dan `FOR EACH STATEMENT`. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createtrigger.html))

Core rule:

```text id="z0twr7"
Trigger is write-path behavior.
Treat it like production code, not just database plumbing.
```

---

## 2. Mental Model

```text id="v2qscr"
Trigger Migration Mental Model
────────────────────────────────────────────

Table / View Event
  │
  ├─ INSERT
  ├─ UPDATE
  ├─ DELETE
  └─ TRUNCATE
       │
       ▼
Trigger Binding
  │
  ├─ BEFORE / AFTER / INSTEAD OF
  ├─ ROW / STATEMENT
  ├─ WHEN condition
  ├─ UPDATE OF column list
  └─ trigger name ordering
       │
       ▼
Trigger Function
  │
  ├─ NEW / OLD
  ├─ TG_OP
  ├─ TG_WHEN
  ├─ TG_LEVEL
  ├─ TG_ARGV
  └─ side effects
       │
       ▼
Impact
  ├─ data mutation
  ├─ audit log
  ├─ validation
  ├─ derived data
  ├─ MyBatis write behavior
  ├─ Jersey API behavior
  ├─ Camunda worker/process behavior
  └─ backfill/batch behavior
```

PostgreSQL PL/pgSQL trigger functions dibuat dengan `CREATE FUNCTION`, tidak mendeklarasikan argument formal, dan memiliki return type `trigger` untuk data-change trigger; argument dari `CREATE TRIGGER` dibaca lewat `TG_ARGV`. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/plpgsql-trigger.html))

---

## 3. Trigger Function vs Trigger Binding

Ini pemisahan paling penting untuk migration.

```text id="i6bcj8"
Trigger function:
  logic yang akan dijalankan

Trigger binding:
  attachment logic tersebut ke table/view/event tertentu
```

### Trigger Function

```sql id="f0j1fj"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_set_updated_at()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    NEW.updated_at := now();
    RETURN NEW;
END;
$$;
```

### Trigger Binding

```sql id="smily7"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_enforcement_case_set_updated_at
BEFORE UPDATE ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_set_updated_at();
```

### Migration Strategy

| Object | Migration Type | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Trigger function body | Repeatable | Logic bisa di-replace dengan same signature. |
| Trigger binding | Versioned | Structural attachment ke table/event. |
| Drop trigger | Versioned | Mengubah write-path behavior. |
| Rename trigger | Versioned | Ordering/semantics bisa berubah. |
| Change trigger timing | Versioned | Behavior contract berubah. |

---

## 4. Basic `updated_at` Trigger

### Table

```sql id="i3h7uk"
CREATE TABLE case_app.enforcement_case (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    case_number TEXT NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

### Trigger Function

```sql id="8f8e3d"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_set_updated_at()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    NEW.updated_at := now();
    RETURN NEW;
END;
$$;
```

### Trigger

```sql id="npsovi"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_010_enforcement_case_set_updated_at
BEFORE UPDATE ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_set_updated_at();
```

Untuk `BEFORE` row-level trigger, return value menentukan apakah operasi row lanjut dan row seperti apa yang akan disimpan; return `NEW` melanjutkan update/insert dengan nilai baru, sedangkan return `NULL` dapat melewati operasi row tersebut. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/plpgsql-trigger.html))

---

## 5. `BEFORE` vs `AFTER` vs `INSTEAD OF`

| Timing | Dipakai Untuk | Catatan |
|---|---|---|
| `BEFORE` | Mengubah row sebelum disimpan, validasi ringan, set timestamp. | Bisa modify `NEW`; lebih efisien jika tidak perlu final persisted state. |
| `AFTER` | Audit, propagate changes, consistency check setelah row tersimpan. | Melihat final row setelah constraint dan trigger lain. |
| `INSTEAD OF` | Write behavior pada view. | Hanya untuk view dan harus row-level. |

PostgreSQL menyebut `BEFORE` trigger dieksekusi sebelum operasi row dicoba, `AFTER` trigger setelah operasi selesai, dan `INSTEAD OF` trigger berlaku untuk insert/update/delete pada view. Trigger `FOR EACH ROW` dipanggil sekali per row, sedangkan `FOR EACH STATEMENT` dipanggil sekali per statement walaupun statement mengubah banyak row. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createtrigger.html))

### Practical Rule

```text id="lq2ib0"
Need to modify NEW row?
  Use BEFORE ROW.

Need audit after final state is known?
  Use AFTER ROW or AFTER STATEMENT.

Need writable view?
  Use INSTEAD OF ROW trigger, but avoid unless strongly justified.
```

---

## 6. Row-Level vs Statement-Level Trigger

### Row-Level

```sql id="h5fzcp"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_case_audit_row
AFTER UPDATE ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_case_audit_row();
```

Dipanggil untuk setiap row yang berubah.

Cocok untuk:

- set column value;
- per-row audit detail;
- per-row validation;
- per-row derived mutation.

### Statement-Level

```sql id="6izmu9"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_case_audit_statement
AFTER UPDATE ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH STATEMENT
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_case_audit_statement();
```

Dipanggil sekali per statement.

Cocok untuk:

- refresh summary marker;
- notify perubahan global;
- audit bulk operation;
- transition table processing.

### Transition Tables

`AFTER` trigger dapat memakai transition tables untuk melihat kumpulan row yang berubah dalam statement; PostgreSQL membuat nama transition relation lewat `REFERENCING OLD TABLE` atau `NEW TABLE`, dan function dapat memperlakukannya seperti read-only temporary table. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/plpgsql-trigger.html))

---

## 7. Transition Table Example

### Audit Table

```sql id="miiwjj"
CREATE TABLE case_app.case_audit_log (
    id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    case_id UUID NOT NULL,
    operation TEXT NOT NULL,
    old_status TEXT,
    new_status TEXT,
    changed_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    changed_by TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT current_user
);
```

### Statement-Level Trigger Function

```sql id="an1ndr"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_audit_case_status_bulk()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    INSERT INTO case_app.case_audit_log (
        case_id,
        operation,
        old_status,
        new_status
    )
    SELECT
        n.id,
        TG_OP,
        o.status,
        n.status
    FROM old_rows o
    JOIN new_rows n
        ON n.id = o.id
    WHERE o.status IS DISTINCT FROM n.status;

    RETURN NULL;
END;
$$;
```

### Trigger Binding

```sql id="iz5ze6"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_020_enforcement_case_audit_status_bulk
AFTER UPDATE ON case_app.enforcement_case
REFERENCING OLD TABLE AS old_rows NEW TABLE AS new_rows
FOR EACH STATEMENT
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_audit_case_status_bulk();
```

Transition relation hanya tersedia untuk `AFTER` trigger pada plain table, bukan foreign table, dan tidak boleh digunakan dengan constraint trigger; untuk `UPDATE`, trigger tidak boleh memakai column list jika memakai transition relation. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createtrigger.html))

---

## 8. `WHEN` Condition

Trigger bisa punya `WHEN` condition.

```sql id="iu766j"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_030_enforcement_case_audit_status
AFTER UPDATE ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
WHEN (OLD.status IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.status)
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_audit_case_status();
```

PostgreSQL menjelaskan bahwa `WHEN` condition diuji untuk menentukan apakah trigger perlu fired; pada row-level trigger, condition bisa memeriksa `OLD` dan/atau `NEW`. Untuk `AFTER` trigger, condition dievaluasi setelah row update terjadi dan menentukan apakah event perlu di-queue untuk firing di akhir statement; ini dapat mempercepat statement besar jika trigger hanya relevan untuk sebagian row. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/trigger-definition.html))

### Practical Rule

```text id="7flgh2"
Use WHEN for cheap filtering.
Use trigger function body for complex logic.
```

---

## 9. `UPDATE OF column_name`

Trigger bisa dibuat hanya untuk update kolom tertentu.

```sql id="rtlk4t"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_040_enforcement_case_status_changed
AFTER UPDATE OF status ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_audit_case_status();
```

Catatan penting: PostgreSQL menyatakan column-specific trigger akan fired jika kolom tersebut disebut dalam `SET` list, bahkan kalau nilainya tidak benar-benar berubah; sebaliknya, perubahan nilai yang terjadi akibat `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger lain tidak dihitung untuk trigger `UPDATE OF` tersebut. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createtrigger.html))

### Safer

```sql id="c0h4cb"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_040_enforcement_case_status_changed
AFTER UPDATE OF status ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
WHEN (OLD.status IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.status)
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_audit_case_status();
```

---

## 10. Trigger Ordering

Jika beberapa trigger dengan jenis yang sama didefinisikan untuk event yang sama pada relation yang sama, PostgreSQL menjalankannya berdasarkan **alphabetical order by trigger name**. Untuk `BEFORE` dan `INSTEAD OF` trigger, row hasil modifikasi trigger sebelumnya menjadi input untuk trigger berikutnya. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/trigger-definition.html))

### Naming Convention

```text id="bliz1v"
trg_010_<table>_<purpose>
trg_020_<table>_<purpose>
trg_030_<table>_<purpose>
```

Example:

```sql id="bjszz5"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_010_enforcement_case_set_updated_at
BEFORE UPDATE ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_set_updated_at();

CREATE TRIGGER trg_020_enforcement_case_validate_status
BEFORE UPDATE ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_validate_status_transition();

CREATE TRIGGER trg_030_enforcement_case_audit
AFTER UPDATE ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_audit_case_change();
```

### Rule

```text id="sg5bpk"
If order matters, encode order in trigger name.
Do not rely on accidental lexical ordering.
```

---

## 11. Trigger Function Variables

PL/pgSQL menyediakan special variables saat function dipanggil sebagai trigger, termasuk `NEW`, `OLD`, `TG_NAME`, `TG_WHEN`, `TG_LEVEL`, `TG_OP`, `TG_TABLE_NAME`, `TG_TABLE_SCHEMA`, `TG_NARGS`, dan `TG_ARGV`. `NEW` tersedia untuk row-level `INSERT`/`UPDATE`, sedangkan `OLD` tersedia untuk row-level `UPDATE`/`DELETE`. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/plpgsql-trigger.html))

| Variable | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `NEW` | Row baru untuk `INSERT`/`UPDATE`. |
| `OLD` | Row lama untuk `UPDATE`/`DELETE`. |
| `TG_OP` | `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, atau `TRUNCATE`. |
| `TG_WHEN` | `BEFORE`, `AFTER`, atau `INSTEAD OF`. |
| `TG_LEVEL` | `ROW` atau `STATEMENT`. |
| `TG_TABLE_NAME` | Nama table yang memicu trigger. |
| `TG_TABLE_SCHEMA` | Schema table. |
| `TG_ARGV` | Argument trigger dari `CREATE TRIGGER`. |

---

## 12. Audit Trigger Pattern

### Audit Table

```sql id="buu7zd"
CREATE TABLE case_app.case_audit_log (
    id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    case_id UUID NOT NULL,
    operation TEXT NOT NULL,
    old_row JSONB,
    new_row JSONB,
    changed_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    changed_by TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT current_user
);
```

### Trigger Function

```sql id="1x2z20"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_audit_enforcement_case()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    IF TG_OP = 'INSERT' THEN
        INSERT INTO case_app.case_audit_log (
            case_id,
            operation,
            old_row,
            new_row
        )
        VALUES (
            NEW.id,
            TG_OP,
            NULL,
            to_jsonb(NEW)
        );

        RETURN NEW;
    ELSIF TG_OP = 'UPDATE' THEN
        INSERT INTO case_app.case_audit_log (
            case_id,
            operation,
            old_row,
            new_row
        )
        VALUES (
            NEW.id,
            TG_OP,
            to_jsonb(OLD),
            to_jsonb(NEW)
        );

        RETURN NEW;
    ELSIF TG_OP = 'DELETE' THEN
        INSERT INTO case_app.case_audit_log (
            case_id,
            operation,
            old_row,
            new_row
        )
        VALUES (
            OLD.id,
            TG_OP,
            to_jsonb(OLD),
            NULL
        );

        RETURN OLD;
    END IF;

    RAISE EXCEPTION 'Unsupported trigger operation: %', TG_OP;
END;
$$;
```

### Trigger Binding

```sql id="4ihrz3"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_030_enforcement_case_audit
AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_audit_enforcement_case();
```

Untuk `AFTER` row-level trigger, return value diabaikan, tetapi function tetap dapat membatalkan operasi keseluruhan dengan raising error. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/plpgsql-trigger.html))

---

## 13. Validation Trigger Pattern

Trigger validation bisa dipakai untuk invariant yang sulit diekspresikan dengan constraint biasa. Tetapi prefer `CHECK`, FK, UNIQUE, atau exclusion constraint jika bisa.

### Example: Status Transition

```sql id="i2lim5"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_validate_case_status_transition()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    IF OLD.status = 'CLOSED' AND NEW.status <> 'CLOSED' THEN
        RAISE EXCEPTION 'Closed case cannot transition back to %', NEW.status
            USING ERRCODE = '22023';
    END IF;

    RETURN NEW;
END;
$$;
```

```sql id="ha1rii"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_020_enforcement_case_validate_status_transition
BEFORE UPDATE OF status ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
WHEN (OLD.status IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.status)
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_validate_case_status_transition();
```

### Rule

```text id="v8w6xo"
Use trigger validation only when declarative constraint is insufficient.
Declarative constraints are usually easier to reason about and validate.
```

---

## 14. Derived Column Trigger

Example: maintain normalized search key.

```sql id="uyyl5h"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_set_case_search_key()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    NEW.search_key := upper(trim(NEW.case_number));
    RETURN NEW;
END;
$$;
```

```sql id="tgobfm"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_010_enforcement_case_set_search_key
BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF case_number ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_set_case_search_key();
```

### Caution

If you can use generated columns safely, evaluate that first. Trigger-derived data can drift if trigger is disabled during bulk load or if older data predates the trigger.

---

## 15. Generic Trigger Function with `TG_ARGV`

Generic function:

```sql id="x1cg0y"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_touch_timestamp_column()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_column_name TEXT;
BEGIN
    v_column_name := TG_ARGV[0];

    IF v_column_name IS NULL THEN
        RAISE EXCEPTION 'Timestamp column name argument is required';
    END IF;

    IF v_column_name <> 'updated_at' THEN
        RAISE EXCEPTION 'Unsupported timestamp column: %', v_column_name;
    END IF;

    NEW.updated_at := now();
    RETURN NEW;
END;
$$;
```

Binding:

```sql id="krtjle"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_010_enforcement_case_touch_updated_at
BEFORE UPDATE ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_touch_timestamp_column('updated_at');
```

PostgreSQL trigger functions tidak mendeklarasikan formal arguments walaupun trigger mengirim argument; argument tersebut disediakan melalui `TG_ARGV`. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/plpgsql-trigger.html))

### Caution

Generic trigger function bisa mengurangi duplikasi, tetapi bisa membuat logic lebih abstrak dan sulit dipahami. Gunakan jika benar-benar ada pola lintas table yang stabil.

---

## 16. Trigger and MyBatis Impact

Trigger dapat membuat MyBatis write tidak sepenuhnya mencerminkan row yang akhirnya tersimpan.

Mapper:

```xml id="4xuv8l"
<update id="updateCaseStatus">
    UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
    SET status = #{status}
    WHERE id = #{id}
</update>
```

Trigger:

```sql id="5gcllw"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_010_enforcement_case_set_updated_at
BEFORE UPDATE ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_set_updated_at();
```

Aplikasi hanya mengubah `status`, tetapi DB juga mengubah `updated_at`.

### Recommended

Jika API perlu tahu value final, gunakan `RETURNING` di SQL mapper.

```xml id="w63cka"
<update id="updateCaseStatusReturning" statementType="PREPARED">
    UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
    SET status = #{status}
    WHERE id = #{id}
    RETURNING id, status, updated_at
</update>
```

### Rule

```text id="n5za4r"
If trigger mutates data, application should either:
  - not care about mutated fields, or
  - fetch/return final persisted state.
```

---

## 17. Trigger and Jersey API Contract

Jika endpoint mengembalikan data setelah update:

```text id="096loc"
PATCH /cases/{id}/status
```

Jangan mengasumsikan request body sama dengan row final.

Example:

```json id="tzk6pb"
{
  "status": "UNDER_REVIEW"
}
```

DB trigger mungkin mengubah:

```json id="0z8wh1"
{
  "status": "UNDER_REVIEW",
  "updatedAt": "2026-07-13T10:00:00+07:00",
  "lastStatusChangedAt": "2026-07-13T10:00:00+07:00"
}
```

API service sebaiknya membaca ulang row atau memakai `RETURNING`.

---

## 18. Trigger and Backfill

Backfill sering memicu trigger tanpa disadari.

```sql id="z5y32e"
UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
SET risk_score = 0
WHERE risk_score IS NULL;
```

Ini akan memicu `BEFORE UPDATE` dan `AFTER UPDATE` trigger.

### Risiko

| Trigger Type | Backfill Impact |
|---|---|
| `updated_at` trigger | Semua row terlihat “baru diupdate”. |
| Audit trigger | Audit log membengkak. |
| Validation trigger | Backfill bisa gagal karena invariant baru. |
| Notification trigger | Bisa mengirim event palsu. |
| Summary trigger | Bisa membebani derived table. |

### Safe Pattern

```text id="k13ewy"
Before backfill:
  - list triggers on table
  - decide which should fire
  - if disabling trigger, document compensating action
  - run in batches
  - validate audit/derived state after backfill
```

---

## 19. Disable / Enable Trigger

PostgreSQL mendukung `ALTER TABLE ... DISABLE TRIGGER` dan `ENABLE TRIGGER`; disabled trigger tetap dikenal database tetapi tidak dieksekusi saat event terjadi. Bisa disable/enable trigger tertentu, semua trigger, atau hanya user triggers, dengan caveat constraint trigger internal dan privilege. Perintah ini mengambil lock `SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE`. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-altertable.html))

```sql id="0li0ev"
ALTER TABLE case_app.enforcement_case
DISABLE TRIGGER trg_030_enforcement_case_audit;
```

```sql id="ivvzn9"
ALTER TABLE case_app.enforcement_case
ENABLE TRIGGER trg_030_enforcement_case_audit;
```

### Warning

```text id="u9xlhp"
Disabling trigger is a production-risk operation.
It creates a window where normal invariants/side effects may not happen.
```

---

## 20. Disable Trigger Safely During Backfill

### Bad

```sql id="b05rq0"
ALTER TABLE case_app.enforcement_case DISABLE TRIGGER ALL;

UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
SET risk_score = 0
WHERE risk_score IS NULL;

ALTER TABLE case_app.enforcement_case ENABLE TRIGGER ALL;
```

Problems:

- may require high privilege;
- may disable constraint-related triggers depending command/role;
- audit/validation/derived behavior absent;
- if migration fails between disable and enable, trigger remains disabled.

### Safer

```sql id="164h3r"
ALTER TABLE case_app.enforcement_case
DISABLE TRIGGER trg_030_enforcement_case_audit;

-- chunked backfill here

ALTER TABLE case_app.enforcement_case
ENABLE TRIGGER trg_030_enforcement_case_audit;
```

Better still: avoid disabling if possible, or make trigger aware of migration context through explicit controlled mechanism.

---

## 21. Trigger Guard for Backfill

Add session variable guard only if your organization accepts this pattern.

```sql id="58xj9e"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_audit_enforcement_case()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    IF current_setting('case_app.skip_audit_trigger', true) = 'on' THEN
        RETURN COALESCE(NEW, OLD);
    END IF;

    -- audit logic here

    RETURN COALESCE(NEW, OLD);
END;
$$;
```

Backfill session:

```sql id="qf5u55"
SET LOCAL case_app.skip_audit_trigger = 'on';

UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
SET risk_score = 0
WHERE risk_score IS NULL;
```

### Caution

This is powerful but dangerous. It must be protected by:

- migration role only;
- strong code review;
- audit of skipped behavior;
- post-backfill reconciliation.

---

## 22. Trigger Recursion and Cascading

If a trigger function executes SQL commands, those commands can fire triggers again. PostgreSQL calls this cascading triggers, and there is no direct limit on cascade levels; preventing infinite recursion is the trigger programmer’s responsibility. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/trigger-definition.html))

### Bad

```sql id="2qj0ze"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_recursive_update()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
    SET updated_at = now()
    WHERE id = NEW.id;

    RETURN NEW;
END;
$$;
```

If this is an `UPDATE` trigger on the same table, it can recursively fire itself.

### Better

For timestamp update, mutate `NEW` in a `BEFORE` trigger:

```sql id="a0n0sz"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_set_updated_at()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    NEW.updated_at := now();
    RETURN NEW;
END;
$$;
```

---

## 23. Trigger and Foreign Key Cascades

PostgreSQL notes that foreign-key referential actions like `ON UPDATE CASCADE` or `ON DELETE SET NULL` are performed via ordinary SQL operations on the referencing table, and triggers on the affected table will fire for those changes. Trigger code that blocks or modifies these effects can break referential integrity. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/trigger-definition.html))

### Review Question

```text id="6hbmdq"
If this table has FK cascade behavior:
  - do triggers fire on cascaded update/delete?
  - does trigger audit this as system change or user change?
  - can trigger reject cascaded change?
  - will it create duplicate side effects?
```

---

## 24. Partitioned Table Trigger Behavior

PostgreSQL documentation states that creating a row-level trigger on a partitioned table creates identical clone triggers on existing partitions, and partitions attached later get identical triggers as well. If a partition with conflicting trigger name exists, PostgreSQL errors unless `CREATE OR REPLACE TRIGGER` is used. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createtrigger.html))

### Practical Rule

```text id="h13xlv"
For partitioned tables:
  test trigger behavior on parent and partitions.
  include attach/detach partition scenario if relevant.
```

---

## 25. Flyway Structure

```text id="qxvjxh"
src/main/resources/db/migration/
├─ repeatable/
│  ├─ R__050_case_trg_fn_set_updated_at.sql
│  ├─ R__051_case_trg_fn_audit_enforcement_case.sql
│  └─ R__052_case_trg_fn_validate_status_transition.sql
│
└─ versioned/
   ├─ V001400__case_create_updated_at_trigger.sql
   ├─ V001401__case_create_audit_trigger.sql
   ├─ V001402__case_create_status_validation_trigger.sql
   ├─ V001430__case_disable_legacy_audit_trigger.sql
   └─ V001440__case_drop_legacy_audit_trigger.sql
```

### Example Repeatable Trigger Function File

```sql id="ac3aaj"
-- R__050_case_trg_fn_set_updated_at.sql

CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_set_updated_at()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    NEW.updated_at := now();
    RETURN NEW;
END;
$$;
```

### Example Versioned Binding File

```sql id="lslidr"
-- V001400__case_create_updated_at_trigger.sql

CREATE TRIGGER trg_010_enforcement_case_set_updated_at
BEFORE UPDATE ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_set_updated_at();
```

---

## 26. Liquibase Structure

```text id="4x6hmm"
src/main/resources/db/changelog/900-triggers/
├─ 901-trg-fn-set-updated-at.yaml
├─ 902-create-updated-at-trigger.yaml
├─ 903-trg-fn-audit-enforcement-case.yaml
├─ 904-create-audit-trigger.yaml
└─ sql/
   ├─ trg_fn_set_updated_at.sql
   └─ trg_fn_audit_enforcement_case.sql
```

### Trigger Function Wrapper

```yaml id="kqb12t"
databaseChangeLog:
  - changeSet:
      id: 901-case-trg-fn-set-updated-at
      author: case-service-team
      runOnChange: true
      labels: trigger-function,plpgsql
      changes:
        - sqlFile:
            path: src/main/resources/db/changelog/900-triggers/sql/trg_fn_set_updated_at.sql
            splitStatements: false
            stripComments: false
      rollback:
        - sql:
            sql: |
              DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS case_app.trg_fn_set_updated_at();
```

### Trigger Binding Changeset

```yaml id="qb05y5"
databaseChangeLog:
  - changeSet:
      id: 902-case-create-updated-at-trigger
      author: case-service-team
      labels: trigger,binding
      changes:
        - sql:
            sql: |
              CREATE TRIGGER trg_010_enforcement_case_set_updated_at
              BEFORE UPDATE ON case_app.enforcement_case
              FOR EACH ROW
              EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_set_updated_at();
      rollback:
        - sql:
            sql: |
              DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS trg_010_enforcement_case_set_updated_at
              ON case_app.enforcement_case;
```

---

## 27. Trigger Naming Convention

```text id="i50pib"
trg_<order>_<table>_<purpose>
trg_fn_<purpose>
```

Examples:

```text id="8zh33y"
trg_fn_set_updated_at
trg_fn_audit_enforcement_case
trg_fn_validate_case_status_transition

trg_010_enforcement_case_set_updated_at
trg_020_enforcement_case_validate_status_transition
trg_030_enforcement_case_audit
```

### Why order prefix?

Because PostgreSQL fires same-kind triggers for same event alphabetically by trigger name. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createtrigger.html))

---

## 28. Trigger Deployment Ordering

Recommended order:

```text id="npex18"
1. Base table exists
2. Audit/target table exists if needed
3. Trigger function created
4. Trigger binding created
5. Grants if trigger function accesses privileged object
6. Backfill strategy reviewed
7. MyBatis/Jersey tests
8. Monitoring/audit validation
```

For audit trigger:

```text id="oquxha"
case_app.enforcement_case
case_app.case_audit_log
case_app.trg_fn_audit_enforcement_case()
trg_030_enforcement_case_audit
```

---

## 29. Trigger Evolution Pattern

### Body Change

Same trigger function signature:

```sql id="sa8drl"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_audit_enforcement_case()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    -- improved audit body
    RETURN COALESCE(NEW, OLD);
END;
$$;
```

Use repeatable migration.

### Timing Change

From:

```sql id="q4dlbt"
AFTER UPDATE
```

to:

```sql id="c4p6xl"
BEFORE UPDATE
```

This is not just body change. Use versioned migration:

```sql id="56fcz0"
DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS trg_030_enforcement_case_audit
ON case_app.enforcement_case;

CREATE TRIGGER trg_030_enforcement_case_audit
BEFORE UPDATE ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_audit_enforcement_case();
```

But prefer create new trigger name, migrate, then drop old if the behavior is high-risk.

---

## 30. Drop Trigger Safely

```sql id="ywbccv"
DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS trg_030_enforcement_case_audit
ON case_app.enforcement_case;
```

Before dropping:

```text id="s4gh2k"
- confirm no audit/compliance requirement depends on it
- confirm no downstream process reads audit rows
- confirm no app assumes trigger side effect
- check backfill/repair plan
- document release note
```

Avoid dropping silently. Trigger removal changes behavior.

---

## 31. Trigger Security

To create a trigger, PostgreSQL requires `TRIGGER` privilege on the table and `EXECUTE` privilege on the trigger function. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createtrigger.html))

### Practical Implication

Migration role needs:

```text id="uugk08"
TRIGGER privilege on target table
EXECUTE privilege on trigger function
CREATE/ALTER function privilege in schema
```

Runtime role generally does not need to execute trigger function manually; triggers execute as part of table operation. But if trigger function uses `SECURITY DEFINER`, apply security review just like ordinary definer functions.

---

## 32. Trigger Function and `SECURITY DEFINER`

Sometimes audit trigger writes to protected audit schema.

```sql id="st61jj"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION audit.trg_fn_audit_case_change()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
SECURITY DEFINER
SET search_path = audit, case_app, pg_temp
AS $$
BEGIN
    INSERT INTO audit.case_audit_log (...)
    VALUES (...);

    RETURN COALESCE(NEW, OLD);
END;
$$;
```

### Review Required

| Question | Why |
|---|---|
| Why definer needed? | Avoid unnecessary privilege escalation. |
| Is `search_path` safe? | Prevent object hijacking. |
| Are objects schema-qualified? | Reduce ambiguity. |
| Who owns function? | Owner privileges matter. |
| Can runtime role indirectly write audit data? | Intended or not? |
| Is dynamic SQL used? | Injection risk. |

---

## 33. Trigger and Notifications

Trigger can call `pg_notify`, but be careful.

```sql id="tk0sfj"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_notify_case_changed()
RETURNS TRIGGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    PERFORM pg_notify(
        'case_changed',
        json_build_object(
            'caseId', NEW.id,
            'operation', TG_OP
        )::text
    );

    RETURN NEW;
END;
$$;
```

Use only if:

- notification volume is bounded;
- consumer failure does not break transaction semantics;
- payload is small;
- notification is not the only source of truth;
- backfill does not emit massive events accidentally.

---

## 34. Trigger Testing Strategy

### Test 1 - Trigger Exists

```sql id="pqxad6"
SELECT
    trigger_schema,
    event_object_schema,
    event_object_table,
    trigger_name,
    action_timing,
    event_manipulation
FROM information_schema.triggers
WHERE event_object_schema = 'case_app'
  AND event_object_table = 'enforcement_case';
```

### Test 2 - `updated_at` Changes

```sql id="vkbu7n"
INSERT INTO case_app.enforcement_case (
    id,
    case_number,
    status
)
VALUES (
    '00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001',
    'CASE-001',
    'OPEN'
);

UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
SET status = 'UNDER_REVIEW'
WHERE id = '00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001';

SELECT updated_at
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
WHERE id = '00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001';
```

### Test 3 - Audit Row Created

```sql id="hhbb4a"
SELECT *
FROM case_app.case_audit_log
WHERE case_id = '00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001';
```

### Test 4 - Backfill Impact

Run a representative batch update and verify:

```text id="h6eoxx"
- audit volume expected
- updated_at behavior expected
- validation trigger does not block valid backfill
- no recursive trigger issue
```

---

## 35. MyBatis Integration Test

Mapper:

```xml id="p48lx1"
<update id="updateCaseStatus">
    UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
    SET status = #{status}
    WHERE id = #{id}
</update>
```

Test flow:

```text id="cc6gcj"
1. Start PostgreSQL
2. Run migration
3. Insert row
4. Call mapper update
5. Verify updated_at changed
6. Verify audit row inserted
7. Verify API response reads final state
```

This catches:

- trigger missing;
- trigger function error;
- grant/privilege issue;
- mapper unaware of side effect;
- validation trigger rejecting app update;
- audit table missing.

---

## 36. CI/CD Gates for Trigger Changes

```text id="gmvilz"
Pull Request
  ├─ Detect trigger function changes
  ├─ Detect trigger binding changes
  ├─ Run migration on fresh PostgreSQL
  ├─ Validate trigger exists
  ├─ Run insert/update/delete tests
  ├─ Run MyBatis integration tests
  ├─ Run backfill simulation if table is large
  ├─ Detect DISABLE TRIGGER
  ├─ Detect DROP TRIGGER
  ├─ Detect SECURITY DEFINER
  ├─ Detect recursive self-update pattern
  └─ Require DB/domain/security reviewer for risky trigger
```

### Dangerous Pattern Detector

```bash id="la5tb3"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

ROOT="${1:-src/main/resources/db}"

if grep -RniE "DISABLE TRIGGER|ENABLE TRIGGER|DROP TRIGGER" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "Trigger lifecycle change detected. Requires DB review."
  exit 1
fi

if grep -RniE "SECURITY DEFINER" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "SECURITY DEFINER detected. Requires security review."
  exit 1
fi

if grep -RniE "UPDATE .*enforcement_case" "$ROOT" | grep -i "trg_fn"; then
  echo "Possible self-update in trigger function. Review recursion risk."
  exit 1
fi
```

---

## 37. Trigger Review Checklist

| Question | Required Review |
|---|---|
| Is this trigger row-level or statement-level? | Performance and semantics. |
| Is it `BEFORE`, `AFTER`, or `INSTEAD OF`? | Mutation vs audit behavior. |
| Does order matter? | Trigger name must encode order. |
| Does it mutate `NEW`? | App final-state awareness. |
| Does it write audit/derived data? | Side effects and transaction cost. |
| Does it raise exceptions? | App error behavior. |
| Is there recursion risk? | Self-update/cascading trigger. |
| Does it fire during backfill? | Batch impact. |
| Should it fire during FK cascade? | Referential action behavior. |
| Does it use `SECURITY DEFINER`? | Security review. |
| Does it use dynamic SQL? | SQL injection review. |
| Does it emit notifications? | Volume/semantic review. |
| Is it partitioned table? | Parent/partition behavior. |
| Are MyBatis tests updated? | Write path verification. |
| Is there runbook for disabling/enabling? | Operational safety. |

---

## 38. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Why Dangerous | Better |
|---|---|---|
| Putting hidden business workflow in trigger | App/Camunda behavior becomes opaque. | Keep workflow visible or heavily governed. |
| Self-update same table in update trigger | Recursion risk. | Use `BEFORE` trigger and mutate `NEW`. |
| Trigger audit fires on massive backfill accidentally | Audit explosion. | Plan trigger behavior during backfill. |
| `DISABLE TRIGGER ALL` in migration | Integrity/audit gap. | Disable specific trigger only with runbook. |
| No `WHEN` on audit trigger | Fires for no-op updates. | Use `WHEN OLD IS DISTINCT FROM NEW` or column-specific guard. |
| Relying on trigger order accidentally | Alphabetical order can surprise. | Prefix trigger names. |
| `SECURITY DEFINER` without safe search path | Privilege escalation risk. | Secure path and explicit grants. |
| Trigger emits external-like notifications for every row | Event flood during batch. | Statement trigger / outbox pattern. |
| Dropping trigger without consumer search | Side effect disappears silently. | Dependency and behavior review. |
| No MyBatis integration test | Runtime write behavior unknown. | Test final DB state. |

---

## 39. Practical Recommendation for Your Stack

Untuk stack **Jersey/JAX-RS + MyBatis + PostgreSQL**, saya rekomendasikan:

```text id="69wkfg"
1. Use triggers sparingly and intentionally.
2. Prefer constraints for invariants where possible.
3. Use BEFORE ROW trigger for simple row mutation like updated_at.
4. Use AFTER ROW/STATEMENT trigger for audit/derived side effects.
5. Separate trigger function from trigger binding.
6. Put trigger function in repeatable migration.
7. Put trigger binding in versioned migration.
8. Prefix trigger name with order number.
9. Test MyBatis writes against real PostgreSQL.
10. Review backfill behavior before production migration.
11. Avoid DISABLE TRIGGER unless runbook-approved.
12. Avoid SECURITY DEFINER unless necessary and reviewed.
```

---

## 40. Ringkasan Seri 13

| Area | Kesimpulan |
|---|---|
| Trigger | Otomatis menjalankan function saat table/view event terjadi. |
| Trigger function | `CREATE FUNCTION`, no formal args, returns `trigger`; uses `NEW`, `OLD`, `TG_*`. |
| Trigger binding | Structural attachment ke table/view/event. |
| Migration split | Function repeatable, binding versioned. |
| `BEFORE` | Cocok untuk modify `NEW` dan validation sebelum write. |
| `AFTER` | Cocok untuk audit/derived side effects setelah final row. |
| `INSTEAD OF` | Untuk writable view; high governance. |
| Row vs statement | Row per row; statement sekali per statement. |
| Transition tables | Cocok untuk bulk audit/statement-level processing. |
| Ordering | Same-kind triggers fire alphabetically by trigger name. |
| MyBatis | Trigger side effects harus dites dari mapper write path. |
| Backfill | Trigger bisa menyebabkan audit explosion, timestamp drift, validation failure. |
| Disable trigger | Bisa, tapi production-risk; gunakan spesifik dan dengan runbook. |
| Security | `SECURITY DEFINER` dan dynamic SQL harus direview. |
| CI/CD | Deteksi trigger lifecycle changes dan jalankan integration test. |

## Response:
7/13/2026, 7:50:39 PM

Saya lanjut ke **Seri 14 dari 18**: Event Trigger & Database Governance Hooks. Ini area yang lebih sensitif karena event trigger berjalan pada level DDL/database governance, bukan write-path table biasa.

Kita lanjut ke **Seri 14 dari 18**.

# Seri 14 - Event Trigger & Database Governance Hooks

## 1. Tujuan Seri Ini

Seri ini membahas **PostgreSQL Event Trigger**, yaitu trigger yang berjalan pada level **DDL/database event**, bukan pada `INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE` table biasa.

Event trigger cocok untuk kebutuhan governance seperti:

```text id="e9mv38"
- audit perubahan schema
- deteksi DROP object
- deteksi table rewrite
- block destructive DDL tertentu
- enforce schema naming policy
- record GRANT / REVOKE
- migration compliance evidence
```

Tetapi event trigger juga berbahaya karena bisa memengaruhi semua DDL di database. PostgreSQL event trigger saat ini mendukung event `login`, `ddl_command_start`, `ddl_command_end`, `table_rewrite`, dan `sql_drop`. Event trigger berjalan pada database tempat ia dibuat. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/event-trigger-definition.html))

Core rule:

```text id="tlbt52"
Normal trigger controls data changes.
Event trigger controls database structural changes.

So event trigger belongs to database governance,
not application business logic.
```

---

## 2. Mental Model

```text id="2gx98q"
Event Trigger Mental Model
────────────────────────────────────────────

DDL Command
  │
  ├─ CREATE TABLE
  ├─ ALTER TABLE
  ├─ DROP VIEW
  ├─ GRANT / REVOKE
  ├─ REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW
  ├─ COMMENT
  └─ REINDEX
       │
       ▼
Event Trigger
  │
  ├─ ddl_command_start
  ├─ ddl_command_end
  ├─ sql_drop
  ├─ table_rewrite
  └─ login
       │
       ▼
Governance Action
  ├─ audit log
  ├─ block risky DDL
  ├─ detect table rewrite
  ├─ record dropped objects
  ├─ enforce naming rule
  ├─ enforce migration role
  └─ alert/platform review
```

Event trigger harus dilihat sebagai **guard rail** untuk database platform, bukan sebagai mekanisme domain logic.

---

## 3. Event Trigger vs Normal Trigger

| Aspect | Normal Trigger | Event Trigger |
|---|---|---|
| Scope | Table/view event | Database-level event |
| Event | `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `TRUNCATE` | `ddl_command_start`, `ddl_command_end`, `sql_drop`, `table_rewrite`, `login` |
| Function return type | `trigger` | `event_trigger` |
| Typical use | Audit row, validation, timestamp, derived data | DDL audit, block risky DDL, governance |
| Risk | Write-path behavior | Migration/schema governance behavior |
| App impact | MyBatis writes, API state | Migration pipeline, DBA/admin operations |
| Creation privilege | Table trigger privilege model | Only superuser can create event triggers |
| Should app own it? | Sometimes | Usually platform/DB governance owner |

`CREATE EVENT TRIGGER` requires a function declared with no argument and return type `event_trigger`; PostgreSQL notes that only superusers can create event triggers. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createeventtrigger.html))

---

## 4. Supported Events

| Event | Kapan Terjadi | Use Case |
|---|---|---|
| `ddl_command_start` | Tepat sebelum DDL command dijalankan. | Block destructive DDL, enforce policy. |
| `ddl_command_end` | Setelah DDL command selesai, sebelum transaksi commit. | Audit DDL yang berhasil. |
| `sql_drop` | Saat object di-drop, sebelum `ddl_command_end`. | Capture object yang dihapus. |
| `table_rewrite` | Sebelum table rewrite oleh beberapa `ALTER TABLE` / `ALTER TYPE`. | Detect high-risk DDL. |
| `login` | Saat user authenticated login. | Sangat jarang; high-risk access governance. |

PostgreSQL menjelaskan `ddl_command_start` berlaku untuk DDL seperti `CREATE`, `ALTER`, `DROP`, `COMMENT`, `GRANT`, `REVOKE`, `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW`, `REINDEX`, dan beberapa command lain; event ini tidak terjadi untuk shared objects seperti database, roles, tablespaces, parameter privileges, `ALTER SYSTEM`, atau command yang menargetkan event trigger itu sendiri. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/event-trigger-definition.html))

---

## 5. Kapan Event Trigger Cocok?

| Use Case | Cocok? | Catatan |
|---|---:|---|
| Audit semua DDL pada schema aplikasi | Ya | Gunakan `ddl_command_end`. |
| Capture object yang di-drop | Ya | Gunakan `sql_drop`. |
| Detect table rewrite | Ya | Gunakan `table_rewrite`. |
| Block `DROP TABLE` di production | Ya, hati-hati | Gunakan `ddl_command_start` dengan allowlist/role policy. |
| Enforce naming convention | Bisa | Jangan terlalu agresif sampai migration sah ikut gagal. |
| Track `GRANT` / `REVOKE` | Ya | Berguna untuk privilege audit. |
| Business workflow | Tidak | Jangan pakai event trigger untuk domain logic. |
| App request audit | Tidak | Gunakan normal audit table/app logging. |
| High-volume runtime behavior | Tidak | Event trigger bukan runtime event bus. |

---

## 6. Basic Event Trigger Function

```sql id="7mkbgo"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_notice_ddl()
RETURNS event_trigger
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    RAISE NOTICE 'DDL command executed: %', tg_tag;
END;
$$;
```

Binding:

```sql id="k4xdfs"
CREATE EVENT TRIGGER evt_ddl_notice
ON ddl_command_start
EXECUTE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_notice_ddl();
```

`CREATE EVENT TRIGGER` mendukung `ON event`, optional `WHEN TAG IN (...)`, dan `EXECUTE FUNCTION function_name()`. Dalam syntax ini, keyword `FUNCTION` dan `PROCEDURE` dianggap equivalent secara historis, tetapi function yang direferensikan tetap harus function dengan return type `event_trigger`, bukan stored procedure biasa. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createeventtrigger.html))

---

## 7. Recommended Governance Schema

Jangan campur event trigger governance object dengan schema aplikasi domain.

```sql id="pgorx1"
CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS governance;
```

```sql id="8jqii8"
CREATE TABLE governance.ddl_audit_log (
    id                BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    event_name         TEXT NOT NULL,
    command_tag        TEXT,
    object_type        TEXT,
    schema_name        TEXT,
    object_identity    TEXT,
    in_extension       BOOLEAN,
    ddl_user           TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT current_user,
    application_name   TEXT DEFAULT current_setting('application_name', true),
    client_addr        INET DEFAULT inet_client_addr(),
    occurred_at        TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

### Kenapa schema `governance`?

| Reason | Penjelasan |
|---|---|
| Separation of concern | Governance object bukan domain object. |
| Privilege clarity | Tidak semua app role boleh akses. |
| Audit durability | DDL audit table jangan ikut lifecycle service biasa. |
| Review ownership | Biasanya dimiliki DBA/platform/security. |

---

## 8. Audit DDL dengan `ddl_command_end`

`ddl_command_end` terjadi setelah DDL dilakukan tetapi sebelum transaksi commit. Pada event ini, system catalog sudah berubah dan detail command bisa diperoleh lewat `pg_event_trigger_ddl_commands()`. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/event-trigger-definition.html))

### Function

```sql id="tcccbp"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_audit_ddl_command_end()
RETURNS event_trigger
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_cmd RECORD;
BEGIN
    FOR v_cmd IN
        SELECT *
        FROM pg_event_trigger_ddl_commands()
    LOOP
        INSERT INTO governance.ddl_audit_log (
            event_name,
            command_tag,
            object_type,
            schema_name,
            object_identity,
            in_extension
        )
        VALUES (
            'ddl_command_end',
            v_cmd.command_tag,
            v_cmd.object_type,
            v_cmd.schema_name,
            v_cmd.object_identity,
            v_cmd.in_extension
        );
    END LOOP;
END;
$$;
```

### Event Trigger

```sql id="4c61ew"
CREATE EVENT TRIGGER evt_900_audit_ddl_command_end
ON ddl_command_end
EXECUTE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_audit_ddl_command_end();
```

`pg_event_trigger_ddl_commands()` mengembalikan daftar DDL command yang dieksekusi dalam event `ddl_command_end`, termasuk `command_tag`, `object_type`, `schema_name`, `object_identity`, dan flag `in_extension`. Fungsi ini error jika dipanggil di konteks selain `ddl_command_end`. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/functions-event-triggers.html))

---

## 9. Audit DROP dengan `sql_drop`

`sql_drop` terjadi untuk operasi yang menjatuhkan database object; bukan hanya `DROP`, beberapa `ALTER` command juga dapat memicu event ini. PostgreSQL menyediakan `pg_event_trigger_dropped_objects()` untuk melihat objek yang dihapus. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/event-trigger-definition.html))

### Drop Audit Table

```sql id="rmtkgq"
CREATE TABLE governance.ddl_drop_audit_log (
    id                BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    command_tag        TEXT,
    object_type        TEXT,
    schema_name        TEXT,
    object_name        TEXT,
    object_identity    TEXT,
    original           BOOLEAN,
    normal_dependency  BOOLEAN,
    is_temporary       BOOLEAN,
    ddl_user           TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT current_user,
    application_name   TEXT DEFAULT current_setting('application_name', true),
    occurred_at        TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

### Function

```sql id="wp5lel"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_audit_sql_drop()
RETURNS event_trigger
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_obj RECORD;
BEGIN
    FOR v_obj IN
        SELECT *
        FROM pg_event_trigger_dropped_objects()
    LOOP
        INSERT INTO governance.ddl_drop_audit_log (
            command_tag,
            object_type,
            schema_name,
            object_name,
            object_identity,
            original,
            normal_dependency,
            is_temporary
        )
        VALUES (
            tg_tag,
            v_obj.object_type,
            v_obj.schema_name,
            v_obj.object_name,
            v_obj.object_identity,
            v_obj.original,
            v_obj.normal,
            v_obj.is_temporary
        );
    END LOOP;
END;
$$;
```

### Event Trigger

```sql id="yz5rq6"
CREATE EVENT TRIGGER evt_910_audit_sql_drop
ON sql_drop
EXECUTE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_audit_sql_drop();
```

`pg_event_trigger_dropped_objects()` mengembalikan objek yang di-drop dalam konteks `sql_drop`, termasuk `object_type`, `schema_name`, `object_name`, `object_identity`, `original`, `normal`, dan `is_temporary`; objek sudah dihapus dari catalog saat trigger ini berjalan, jadi tidak bisa lagi di-lookup seperti object aktif. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/functions-event-triggers.html))

---

## 10. Detect Table Rewrite dengan `table_rewrite`

Table rewrite adalah salah satu risiko production terbesar karena bisa memakan waktu lama, menghasilkan banyak IO/WAL, dan memerlukan storage tambahan.

`table_rewrite` terjadi sebelum table rewrite oleh beberapa action dari `ALTER TABLE` dan `ALTER TYPE`; PostgreSQL menyediakan `pg_event_trigger_table_rewrite_oid()` untuk OID table dan `pg_event_trigger_table_rewrite_reason()` untuk alasan rewrite. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/event-trigger-definition.html))

### Audit Table

```sql id="05uyjs"
CREATE TABLE governance.table_rewrite_audit_log (
    id                BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    command_tag        TEXT,
    table_oid          OID,
    table_identity     TEXT,
    rewrite_reason     INTEGER,
    ddl_user           TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT current_user,
    application_name   TEXT DEFAULT current_setting('application_name', true),
    occurred_at        TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

### Function

```sql id="40v2sr"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_audit_table_rewrite()
RETURNS event_trigger
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_table_oid OID;
    v_reason INTEGER;
BEGIN
    v_table_oid := pg_event_trigger_table_rewrite_oid();
    v_reason := pg_event_trigger_table_rewrite_reason();

    INSERT INTO governance.table_rewrite_audit_log (
        command_tag,
        table_oid,
        table_identity,
        rewrite_reason
    )
    VALUES (
        tg_tag,
        v_table_oid,
        v_table_oid::regclass::text,
        v_reason
    );
END;
$$;
```

### Event Trigger

```sql id="o2ft50"
CREATE EVENT TRIGGER evt_920_audit_table_rewrite
ON table_rewrite
EXECUTE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_audit_table_rewrite();
```

`pg_event_trigger_table_rewrite_reason()` mengembalikan bitmap reason seperti persistence berubah, default column berubah, column type berubah, atau table access method berubah. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/functions-event-triggers.html))

---

## 11. Blocking Dangerous DDL

Event trigger dapat dipakai untuk menolak DDL tertentu.

Contoh: block `DROP TABLE`, `DROP SCHEMA`, dan `DROP DATABASE` di database aplikasi.

```sql id="gumj2p"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_block_destructive_ddl()
RETURNS event_trigger
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    IF current_user = 'case_app_migration_breakglass' THEN
        RETURN;
    END IF;

    RAISE EXCEPTION
        'DDL command % is blocked for user %. Use reviewed migration/breakglass path.',
        tg_tag,
        current_user
        USING ERRCODE = '42501';
END;
$$;
```

Binding dengan filter `TAG`:

```sql id="p5x12h"
CREATE EVENT TRIGGER evt_100_block_destructive_ddl
ON ddl_command_start
WHEN TAG IN ('DROP TABLE', 'DROP SCHEMA', 'DROP VIEW', 'DROP MATERIALIZED VIEW')
EXECUTE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_block_destructive_ddl();
```

`CREATE EVENT TRIGGER` mendukung `WHEN TAG IN (...)` untuk membatasi event trigger hanya pada command tag tertentu. Saat ini `TAG` adalah satu-satunya `filter_variable` yang didukung. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createeventtrigger.html))

---

## 12. Block vs Audit: Decision

| Policy | Cocok Untuk | Risiko |
|---|---|---|
| Audit only | Default awal, production-safe | Tidak mencegah DDL berbahaya. |
| Warn via `RAISE NOTICE` | Edukasi developer/DBA | Tidak enforce. |
| Block selected DDL | Production protection | Bisa mematahkan migration sah. |
| Block broad DDL | Highly regulated DB | Bisa membuat deployment sulit. |
| Login trigger enforcement | Sangat jarang | Bug bisa mencegah login. |

Practical recommendation:

```text id="wt2cd2"
Start with audit-only.
Observe migration behavior.
Then block only narrow destructive DDL with breakglass path.
```

---

## 13. Event Trigger Ordering

Jika lebih dari satu event trigger didefinisikan untuk event yang sama, PostgreSQL menjalankannya dalam urutan alfabetis berdasarkan nama trigger. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/event-trigger-definition.html))

Gunakan prefix:

```text id="h8n3v9"
evt_100_block_destructive_ddl
evt_900_audit_ddl_command_end
evt_910_audit_sql_drop
evt_920_audit_table_rewrite
```

Rule:

```text id="fnmr4w"
Blocking trigger should run before audit/secondary governance trigger
if policy depends on early rejection.
```

---

## 14. Event Trigger Failure Behavior

Event trigger adalah bagian dari transaksi DDL.

PostgreSQL menjelaskan: jika `ddl_command_start` trigger gagal, command DDL tidak dijalankan; jika `ddl_command_end` trigger gagal, efek DDL rollback karena transaksi abort. Event trigger tidak dieksekusi dalam transaksi yang sudah aborted. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/event-trigger-definition.html))

### Practical Impact

```text id="52c9ev"
Bug in event trigger can break all migrations.
Bug in audit insert can rollback valid DDL.
Bug in login trigger can block user login.
```

Karena itu event trigger function harus:

- kecil;
- deterministic;
- minim dependency;
- tidak memanggil network/external service;
- tidak bergantung pada table yang sering diubah;
- punya emergency disable plan.

---

## 15. Emergency Disable Plan

PostgreSQL notes: event triggers disabled in single-user mode and when `event_triggers` is set to `false`; if event trigger error makes database hard to use, restart with `event_triggers=false` or use single-user mode to disable/drop it. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createeventtrigger.html))

### Runbook Minimal

```md id="rlk0ek"
# Event Trigger Emergency Runbook

## Symptom
DDL migration fails because event trigger raises unexpected error.

## Immediate Checks
- Identify failing event trigger from error message.
- Confirm command tag and current_user.
- Check governance audit table if insert partially attempted.
- Confirm whether production DDL is blocked intentionally.

## Emergency Path
- Prefer ALTER EVENT TRIGGER ... DISABLE if possible.
- If database is unusable because trigger blocks DDL, use platform-approved emergency procedure.
- For PostgreSQL self-managed, event_triggers=false or single-user mode may be required.
- For managed DB, follow provider-specific support/runbook.

## Recovery
- Fix event trigger function.
- Re-enable trigger.
- Re-run failed migration.
- Add regression test.
```

---

## 16. `login` Event Trigger: Almost Always Avoid

`login` event trigger fires when authenticated user logs in. PostgreSQL documentation explicitly warns that bugs in login event trigger can prevent successful login; on standby servers, login triggers also fire and should avoid database writes, and long-running login triggers should be avoided. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/event-trigger-definition.html))

### Recommendation

```text id="com0gc"
Avoid login event triggers for application microservice databases
unless DBA/platform team has a very specific access-control use case.
```

Better alternatives:

- PostgreSQL roles/privileges;
- network/security group controls;
- IAM database authentication;
- proxy-level policy;
- connection pool policy;
- audit logging outside login trigger.

---

## 17. What Event Triggers Do Not Catch

Event trigger coverage has important exceptions.

`ddl_command_start` does not fire for shared object commands like databases, roles, tablespaces, parameter privileges, `ALTER SYSTEM`, or commands targeting event triggers themselves. It also does not check whether affected object exists before firing. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/event-trigger-definition.html))

Implication:

```text id="ywa47d"
Do not assume event trigger is complete database security.
It is governance for supported database-local DDL events.
```

Still use:

- least privilege;
- migration role separation;
- CI/CD policy;
- DB audit logs;
- cloud/on-prem operational controls.

---

## 18. Event Trigger and Flyway/Liquibase

Event trigger will also see DDL from migration tools.

That means:

```text id="488ze2"
Flyway migrate
  → CREATE TABLE
  → ALTER TABLE
  → CREATE INDEX
  → GRANT
  → event trigger fires

Liquibase update
  → CREATE/ALTER/DROP/GRANT
  → event trigger fires
```

### Risk

If event trigger blocks `DROP VIEW`, `ALTER TABLE`, or `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW`, valid migration can fail.

### Recommended Policy

```text id="6akvtj"
1. Migration role must be recognized.
2. Destructive DDL requires reviewed migration label/process.
3. Breakglass role exists but monitored.
4. Event trigger should audit all migration DDL.
5. Blocking trigger should be narrow and predictable.
```

---

## 19. Example: Allow Migration Role, Block Manual DDL

```sql id="798zh3"
CREATE TABLE governance.approved_ddl_roles (
    role_name TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    can_run_destructive_ddl BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false,
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

```sql id="a5pfud"
INSERT INTO governance.approved_ddl_roles (
    role_name,
    can_run_destructive_ddl
)
VALUES
    ('case_app_migration', true),
    ('case_app_migration_breakglass', true)
ON CONFLICT (role_name) DO UPDATE
SET
    can_run_destructive_ddl = EXCLUDED.can_run_destructive_ddl,
    updated_at = now();
```

Blocking function:

```sql id="ozdquk"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_block_manual_destructive_ddl()
RETURNS event_trigger
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_allowed BOOLEAN;
BEGIN
    SELECT can_run_destructive_ddl
    INTO v_allowed
    FROM governance.approved_ddl_roles
    WHERE role_name = current_user;

    IF COALESCE(v_allowed, false) THEN
        RETURN;
    END IF;

    RAISE EXCEPTION
        'Destructive DDL command % is blocked for role %',
        tg_tag,
        current_user
        USING ERRCODE = '42501';
END;
$$;
```

Event trigger:

```sql id="d4eedv"
CREATE EVENT TRIGGER evt_100_block_manual_destructive_ddl
ON ddl_command_start
WHEN TAG IN ('DROP TABLE', 'DROP SCHEMA', 'DROP VIEW', 'DROP MATERIALIZED VIEW')
EXECUTE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_block_manual_destructive_ddl();
```

### Caution

This function depends on `governance.approved_ddl_roles`. Do not drop/alter that table without a governance migration plan.

---

## 20. Event Trigger Function Dependency Risk

Bad governance design:

```text id="pl8co5"
event trigger function depends on application table case_app.config
```

If app migration changes `case_app.config`, DDL audit itself can fail.

Better:

```text id="nwfugg"
event trigger depends only on governance schema
and stable PostgreSQL system functions.
```

### Rule

```text id="ij0v7z"
Event trigger dependencies must be more stable than application schema.
```

---

## 21. Audit Table Design: Avoid Recursion Confusion

Event trigger audits DDL, not DML. Inserting into audit table does not fire event trigger because it is DML, not DDL.

But audit table itself can be affected by DDL. If you alter/drop governance audit table, event trigger may fail or recursively interact with DDL audit logic.

Recommended:

```text id="xcqcpy"
- Create governance schema first.
- Create audit tables before enabling event trigger.
- Do not casually migrate governance tables.
- Keep governance migration separate from app domain migration.
```

---

## 22. Migration Strategy

### Event Trigger Function

Use repeatable only for audit-only logic if organization is comfortable.

```text id="l0ou6b"
R__900_governance_evt_fn_audit_ddl_command_end.sql
```

For blocking/security-sensitive logic, prefer versioned changes with explicit review:

```text id="f9wpo3"
V009001__governance_create_evt_fn_block_destructive_ddl.sql
V009002__governance_create_event_trigger_block_destructive_ddl.sql
```

### Event Trigger Binding

Always versioned.

```text id="3x3emp"
V009010__governance_create_event_trigger_audit_ddl.sql
V009020__governance_disable_event_trigger_legacy_policy.sql
V009030__governance_drop_event_trigger_legacy_policy.sql
```

Because binding changes database-wide behavior.

---

## 23. Flyway Structure

```text id="w9xmy6"
src/main/resources/db/migration/
├─ versioned/
│  ├─ V009000__governance_create_schema.sql
│  ├─ V009001__governance_create_ddl_audit_tables.sql
│  ├─ V009002__governance_create_event_trigger_audit_ddl.sql
│  ├─ V009003__governance_create_event_trigger_audit_drop.sql
│  ├─ V009004__governance_create_event_trigger_audit_table_rewrite.sql
│  └─ V009010__governance_create_event_trigger_block_destructive_ddl.sql
│
└─ repeatable/
   ├─ R__900_governance_evt_fn_audit_ddl_command_end.sql
   ├─ R__910_governance_evt_fn_audit_sql_drop.sql
   ├─ R__920_governance_evt_fn_audit_table_rewrite.sql
   └─ R__930_governance_evt_fn_block_destructive_ddl.sql
```

### Ordering

```text id="km8b9o"
1. governance schema
2. audit tables
3. event trigger functions
4. event trigger bindings
5. tests
6. production enablement
```

---

## 24. Liquibase Structure

```text id="4zlwve"
src/main/resources/db/changelog/990-governance/
├─ 991-create-governance-schema.yaml
├─ 992-create-ddl-audit-tables.yaml
├─ 993-event-trigger-functions.yaml
├─ 994-create-ddl-audit-event-trigger.yaml
├─ 995-create-drop-audit-event-trigger.yaml
├─ 996-create-table-rewrite-event-trigger.yaml
├─ 997-create-destructive-ddl-block-trigger.yaml
└─ sql/
   ├─ evt_fn_audit_ddl_command_end.sql
   ├─ evt_fn_audit_sql_drop.sql
   ├─ evt_fn_audit_table_rewrite.sql
   └─ evt_fn_block_destructive_ddl.sql
```

### Wrapper Example

```yaml id="8rg57f"
databaseChangeLog:
  - changeSet:
      id: 993-governance-event-trigger-functions
      author: platform-db-team
      labels: governance,event-trigger
      changes:
        - sqlFile:
            path: src/main/resources/db/changelog/990-governance/sql/evt_fn_audit_ddl_command_end.sql
            splitStatements: false
            stripComments: false
        - sqlFile:
            path: src/main/resources/db/changelog/990-governance/sql/evt_fn_audit_sql_drop.sql
            splitStatements: false
            stripComments: false
```

Binding changeset:

```yaml id="k197kb"
databaseChangeLog:
  - changeSet:
      id: 994-governance-create-ddl-audit-event-trigger
      author: platform-db-team
      labels: governance,event-trigger,audit
      changes:
        - sql:
            sql: |
              CREATE EVENT TRIGGER evt_900_audit_ddl_command_end
              ON ddl_command_end
              EXECUTE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_audit_ddl_command_end();
      rollback:
        - sql:
            sql: |
              DROP EVENT TRIGGER IF EXISTS evt_900_audit_ddl_command_end;
```

---

## 25. Enable / Disable Event Trigger

```sql id="alksjs"
ALTER EVENT TRIGGER evt_900_audit_ddl_command_end DISABLE;
```

```sql id="1pp3sc"
ALTER EVENT TRIGGER evt_900_audit_ddl_command_end ENABLE;
```

Use case:

- emergency disable;
- staged rollout;
- test-specific disable;
- migration of governance schema.

### Rule

```text id="aw83xa"
Disabling event trigger must itself be audited and approved.
```

---

## 26. Drop Event Trigger Safely

```sql id="6ast8w"
DROP EVENT TRIGGER IF EXISTS evt_900_audit_ddl_command_end;
```

Before dropping:

```text id="fc7i0k"
- identify governance owner
- confirm replacement policy
- check compliance requirement
- ensure audit continuity
- document reason
- ensure rollback/recreate script exists
```

Dropping event trigger removes a governance guard. Treat it like disabling a security control.

---

## 27. Event Trigger and Superuser Reality

PostgreSQL requires superuser to create event triggers. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-createeventtrigger.html))

Practical implications:

```text id="z0jbag"
- application migration role may not be able to create event triggers
- managed database environments may restrict superuser-like privileges
- governance trigger deployment may require DBA/platform pipeline
- app team should not assume they can own this lifecycle independently
```

For on-prem/AWS/Azure, validate actual privilege model with your DBA/platform team.

---

## 28. Event Trigger and Kubernetes/CI/CD

Event trigger should be deployed through controlled database release path, not application pod startup.

Recommended:

```text id="yd6l7e"
Platform DB Migration Pipeline
  ├─ Create governance schema/tables
  ├─ Deploy audit-only event triggers
  ├─ Observe behavior in staging
  ├─ Enable narrow blocking policy
  ├─ Document breakglass
  └─ Monitor audit logs
```

Avoid:

```text id="4djdqe"
Jersey app pod starts
  → tries to create event trigger
  → requires superuser
  → fails or creates governance drift
```

---

## 29. Event Trigger and MyBatis/Jersey

Normally, MyBatis and Jersey should not trigger event triggers because they should not run DDL at runtime.

But event triggers matter indirectly:

| Area | Impact |
|---|---|
| Flyway/Liquibase migration | DDL audit/blocking can affect deploy. |
| Test setup | Schema creation can be audited/blocked. |
| Integration tests | Need governance triggers disabled or expected. |
| Admin endpoint | Must not run arbitrary DDL. |
| MyBatis migration scripts | If any DDL exists, event trigger fires. |

### Rule

```text id="81kthm"
Runtime app should not execute DDL.
If it does, event trigger may expose a design smell.
```

---

## 30. Event Trigger and Camunda

Camunda-related DB operations can include schema creation/update depending on engine/deployment model.

If using event triggers that block DDL:

```text id="6rc9tu"
- ensure Camunda schema migration path is understood
- ensure migration role allowed if Camunda tables are managed by deployment
- avoid blocking legitimate Camunda migration DDL
- audit Camunda-related DDL separately if needed
```

Do not assume Camunda engine startup should be allowed to mutate schema in production. In mature environments, schema changes should go through explicit migration pipeline.

---

## 31. Staged Rollout Pattern

### Stage 1 - Audit Only

```text id="i51pix"
Enable:
  ddl_command_end audit
  sql_drop audit
  table_rewrite audit

No blocking yet.
```

Goal:

- learn actual DDL patterns;
- detect migration tools’ command tags;
- detect extensions/ownership/grant operations;
- avoid breaking valid deploys.

### Stage 2 - Alert on Risky DDL

```text id="ks9n2f"
Audit DROP / table rewrite
Alert platform team
Do not block yet
```

### Stage 3 - Block Narrow Destructive DDL

```text id="o5699v"
Block:
  DROP TABLE
  DROP SCHEMA
  DROP MATERIALIZED VIEW

Allow:
  migration role
  breakglass role
```

### Stage 4 - Policy Hardening

```text id="85jbwq"
Add:
  naming policy
  schema ownership checks
  unauthorized GRANT/REVOKE detection
```

---

## 32. Policy: Block Table Rewrite in Production?

You can block table rewrite:

```sql id="cb1pwy"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_block_table_rewrite()
RETURNS event_trigger
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
BEGIN
    IF current_setting('governance.allow_table_rewrite', true) = 'on' THEN
        RETURN;
    END IF;

    RAISE EXCEPTION
        'Table rewrite is blocked for command %. Table: %, reason: %',
        tg_tag,
        pg_event_trigger_table_rewrite_oid()::regclass::text,
        pg_event_trigger_table_rewrite_reason()
        USING ERRCODE = '42501';
END;
$$;
```

```sql id="v7bz30"
CREATE EVENT TRIGGER evt_110_block_table_rewrite
ON table_rewrite
EXECUTE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_block_table_rewrite();
```

### Caution

This can block legitimate migrations like type changes. Use only after testing and with breakglass mechanism.

---

## 33. Naming Policy Example

Block table creation outside allowed schemas.

```sql id="2ernx1"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_enforce_schema_policy()
RETURNS event_trigger
LANGUAGE plpgsql
AS $$
DECLARE
    v_cmd RECORD;
BEGIN
    FOR v_cmd IN
        SELECT *
        FROM pg_event_trigger_ddl_commands()
    LOOP
        IF v_cmd.command_tag = 'CREATE TABLE'
           AND v_cmd.schema_name NOT IN ('case_app', 'governance')
        THEN
            RAISE EXCEPTION
                'CREATE TABLE is not allowed in schema %. Object: %',
                v_cmd.schema_name,
                v_cmd.object_identity
                USING ERRCODE = '42501';
        END IF;
    END LOOP;
END;
$$;
```

```sql id="d8maaq"
CREATE EVENT TRIGGER evt_120_enforce_schema_policy
ON ddl_command_end
WHEN TAG IN ('CREATE TABLE')
EXECUTE FUNCTION governance.evt_fn_enforce_schema_policy();
```

### Warning

This fires after DDL actions have occurred but before transaction commits. If it raises exception, DDL is rolled back. That is useful, but failure messages must be clear. PostgreSQL states `ddl_command_end` fires after actions take place but before commit, and a failing `ddl_command_end` trigger rolls back the DDL transaction. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/event-trigger-definition.html))

---

## 34. Audit Query Examples

### Recent DDL

```sql id="5mca57"
SELECT
    occurred_at,
    ddl_user,
    application_name,
    command_tag,
    object_type,
    schema_name,
    object_identity
FROM governance.ddl_audit_log
ORDER BY occurred_at DESC
LIMIT 50;
```

### Recent Drops

```sql id="8pbwgs"
SELECT
    occurred_at,
    ddl_user,
    command_tag,
    object_type,
    schema_name,
    object_name,
    object_identity,
    original,
    normal_dependency
FROM governance.ddl_drop_audit_log
ORDER BY occurred_at DESC
LIMIT 50;
```

### Table Rewrites

```sql id="280j2i"
SELECT
    occurred_at,
    ddl_user,
    command_tag,
    table_identity,
    rewrite_reason
FROM governance.table_rewrite_audit_log
ORDER BY occurred_at DESC;
```

---

## 35. Observability and Alerting

Recommended alerts:

| Alert | Why |
|---|---|
| `DROP TABLE` / `DROP SCHEMA` observed | Destructive DDL. |
| Table rewrite observed in production | Availability risk. |
| DDL by non-migration role | Manual drift risk. |
| DDL outside deployment window | Change management issue. |
| Event trigger disabled | Governance control removed. |
| Event trigger function error | Migration risk. |
| GRANT/REVOKE on app schema | Privilege drift. |

### Metadata to Capture

```text id="0dppzr"
- current_user
- application_name
- client_addr
- command_tag
- object_identity
- schema_name
- event name
- timestamp
- migration tool context if available
```

For Flyway/Liquibase, set `application_name` in JDBC URL if possible to make audit logs easier to interpret.

---

## 36. Testing Strategy

### Test 1 - Audit `CREATE TABLE`

```sql id="q7iq4q"
CREATE TABLE case_app.event_trigger_test_table (
    id BIGINT PRIMARY KEY
);
```

Then:

```sql id="2k5c0x"
SELECT *
FROM governance.ddl_audit_log
WHERE object_identity ILIKE '%event_trigger_test_table%'
ORDER BY occurred_at DESC;
```

### Test 2 - Audit Drop

```sql id="mda55i"
DROP TABLE case_app.event_trigger_test_table;
```

Then:

```sql id="q2v75c"
SELECT *
FROM governance.ddl_drop_audit_log
WHERE object_identity ILIKE '%event_trigger_test_table%'
ORDER BY occurred_at DESC;
```

### Test 3 - Blocking

```sql id="n2c41p"
DROP TABLE case_app.some_protected_table;
```

Expected:

```text id="4lw2mm"
ERROR: destructive DDL is blocked
```

### Test 4 - Flyway/Liquibase Compatibility

Run full migration suite in ephemeral PostgreSQL with event triggers enabled.

Expected:

```text id="5sm0qz"
- valid migrations pass
- audit rows are created
- destructive migrations fail only when policy says so
```

---

## 37. CI/CD Gates

```text id="o4ilt4"
Pull Request
  ├─ Detect event trigger changes
  ├─ Require DBA/platform/security reviewer
  ├─ Run migration in fresh PostgreSQL
  ├─ Test audit create/alter/drop
  ├─ Test blocking rules
  ├─ Test Flyway/Liquibase migration compatibility
  ├─ Test emergency disable script in non-prod
  ├─ Detect broad DDL blocking
  ├─ Detect login event trigger
  └─ Publish governance policy summary
```

### Dangerous Pattern Detector

```bash id="y4pzel"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

ROOT="${1:-src/main/resources/db}"

if grep -RniE "CREATE EVENT TRIGGER .* ON login|ON login" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "login event trigger detected. Requires platform architecture approval."
  exit 1
fi

if grep -RniE "CREATE EVENT TRIGGER .*ddl_command_start" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "ddl_command_start blocking/audit trigger detected. Requires migration compatibility test."
  exit 1
fi

if grep -RniE "DROP EVENT TRIGGER|ALTER EVENT TRIGGER .* DISABLE" "$ROOT"; then
  echo "Event trigger disable/drop detected. Requires governance approval."
  exit 1
fi
```

---

## 38. Production Review Checklist

| Question | Required Answer |
|---|---|
| What event does it hook? | `ddl_command_end`, `sql_drop`, etc. |
| Is it audit-only or blocking? | Blocking requires stronger approval. |
| Who owns it? | DBA/platform/security owner. |
| Does it require superuser? | Yes; deployment path must support it. |
| What tables/functions does it depend on? | Must be stable governance objects. |
| Can it break Flyway/Liquibase? | Must be tested. |
| What command tags are filtered? | Avoid broad accidental block. |
| Does it handle extensions? | Check `in_extension` if needed. |
| Does it block table rewrite? | Requires breakglass. |
| Does it use `login` event? | Avoid unless explicitly approved. |
| Is emergency disable documented? | Mandatory. |
| Is audit data monitored? | Required for governance value. |
| Does it run in staging first? | Mandatory. |
| Is there a rollback plan? | Drop/disable/recreate script. |

---

## 39. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Why Dangerous | Better |
|---|---|---|
| Blocking all DDL broadly | Breaks valid migrations. | Start audit-only, block narrow tags. |
| Event trigger depends on app tables | App schema migration can break governance. | Use stable `governance` schema. |
| No emergency disable runbook | Bad trigger can block DDL. | Document disable path. |
| Login event trigger for app DB | Bug can block login. | Use roles/IAM/network controls. |
| No Flyway/Liquibase compatibility test | Deploy can fail unexpectedly. | Run full migration with trigger enabled. |
| Drop event trigger casually | Removes governance control. | Require approval and replacement plan. |
| No audit table retention policy | Logs grow forever. | Define retention/archive. |
| Treat event trigger as security replacement | Incomplete coverage. | Combine with privileges and CI/CD. |
| Block table rewrite without breakglass | Legitimate migration impossible. | Alert first, then controlled block. |
| Event trigger function too complex | Hard to reason/debug. | Keep small and deterministic. |

---

## 40. Practical Recommendation untuk Stack Ini

Untuk environment **Jersey/JAX-RS + MyBatis + PostgreSQL + Flyway/Liquibase**, saya rekomendasikan:

```text id="turklu"
1. Do not let application service own event trigger lifecycle.
2. Put event trigger under DBA/platform governance.
3. Start with audit-only:
   - ddl_command_end
   - sql_drop
   - table_rewrite
4. Avoid login event trigger.
5. Do not block DDL until migration behavior is observed in staging.
6. Use narrow TAG filters for blocking.
7. Recognize migration role and breakglass role.
8. Keep governance schema separate from app schema.
9. Test full Flyway/Liquibase migration with triggers enabled.
10. Maintain emergency disable runbook.
```

---

## 41. Minimal Governance Setup

Minimum production-safe starting point:

```text id="fvx6uj"
governance schema
  ├─ ddl_audit_log
  ├─ ddl_drop_audit_log
  ├─ table_rewrite_audit_log
  ├─ evt_fn_audit_ddl_command_end()
  ├─ evt_fn_audit_sql_drop()
  ├─ evt_fn_audit_table_rewrite()
  ├─ evt_900_audit_ddl_command_end
  ├─ evt_910_audit_sql_drop
  └─ evt_920_audit_table_rewrite
```

Avoid initially:

```text id="cdyke4"
- blocking all DDL
- login event trigger
- dependency on app schema
- external calls
- complex policy engine inside PL/pgSQL
```

---

## 42. Ringkasan Seri 14

| Area | Kesimpulan |
|---|---|
| Event trigger | Trigger untuk event database-level, terutama DDL governance. |
| Supported events | `login`, `ddl_command_start`, `ddl_command_end`, `table_rewrite`, `sql_drop`. |
| Function return | Harus return `event_trigger`, no arguments. |
| Privilege | Hanya superuser dapat create event trigger. |
| `ddl_command_start` | Cocok untuk blocking sebelum DDL dijalankan. |
| `ddl_command_end` | Cocok untuk audit DDL yang berhasil sebelum commit. |
| `sql_drop` | Cocok untuk capture object yang dihapus. |
| `table_rewrite` | Cocok untuk mendeteksi high-risk table rewrite. |
| `login` | High-risk; biasanya hindari. |
| Migration strategy | Function bisa repeatable terbatas; binding event trigger harus versioned. |
| Flyway/Liquibase | Event trigger akan melihat dan bisa memblokir migration DDL. |
| Governance schema | Pisahkan dari app schema. |
| Blocking policy | Mulai audit-only, lalu block narrow destructive DDL. |
| Emergency | Harus ada disable/breakglass runbook. |
| Testing | Full migration compatibility test wajib. |
