# Seri 17 - Application Compatibility with Jersey/JAX-RS + MyBatis + Camunda

## 1. Tujuan Seri Ini

Seri ini membahas bagaimana memastikan perubahan database migration tetap kompatibel dengan aplikasi selama:

```text id="77q552"
rolling deployment
blue/green deployment
canary deployment
Camunda process masih berjalan
MyBatis mapper masih memakai SQL lama
Jersey API masih melayani client lama
```

Core rule:

```text id="3n4v1f"
Database migration is only safe if application compatibility is safe.
```

Di Kubernetes, Deployment rolling update bisa menjalankan old pod dan new pod secara bersamaan selama proses rollout, dengan konfigurasi seperti `maxUnavailable` dan `maxSurge`. Artinya, schema database harus kompatibel dengan kedua versi aplikasi selama transisi. ([Kubernetes](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/controllers/deployment/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 2. Mental Model

```text id="mn7n9q"
Application Compatibility Mental Model
────────────────────────────────────────────

Database Schema
  │
  ├─ table
  ├─ column
  ├─ constraint
  ├─ view
  ├─ materialized view
  ├─ function
  ├─ procedure
  ├─ trigger
  └─ seed/reference data
       │
       ▼
Application Contracts
  │
  ├─ MyBatis SQL mapper
  ├─ Java DTO/entity
  ├─ Jersey resource/API response
  ├─ transaction boundary
  ├─ Camunda process variable
  ├─ worker query
  ├─ authorization rule
  └─ external client contract
```

Kalau salah satu layer berubah tanpa compatibility plan, migration bisa valid secara SQL tetapi gagal secara aplikasi.

---

## 3. Compatibility Invariant

```text id="gdgmyp"
At every point during deployment:

old app version must work
new app version must work
database must support mixed old/new data
Camunda running instances must remain executable
API clients must not be surprised by breaking response/request changes
```

Ini berarti kita tidak hanya mengetes “migration sukses”, tetapi juga:

```text id="rxch29"
old app + new schema
new app + old-compatible data
new app + partially backfilled data
workflow old version + new schema
```

---

## 4. Why MyBatis Is a Hard Contract

MyBatis sengaja membuat SQL eksplisit. Dokumentasi MyBatis menyebut `resultMap` sebagai elemen yang sangat penting dan powerful untuk memetakan `ResultSet` ke Java object. Ini berarti nama kolom, alias, tipe data, dan struktur query adalah kontrak nyata antara DB dan Java. ([MyBatis](https://mybatis.org/mybatis-3/sqlmap-xml.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Contoh mapper:

```xml id="xmsb3t"
<select id="findCaseSummary" resultMap="CaseSummaryResultMap">
    SELECT
        id,
        case_number,
        status,
        priority,
        created_at,
        updated_at
    FROM case_app.v_case_summary
    WHERE id = #{id}
</select>
```

Kalau migration menghapus `case_number` dari view/table, mapper lama gagal.

Core rule:

```text id="saitr9"
Every MyBatis SQL statement is a schema contract.
```

---

## 5. Why Jersey/JAX-RS Is Also a Contract

Jakarta RESTful Web Services / Jakarta REST menyediakan API untuk membangun REST-style web services. Pada sisi request/response, entity provider seperti `MessageBodyReader` dan `MessageBodyWriter` memetakan HTTP body ke Java type dan sebaliknya. ([Jakarta EE](https://jakarta.ee/learn/docs/jakartaee-tutorial/current/websvcs/rest/rest.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Artinya perubahan DB sering naik menjadi perubahan API:

```text id="4rs8va"
DB column renamed
  → Java DTO field renamed
  → JSON property renamed
  → API client breaks
```

Contoh:

```json id="vvvtg8"
{
  "caseNumber": "CASE-001"
}
```

Jika tiba-tiba berubah menjadi:

```json id="s7wbmr"
{
  "referenceNumber": "CASE-001"
}
```

itu breaking change untuk client lama.

---

## 6. Compatibility Matrix

| DB Change | MyBatis Risk | Jersey/API Risk | Camunda Risk | Safe Strategy |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Add nullable column | Low | Low if not exposed | Low | Expand only. |
| Add required column | Medium | Medium | Medium | Nullable → app writes → backfill → constraint. |
| Rename column | High | High | Medium/high | Add new column, dual-read/write, contract later. |
| Drop column | High | High | High if process uses it | Contract after dependency zero. |
| Change column type | High | Medium/high | Medium | New column, backfill, switch. |
| Add constraint | Medium | Medium | Medium | Validate data first; staged constraint. |
| Change status/reference code | High | High | High | Add new code, support both, migrate, deactivate old. |
| Change view output | High | Medium | Medium | Additive or v2 view. |
| Change function return/signature | High | Medium | Medium | v2 function. |
| Change trigger behavior | Medium/high | Medium/high | Medium/high | Treat as business behavior change. |
| Replace materialized view | Medium/high | Medium/high | Medium | v2 matview + readiness/freshness. |

---

## 7. Old App / New Schema Test

Sebelum production, uji skenario ini:

```text id="65zt2x"
Given:
  old application artifact
  migrated database schema

Then:
  old app can still start
  old MyBatis mapper queries still pass
  old API behavior still works
```

Ini penting untuk rolling deployment. Jika migration dilakukan sebelum deploy app baru, old pod masih bisa hidup sebentar di schema baru.

### Test Example

```text id="8my62l"
1. Start PostgreSQL
2. Run latest DB migration
3. Start previous app version against migrated DB
4. Run smoke tests
5. Confirm no mapper failure
```

Kalau test ini gagal, migration tidak backward-compatible.

---

## 8. New App / Old-Compatible Data Test

Setelah app baru naik, database mungkin belum fully backfilled.

```text id="lw3wcs"
new app
  + partially populated new column
  + old rows with NULL
```

Aplikasi baru harus bisa menangani mixed state.

Contoh:

```java id="93m1am"
Integer riskScore = caseEntity.getRiskScore();

if (riskScore == null) {
    riskScore = riskScoreCalculator.calculate(caseEntity);
}
```

Atau query fallback:

```sql id="4u8hd4"
SELECT
    id,
    COALESCE(risk_score, 0) AS risk_score
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
WHERE id = #{id};
```

### Rule

```text id="p1d7ub"
Before backfill is complete,
new app must tolerate old data shape.
```

---

## 9. Expand / Contract for Application Code

```text id="3i6inv"
Release 1 - DB Expand
  Add new nullable column / new table / new view v2

Release 2 - App Dual-Compatible
  App reads old + new
  App writes both if needed

Release 3 - Backfill
  Fill historical data

Release 4 - App Switch
  App reads new field primarily

Release 5 - Contract
  Remove old column/view/function only after usage is zero
```

Ini adalah pattern utama untuk menjaga compatibility di MyBatis/Jersey/Camunda.

---

## 10. Pattern: Rename Column Safely

Requirement:

```text id="yhhiax"
case_number → reference_number
```

### Release 1 - Expand

```sql id="79ud6o"
ALTER TABLE case_app.enforcement_case
ADD COLUMN reference_number TEXT;
```

### Release 2 - Dual Write

```xml id="icy45m"
<insert id="insertCase">
    INSERT INTO case_app.enforcement_case (
        id,
        case_number,
        reference_number,
        status
    )
    VALUES (
        #{id},
        #{referenceNumber},
        #{referenceNumber},
        #{status}
    )
</insert>
```

### Release 3 - Backfill

```sql id="7c4ydp"
UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
SET reference_number = case_number
WHERE reference_number IS NULL;
```

### Release 4 - Dual Read / Switch

```xml id="074wmd"
<select id="findCase" resultMap="CaseResultMap">
    SELECT
        id,
        COALESCE(reference_number, case_number) AS reference_number,
        status
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case
    WHERE id = #{id}
</select>
```

### Release 5 - Contract

```sql id="i4t2k3"
ALTER TABLE case_app.enforcement_case
DROP COLUMN case_number;
```

Drop hanya setelah semua mapper lama, report, worker, dan BPMN/DMN reference sudah bersih.

---

## 11. Pattern: API Field Rename

Bad:

```json id="eazffy"
{
  "referenceNumber": "CASE-001"
}
```

langsung menggantikan:

```json id="2fbzq9"
{
  "caseNumber": "CASE-001"
}
```

Safe transitional response:

```json id="y66s3w"
{
  "caseNumber": "CASE-001",
  "referenceNumber": "CASE-001"
}
```

Service mapping:

```java id="os1dc6"
public CaseResponse toResponse(CaseEntity entity) {
    String referenceNumber = firstNonNull(
        entity.getReferenceNumber(),
        entity.getCaseNumber()
    );

    return new CaseResponse(
        entity.getCaseNumber(),
        referenceNumber,
        entity.getStatus()
    );
}
```

### Rule

```text id="f77s0e"
DB rename does not require immediate API rename.
Preserve API contract while DB evolves.
```

---

## 12. Pattern: Add Required API Field from New DB Column

Requirement:

```json id="sa7qlu"
{
  "riskScore": 80
}
```

But old rows do not have `risk_score`.

Safe progression:

```text id="1xuwka"
1. Add nullable risk_score
2. App writes risk_score for new rows
3. API exposes riskScore as nullable or with readiness metadata
4. Backfill old rows
5. Add DB constraint
6. Make API field required in later API version/contract
```

Transitional API:

```json id="40fwmt"
{
  "caseId": "123",
  "riskScore": null,
  "riskScoreReady": false
}
```

After backfill:

```json id="si81b5"
{
  "caseId": "123",
  "riskScore": 80,
  "riskScoreReady": true
}
```

---

## 13. Pattern: Reference Data / Status Compatibility

Status code is dangerous because it appears everywhere.

```text id="5gwheg"
DB:
  enforcement_case.status

MyBatis:
  WHERE status IN (...)

Jersey API:
  "status": "UNDER_REVIEW"

Camunda:
  gateway condition / process variable / worker logic

Reports:
  status filters
```

### Bad

```sql id="2btmnn"
UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
SET status = 'REVIEWING'
WHERE status = 'UNDER_REVIEW';
```

without app compatibility.

### Safe

```text id="dnmvjs"
1. Add new status REVIEWING
2. App accepts UNDER_REVIEW and REVIEWING
3. API may emit old value until clients ready
4. Camunda workers handle both
5. Backfill rows
6. Switch API emission
7. Deactivate old status later
```

Mapper transitional query:

```xml id="g5n3fi"
<select id="findActiveReviewCases" resultMap="CaseResultMap">
    SELECT *
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case
    WHERE status IN ('UNDER_REVIEW', 'REVIEWING')
</select>
```

---

## 14. Pattern: Function Versioning for Mapper Compatibility

Old mapper:

```xml id="gwblpk"
<select id="calculateSla" resultType="int">
    SELECT case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla(#{caseId})
</select>
```

New function requirement:

```text id="k8ihdb"
fn_calculate_case_sla(case_id, timezone)
```

Do not replace old function directly.

Create v2:

```sql id="3790k9"
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla_v2(
    p_case_id UUID,
    p_timezone TEXT
)
RETURNS INTEGER
LANGUAGE plpgsql
STABLE
AS $$
BEGIN
    RETURN 0;
END;
$$;
```

New mapper:

```xml id="tdk2dp"
<select id="calculateSlaV2" resultType="int">
    SELECT case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla_v2(#{caseId}, #{timezone})
</select>
```

Drop v1 later.

---

## 15. Pattern: View v2 for Read Model Compatibility

Old view:

```sql id="bqwp3z"
case_app.v_case_summary
```

New contract:

```sql id="zwu9mu"
case_app.v_case_summary_v2
```

Old mapper:

```xml id="4jpc5b"
SELECT id, case_number, status
FROM case_app.v_case_summary
```

New mapper:

```xml id="codzxr"
SELECT id, reference_number, status, risk_score
FROM case_app.v_case_summary_v2
```

### Why Good?

```text id="oz2gfz"
old app can keep using old view
new app can use v2 view
contract cleanup happens later
```

This is often cleaner than repeatedly mutating one shared view when output semantics change.

---

## 16. Materialized View Compatibility

Materialized view adds one more contract:

```text id="cv2nlf"
freshness
```

If API reads materialized view, response must not pretend it is real-time.

Transitional API:

```json id="wgjs4m"
{
  "metrics": [
    {
      "status": "OPEN",
      "caseCount": 120
    }
  ],
  "source": "MATERIALIZED_VIEW",
  "lastRefreshedAt": "2026-07-13T02:00:00Z"
}
```

Compatibility rules:

```text id="3hlp8r"
- create mv_v2 first
- initial refresh before consumer switch
- expose freshness
- keep old mv until old app/report is migrated
```

---

## 17. Trigger Compatibility

Trigger changes can alter application behavior without changing mapper SQL.

Example:

```sql id="0dcf71"
CREATE TRIGGER trg_020_validate_status_transition
BEFORE UPDATE OF status ON case_app.enforcement_case
FOR EACH ROW
EXECUTE FUNCTION case_app.trg_fn_validate_status_transition();
```

Old app may perform status transition that new trigger rejects.

### Safe Strategy

```text id="h9yi9n"
1. Audit existing application behavior
2. Deploy app that follows new invariant
3. Monitor violations in soft mode if possible
4. Enable blocking trigger later
```

Soft-mode trigger pattern:

```sql id="zsd09g"
-- Instead of raising exception immediately,
-- write violation to governance table first.
```

Use blocking only after app behavior is clean.

---

## 18. Transaction Boundary Compatibility

Database migration can affect transaction behavior.

Example:

```text id="1tdpn4"
old app:
  update case
  insert audit
  commit

new DB trigger:
  automatically inserts audit

result:
  duplicate audit entries
```

Or:

```text id="nycfh9"
new procedure:
  contains COMMIT

app:
  calls procedure inside service transaction

result:
  transaction error
```

Compatibility review must include:

```text id="baj8ro"
- app-managed transaction
- DB trigger/procedure side effects
- retry behavior
- duplicate writes
- rollback semantics
```

---

## 19. MyBatis `SELECT *` Compatibility Risk

Bad mapper:

```xml id="ze2huq"
<select id="findCase" resultMap="CaseResultMap">
    SELECT *
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case
    WHERE id = #{id}
</select>
```

Risks:

- hidden dependency on column order/names;
- new columns may create ambiguous mapping;
- dropped columns break unexpectedly;
- query contract unclear.

Better:

```xml id="dqcyuz"
<select id="findCase" resultMap="CaseResultMap">
    SELECT
        id,
        case_number,
        status,
        priority,
        created_at,
        updated_at
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case
    WHERE id = #{id}
</select>
```

### Rule

```text id="73b11c"
Explicit column list is mandatory for migration-safe MyBatis.
```

---

## 20. MyBatis Result Mapping Compatibility

Result map:

```xml id="50omcq"
<resultMap id="CaseResultMap" type="com.example.caseapp.CaseEntity">
    <id property="id" column="id"/>
    <result property="caseNumber" column="case_number"/>
    <result property="status" column="status"/>
    <result property="priority" column="priority"/>
</resultMap>
```

If DB column changes:

```text id="s7uock"
case_number → reference_number
```

Transitional mapper:

```xml id="bzyx1l"
<select id="findCase" resultMap="CaseResultMap">
    SELECT
        id,
        COALESCE(reference_number, case_number) AS case_number,
        status,
        priority
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case
    WHERE id = #{id}
</select>
```

Java object can stay stable while DB transitions.

---

## 21. MyBatis Write Compatibility

Old insert:

```xml id="fwhvoq"
<insert id="insertCase">
    INSERT INTO case_app.enforcement_case (
        id,
        case_number,
        status
    )
    VALUES (
        #{id},
        #{caseNumber},
        #{status}
    )
</insert>
```

If new column is required but not nullable, old app breaks.

Safe DB order:

```text id="oe34ku"
1. Add nullable column
2. Deploy app that writes it
3. Backfill old data
4. Add constraint later
```

Do not add required column directly if old app can still insert rows.

---

## 22. API Compatibility Rules

| API Change | Safe? | Strategy |
|---|---:|---|
| Add response field | Usually safe | Ensure clients tolerate unknown fields. |
| Remove response field | Breaking | Deprecate first. |
| Rename response field | Breaking | Emit both temporarily. |
| Change field type | Breaking | New field or API version. |
| Change enum/status value | Breaking | Accept both; emit old until clients ready. |
| Make request field required | Breaking | Accept optional first. |
| Change error code/body | Potentially breaking | Version/contract test. |

### Rule

```text id="4oo6cp"
DB migration should not accidentally become API breaking change.
```

---

## 23. DTO Compatibility Layer

Use DTO mapping to isolate DB evolution from API contract.

Entity:

```java id="2qbxwx"
public record CaseEntity(
    UUID id,
    String caseNumber,
    String referenceNumber,
    String status,
    Integer riskScore
) {}
```

API response:

```java id="7tmfae"
public record CaseResponse(
    UUID id,
    String caseNumber,
    String referenceNumber,
    String status,
    Integer riskScore
) {}
```

Mapper:

```java id="920l3a"
public CaseResponse toResponse(CaseEntity entity) {
    String stableCaseNumber =
        entity.caseNumber() != null
            ? entity.caseNumber()
            : entity.referenceNumber();

    String stableReferenceNumber =
        entity.referenceNumber() != null
            ? entity.referenceNumber()
            : entity.caseNumber();

    return new CaseResponse(
        entity.id(),
        stableCaseNumber,
        stableReferenceNumber,
        entity.status(),
        entity.riskScore()
    );
}
```

### Rule

```text id="z64w5i"
Do not expose database migration turbulence directly to API clients.
```

---

## 24. Feature Flag Compatibility

Feature flag separates deploy from activation.

```text id="zy3enq"
DB migration:
  add risk_score

App deploy:
  code can write/read risk_score

Feature flag:
  enable risk_score behavior gradually
```

Example:

```java id="dgja8j"
if (featureFlags.isEnabled("case.risk-score.write")) {
    entity.setRiskScore(calculateRiskScore(request));
}
```

### Warning

Feature flag cannot protect SQL that references missing column if query still executes.

Bad:

```xml id="b6ni45"
SELECT id, risk_score
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
```

If column does not exist yet, Java flag cannot save the query.

### Rule

```text id="23gqy2"
Schema expand must happen before code path can reference new schema object.
```

---

## 25. Deployment Order Patterns

### Pattern A - Schema First

Use when migration is backward-compatible.

```text id="eo18ub"
1. DB migration
2. Deploy app
```

Examples:

- add nullable column;
- add optional view column;
- add function v2;
- add table unused by old app;
- seed new permission before app checks it.

### Pattern B - App First

Use only when app does not reference missing schema or uses runtime detection safely.

```text id="4vldl5"
1. Deploy app compatible with old schema
2. Run DB migration
3. Enable feature
```

Rare with MyBatis.

### Pattern C - Multi-Release

Use for breaking changes.

```text id="47ho4y"
expand → dual-compatible app → backfill → switch → contract
```

---

## 26. Compatibility Testing Matrix

| Test Scenario | Purpose |
|---|---|
| Latest app + latest schema | Normal target state. |
| Previous app + latest schema | Rolling deploy backward compatibility. |
| Latest app + partially backfilled data | Mixed data state compatibility. |
| Latest app + feature flag off | Safe deploy before activation. |
| Latest app + feature flag on | New behavior works. |
| Camunda old process + latest schema | Running instance compatibility. |
| Mapper tests against migrated DB | SQL contract validation. |
| API contract tests | Client compatibility. |

---

## 27. CI Test Flow

```text id="ts1u1p"
PR / pre-merge:
  1. Start PostgreSQL
  2. Run migrations
  3. Run MyBatis integration tests
  4. Run API contract tests
  5. Run migration compatibility smoke
  6. Run dangerous DDL detection

Pre-production:
  1. Restore staging-like data
  2. Run migration
  3. Run old app smoke
  4. Run new app smoke
  5. Run Camunda worker/process tests
  6. Run backfill simulation if needed
```

---

## 28. API Contract Tests

Example contract test expectations:

```json id="q8m0rp"
{
  "id": "00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001",
  "caseNumber": "CASE-001",
  "status": "OPEN"
}
```

If adding field:

```json id="m027hq"
{
  "id": "00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001",
  "caseNumber": "CASE-001",
  "referenceNumber": "CASE-001",
  "status": "OPEN"
}
```

Test should confirm old fields still exist.

### Rule

```text id="1y109s"
API tests must assert non-removal of old contract fields during transition.
```

---

## 29. Camunda Compatibility

Camunda changes deserve special handling because process instances can run longer than application release windows. Official Camunda material emphasizes process definitions are versioned and that in-flight cases can continue on the version they started under; Camunda 7 process instance migration also has explicit migration plan execution APIs and notes limitations around migrating transition instances and async continuations. ([camunda.com](https://camunda.com/platform/modeler/))

Implication:

```text id="zfz16s"
Do not assume deploying new app version means all process instances use new assumptions.
```

---

## 30. Camunda Risk Areas

| Area | Compatibility Risk |
|---|---|
| Process variable name | Old process uses old variable. |
| Process variable type | Serialized object or JSON shape changes. |
| Business key | Message correlation can fail. |
| Status/reference code | Gateway condition can route differently. |
| Worker topic/job type | Old process may create old job type. |
| External task payload | Worker expects old/new fields. |
| DB query inside worker | Mapper may break after schema change. |
| Form/task data | User task rendering may rely on old field. |
| DMN decision input | Old variable/status name may still be used. |
| Retry behavior | Non-idempotent DB repair can duplicate effect. |

---

## 31. Camunda Process Variable Compatibility

Old process variable:

```json id="72cniu"
{
  "caseNumber": "CASE-001"
}
```

New application wants:

```json id="sfqtde"
{
  "referenceNumber": "CASE-001"
}
```

Safe transition:

```json id="kd2lqv"
{
  "caseNumber": "CASE-001",
  "referenceNumber": "CASE-001"
}
```

Worker:

```java id="0tmkoa"
String referenceNumber =
    variables.containsKey("referenceNumber")
        ? variables.get("referenceNumber").toString()
        : variables.get("caseNumber").toString();
```

Later, after all old process instances complete or are migrated, remove fallback.

---

## 32. Camunda Worker Database Compatibility

Worker query:

```xml id="6l8a2j"
<select id="findCaseForWorker" resultMap="CaseResultMap">
    SELECT
        id,
        case_number,
        status
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case
    WHERE id = #{caseId}
</select>
```

If schema changed to `reference_number`, old worker fails.

Safe transitional query:

```xml id="v54cdt"
<select id="findCaseForWorker" resultMap="CaseResultMap">
    SELECT
        id,
        COALESCE(reference_number, case_number) AS case_number,
        status
    FROM case_app.enforcement_case
    WHERE id = #{caseId}
</select>
```

### Rule

```text id="6gugjs"
Camunda worker mapper must be included in DB migration compatibility tests.
```

---

## 33. Camunda Retry and Idempotency

Workers may retry failed jobs. If a worker calls a procedure or writes repair data, operation must be idempotent.

Bad:

```sql id="o9hkdq"
INSERT INTO case_app.case_task (case_id, task_type)
VALUES (#{caseId}, 'ESCALATION');
```

Retry creates duplicate task.

Better:

```sql id="dv2o7q"
INSERT INTO case_app.case_task (case_id, task_type)
VALUES (#{caseId}, 'ESCALATION')
ON CONFLICT (case_id, task_type) DO NOTHING;
```

### Rule

```text id="5xcnv4"
Every DB side effect triggered by Camunda worker must be retry-safe.
```

---

## 34. Camunda Status Change Compatibility

If BPMN gateway uses status:

```text id="7qt0wi"
${caseStatus == "UNDER_REVIEW"}
```

Changing DB seed/status to `REVIEWING` can break routing.

Safe worker/service logic:

```java id="s9gcgq"
boolean isUnderReview =
    status.equals("UNDER_REVIEW") || status.equals("REVIEWING");
```

DMN/BPMN migration plan:

```text id="kgyobu"
1. Add new status
2. Support old + new in workers
3. Deploy new BPMN/DMN
4. Migrate/complete old instances
5. Backfill status
6. Remove old status after zero usage
```

---

## 35. Camunda and Contract Phase

Before dropping old column/status/function:

```text id="nq9qos"
Check:
  - active process instances
  - incidents
  - jobs waiting/retrying
  - external task workers
  - BPMN/DMN variables
  - process definitions still deployed
  - form/task data dependencies
```

Do not contract if active process instances still need old assumptions.

---

## 36. Authorization Compatibility

DB seed and schema changes often affect authorization.

Example:

```text id="1osoh6"
new permission CASE_REASSIGN
```

Safe sequence:

```text id="5rkpjm"
1. Seed permission
2. Deploy app that knows permission
3. Assign permission to role
4. Enable feature
5. Audit access behavior
```

Do not deploy app that checks a permission before the permission exists, unless fallback is explicit.

---

## 37. Error Compatibility

DB constraint changes can alter API error behavior.

Before:

```text id="2q6x6c"
invalid status accepted by DB,
app validation catches it and returns 400
```

After:

```text id="n0doai"
DB CHECK constraint rejects it,
PostgreSQL exception leaks as 500
```

Safe service layer:

```java id="trzeaz"
try {
    mapper.updateStatus(caseId, status);
} catch (PersistenceException ex) {
    if (isConstraintViolation(ex)) {
        throw new BadRequestException("Invalid status transition");
    }
    throw ex;
}
```

### Rule

```text id="dqbzf9"
New DB constraints must map to stable API error semantics.
```

---

## 38. Runtime Schema Detection: Use Sparingly

Sometimes app checks whether a column exists. This can support transition, but it adds complexity.

Example:

```sql id="lp495j"
SELECT EXISTS (
    SELECT 1
    FROM information_schema.columns
    WHERE table_schema = 'case_app'
      AND table_name = 'enforcement_case'
      AND column_name = 'risk_score'
);
```

Use only for:

- library/shared product deployed to multiple schema versions;
- gradual migration across many tenants;
- admin compatibility checks.

Avoid for normal service releases if explicit migration order is available.

---

## 39. Compatibility Smoke Tests

After migration and before/after app deploy:

### DB Smoke

```sql id="ercq1f"
SELECT COUNT(*)
FROM case_app.case_status_reference;
```

### Mapper Smoke

```text id="se9m0p"
CaseMapper.findCaseSummary(sampleCaseId)
CaseMapper.insertCase(sampleInput)
CaseMapper.updateCaseStatus(sampleCaseId)
```

### API Smoke

```text id="95luys"
GET /cases/{id}
PATCH /cases/{id}/status
POST /cases
```

### Workflow Smoke

```text id="aybbla"
start simple process instance
complete relevant task
worker executes query
status transition succeeds
```

---

## 40. Dependency Search Before Contract

Repository search:

```bash id="owu55u"
grep -R "case_number" \
  src/main/java \
  src/main/resources/mybatis \
  src/main/resources/db \
  src/main/resources/**/*.bpmn \
  src/main/resources/**/*.dmn \
  src/main/resources/**/*.json \
  docs \
  --line-number
```

Database search:

```sql id="il1bps"
SELECT
    schemaname,
    viewname,
    definition
FROM pg_views
WHERE definition ILIKE '%case_number%';
```

Function search:

```sql id="5ctzyt"
SELECT
    n.nspname,
    p.proname,
    pg_get_functiondef(p.oid)
FROM pg_proc p
JOIN pg_namespace n
    ON n.oid = p.pronamespace
WHERE pg_get_functiondef(p.oid) ILIKE '%case_number%';
```

Do not contract while references still exist.

---

## 41. Compatibility PR Template

```md id="1gxmp9"
## Application Compatibility Review

### DB Change Type
- [ ] Additive
- [ ] Rename
- [ ] Drop
- [ ] Type change
- [ ] Constraint
- [ ] View/function/procedure
- [ ] Trigger
- [ ] Seed/reference data

### MyBatis Impact
- [ ] Mapper search completed
- [ ] No SELECT *
- [ ] ResultMap updated
- [ ] Old mapper compatibility tested
- [ ] New mapper compatibility tested

### Jersey/API Impact
- [ ] Response field compatibility reviewed
- [ ] Request compatibility reviewed
- [ ] Error semantics reviewed
- [ ] API contract tests updated
- [ ] Deprecated fields documented

### Camunda Impact
- [ ] BPMN/DMN search completed
- [ ] Process variable compatibility reviewed
- [ ] Worker retry/idempotency reviewed
- [ ] Active instance impact reviewed
- [ ] Old process version compatibility reviewed

### Deployment
- [ ] Migration order defined
- [ ] Feature flag if needed
- [ ] Backfill plan if needed
- [ ] Contract phase delayed
```

---

## 42. CI/CD Gates

```text id="r63lsu"
Pull Request
  ├─ detect DB migration changes
  ├─ detect mapper changes
  ├─ detect API DTO changes
  ├─ detect BPMN/DMN changes
  ├─ run PostgreSQL migration
  ├─ run MyBatis integration tests
  ├─ run API contract tests
  ├─ run worker tests
  ├─ run old-app/new-schema smoke if high risk
  └─ require compatibility checklist
```

Danger detector:

```bash id="4v1yci"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

if git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD | grep -E "db/migration|db/changelog"; then
  echo "DB migration changed. Compatibility review required."
fi

if git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD | grep -E "mybatis|Mapper.xml"; then
  echo "MyBatis mapper changed. Run DB integration tests."
fi

if git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD | grep -E "bpmn|dmn"; then
  echo "BPMN/DMN changed. Camunda compatibility review required."
fi
```

---

## 43. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Why Dangerous | Better |
|---|---|---|
| Migration succeeds, app not tested | Runtime mapper/API failure. | PostgreSQL + MyBatis integration test. |
| Drop column in same release as mapper change | Old pods break. | Contract later. |
| Rename API field because DB renamed | Client break. | Emit both temporarily. |
| Add NOT NULL before old app stops writing null | Insert failures. | Add nullable, backfill, enforce later. |
| Change status code directly | Workflow/API/report break. | Support old + new, migrate gradually. |
| Change function signature in place | Old mapper fails. | Create v2 function. |
| Replace view destructively | Mapper/report break. | Additive or v2 view. |
| Feature flag references missing column | SQL still fails. | Schema expand first. |
| Ignore Camunda active instances | Running process fails later. | Process compatibility review. |
| Non-idempotent worker DB write | Retry duplicates side effect. | Unique key / operation id. |
| DB constraint leaks as API 500 | Bad client behavior. | Exception translation. |

---

## 44. Practical Recommendation for Your Stack

Untuk stack **Jersey/JAX-RS + MyBatis + Camunda + PostgreSQL**, standard compatibility yang saya rekomendasikan:

```text id="0abqqm"
1. Treat MyBatis SQL as database contract.
2. Treat Jersey DTO/JSON as client contract.
3. Treat Camunda variables/status/job payload as workflow contract.
4. Use expand/contract for any breaking DB change.
5. Never drop/rename in the same release that introduces replacement.
6. Use v2 view/function/materialized view for major contract change.
7. Keep API fields stable even if DB fields change.
8. Make workers idempotent.
9. Test old app + new schema for high-risk migrations.
10. Delay contract until repository, DB, and workflow dependencies are zero.
```

---

## 45. Production Readiness Checklist

| Area | Required |
|---|---|
| MyBatis | Mapper tests pass against migrated PostgreSQL. |
| API | Contract tests prove old fields/errors stable. |
| DTO mapping | Handles old/new fields where needed. |
| Feature flag | Does not reference missing schema before migration. |
| Camunda | Active process/worker compatibility reviewed. |
| Status/reference data | Old and new values supported during transition. |
| Backfill | App tolerates partial state. |
| Deployment | Order documented: migration/app/backfill/switch/contract. |
| Rollback | Old app can run on expanded schema. |
| Contract | Dependency search complete before drop/removal. |
| Observability | Logs/metrics catch mapper SQL errors and workflow incidents. |

---

## 46. Ringkasan Seri 17

| Area | Kesimpulan |
|---|---|
| Application compatibility | Sama pentingnya dengan validitas SQL migration. |
| Rolling deployment | Old dan new pods bisa hidup bersama, maka DB harus kompatibel. |
| MyBatis | SQL mapper, resultMap, column alias adalah kontrak. |
| Jersey/JAX-RS | DTO/JSON request-response adalah client contract. |
| Camunda | Running process instances bisa hidup melewati beberapa release. |
| Rename/drop/type change | Harus expand/contract. |
| Status/reference data | Treat as API/workflow contract. |
| Function/view/matview | Gunakan v2 untuk breaking contract. |
| Trigger | Bisa mengubah behavior app tanpa mapper berubah. |
| Feature flag | Membantu activation, tetapi tidak menyelamatkan missing schema. |
| Testing | Perlu MyBatis, API contract, worker, dan old-app/new-schema smoke. |
| Contract phase | Baru boleh setelah dependency benar-benar zero. |

## Response:
7/13/2026, 8:11:20 PM

Saya lanjut ke **Seri 18 dari 18**: Production Runbook, Failure Handling, and Auditability. Ini seri penutup yang mengikat seluruh migration lifecycle dari pre-check, eksekusi, incident handling, sampai evidence/audit.

# Seri 18 - Production Runbook, Failure Handling, and Auditability

## 1. Tujuan Seri Ini

Seri terakhir ini membahas bagaimana menjalankan database migration di production dengan **runbook, failure handling, audit trail, dan recovery discipline**.

Fokusnya bukan lagi hanya “migration sukses”, tetapi:

```text id="6ha3tc"
- apakah migration siap dijalankan?
- bagaimana kalau gagal di tengah?
- siapa yang boleh mengambil keputusan?
- kapan rollback realistis?
- kapan roll-forward lebih aman?
- evidence apa yang harus disimpan?
- bagaimana mencegah metadata Flyway/Liquibase rusak?
- bagaimana memastikan app, MyBatis, Jersey API, dan Camunda tetap sehat?
```

PostgreSQL sendiri menekankan bahwa database bernilai harus dibackup secara reguler, dan dokumentasinya membagi pendekatan backup menjadi SQL dump, file system level backup, dan continuous archiving/PITR; setiap pendekatan punya trade-off sendiri. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/backup.html))

---

## 2. Mental Model Production Migration

```text id="hhm87i"
Production Migration Lifecycle
────────────────────────────────────────────

Pre-Check
  ├─ PR approved
  ├─ staging passed
  ├─ backup/restore point confirmed
  ├─ migration risk classified
  ├─ app compatibility verified
  └─ runbook ready

Execution
  ├─ acquire deployment lock
  ├─ run migration job once
  ├─ capture logs
  ├─ verify schema history
  ├─ run smoke checks
  └─ deploy app if safe

Failure Handling
  ├─ classify failure
  ├─ stop rollout
  ├─ inspect DB/tool metadata
  ├─ decide fix-forward / rollback / restore
  └─ document incident

Post-Migration
  ├─ verify app/API/MyBatis/Camunda
  ├─ archive evidence
  ├─ monitor DB and app
  ├─ close runbook
  └─ schedule follow-up contract/backfill if needed
```

Core rule:

```text id="l2ly2e"
Production migration is a controlled release event,
not a casual command execution.
```

---

## 3. Production Migration Risk Classification

| Risk Level | Example | Required Control |
|---|---|---|
| **Low** | Add nullable column, add small lookup seed, add backward-compatible function | Normal PR + CI + staging. |
| **Medium** | Add index, add view column, add constraint `NOT VALID`, seed permission catalog | DB reviewer + smoke test + rollback/roll-forward plan. |
| **High** | Rename/type change staged, large backfill, trigger behavior change, materialized view replacement | DBA/domain approval + runbook + monitoring + staged rollout. |
| **Critical** | Drop column/table, destructive cleanup, broad permission change, event trigger blocking, table rewrite on large table | Formal change window + backup/restore confirmation + breakglass plan. |

Practical rule:

```text id="2pgocf"
The more destructive or irreversible the migration,
the more you should prefer staged roll-forward over direct rollback.
```

---

## 4. Pre-Production Checklist

Sebelum production migration:

| Area | Checklist |
|---|---|
| Code | PR approved, no unreviewed migration file. |
| Tool | Flyway/Liquibase validation passed. |
| DB | Migration tested against real PostgreSQL, not only mock/H2. |
| App | MyBatis integration tests pass. |
| API | Jersey API smoke/contract tests pass. |
| Workflow | Camunda worker/process compatibility reviewed. |
| Data | Backfill/data repair plan exists if needed. |
| Security | Migration role and runtime role separated. |
| Backup | Restore point / PITR / snapshot policy confirmed. |
| Roll-forward | Corrective migration path documented. |
| Rollback | Only claimed if tested and realistic. |
| Owner | DB owner, service owner, and domain owner identified. |
| Evidence | Log/report capture location known. |

---

## 5. Production Runbook Template

```md id="qql0ey"
# Production DB Migration Runbook

## Service
case-service

## Release
2026.07.13-001

## Migration Tool
Flyway / Liquibase

## Migration Scope
- Add nullable column risk_score
- Update v_case_summary
- Seed CASE_RISK_READ permission

## Risk Level
Medium

## Owners
- Service owner:
- DB reviewer:
- Domain owner:
- Release commander:

## Pre-Checks
- CI passed
- Staging migration passed
- MyBatis tests passed
- API smoke tests passed
- Camunda compatibility reviewed
- Backup/restore point confirmed
- Migration credentials verified
- Runtime credentials do not have DDL

## Execution Steps
1. Acquire deployment lock.
2. Run migration job.
3. Capture migration logs.
4. Verify migration metadata.
5. Run schema smoke checks.
6. Deploy application.
7. Run API smoke checks.
8. Monitor DB/app/workflow.

## Failure Handling
- If migration fails before app deploy: stop rollout.
- If schema partially changed: classify and inspect.
- If app fails after DB migration: decide app rollback vs DB fix-forward.
- Do not manually edit metadata tables without approval.

## Evidence
- Migration job log:
- Flyway info / Liquibase status:
- Smoke test result:
- Approval link:
- Incident link if any:
```

---

## 6. Execution Command Pattern

### Flyway

```bash id="ypvo6f"
flyway info
flyway validate
flyway migrate
flyway info
```

Flyway’s Maven plugin exposes goals such as `migrate`, `info`, `validate`, `baseline`, `repair`, and `clean`; in production flow, `info`, `validate`, and `migrate` are normal commands, while `repair` is a recovery command, not a routine deployment step. ([documentation.red-gate.com](https://documentation.red-gate.com/fd/flyway-schema-history-table-273973417.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

### Liquibase

```bash id="5esovy"
liquibase validate
liquibase status --verbose
liquibase updateSQL
liquibase update
liquibase status --verbose
```

Liquibase `status --verbose` can list undeployed changesets, including path, author, and other identifying information, which makes it useful before and after deployment verification. ([docs.liquibase.com](https://docs.liquibase.com/secure/reference-guide-5-1/database-inspection-change-tracking-and-utility-commands/what-are-change-tracking-commands?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 7. Pre-Migration Verification Queries

### Current DB Identity

```sql id="68f2jg"
SELECT
    current_database(),
    current_user,
    inet_client_addr(),
    current_setting('application_name', true) AS application_name,
    now();
```

### Current Schema Version - Flyway

```sql id="lnnhd6"
SELECT
    installed_rank,
    version,
    description,
    type,
    script,
    checksum,
    installed_by,
    installed_on,
    execution_time,
    success
FROM flyway_schema_history
ORDER BY installed_rank DESC
LIMIT 20;
```

### Current Changelog - Liquibase

```sql id="1hjj4u"
SELECT
    id,
    author,
    filename,
    dateexecuted,
    orderexecuted,
    exectype,
    md5sum,
    description,
    comments,
    tag
FROM databasechangelog
ORDER BY orderexecuted DESC
LIMIT 20;
```

### Lock Check

```sql id="lspsr1"
SELECT
    pid,
    usename,
    application_name,
    state,
    wait_event_type,
    wait_event,
    query
FROM pg_stat_activity
WHERE datname = current_database()
ORDER BY query_start NULLS LAST;
```

---

## 8. Backup / Restore Point Confirmation

Untuk migration high-risk, jangan hanya bilang “sudah ada backup”. Konfirmasi jenis backup dan recovery method.

| Backup Type | Use Case | Catatan |
|---|---|---|
| SQL dump | Small/medium logical backup, selective restore | `pg_dump` membuat export konsisten meski database sedang digunakan, dan tidak memblokir reader/writer; tetapi docs juga menyebut `pg_dump` bukan pilihan utama untuk backup reguler production kecuali kasus tertentu. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/app-pgdump.html)) |
| Custom/directory dump | Lebih fleksibel untuk restore selektif | Archive format seperti custom/directory dipulihkan dengan `pg_restore` dan memungkinkan pemilihan/reordering object. ([PostgreSQL](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/app-pgdump.html)) |
| Snapshot storage/cloud | Restore database/volume cepat | Bergantung platform AWS/Azure/on-prem. |
| PITR / continuous archiving | Recovery ke titik waktu tertentu | Cocok untuk production database penting. |
| Table-level export | Data repair spesifik | Tidak menggantikan full restore strategy. |

### Backup Confirmation Template

```md id="si83tx"
## Backup Confirmation

Backup type:
- [ ] PITR available
- [ ] Snapshot available
- [ ] Logical dump available
- [ ] Table-level export available

Restore target tested?
- [ ] Yes
- [ ] No

Restore owner:
Restore estimated time:
Latest restore point timestamp:
Backup reference:
```

---

## 9. Failure Classification

Ketika migration gagal, jangan langsung menjalankan `repair`, `rollback`, atau edit metadata. Klasifikasikan dulu.

| Failure Type | Example | First Action |
|---|---|---|
| Syntax error | SQL typo, invalid object name | Stop, fix migration, rerun only if not applied. |
| Permission error | Migration role lacks `ALTER`/`CREATE`/`EXECUTE` | Stop, fix role/grant, rerun. |
| Lock timeout | DDL cannot acquire lock | Stop or retry in window; inspect blockers. |
| Statement timeout | Backfill/index/validation too long | Stop, redesign chunk/window. |
| Constraint violation | Existing data invalid | Repair data, then validate. |
| Dependency error | View/function depends on missing object | Fix ordering or create dependency. |
| Partial non-transactional change | Concurrent index failed, external side effect occurred | Inspect object state manually. |
| Tool metadata mismatch | Checksum mismatch, missing migration file | Investigate source control/history. |
| App compatibility failure | Migration succeeds, app fails | Roll app only if DB compatible; otherwise fix-forward. |
| Data correctness issue | Migration produced wrong values | Corrective repair/backfill. |

---

## 10. Immediate Failure Response

```text id="u57pfp"
1. Stop rollout.
2. Do not deploy app if migration failed before app deploy.
3. Capture logs.
4. Capture DB metadata.
5. Identify whether transaction rolled back.
6. Identify whether any non-transactional object was created.
7. Decide: rerun, fix-forward, rollback, or restore.
8. Document decision.
```

### Evidence to Capture Immediately

```bash id="xq0zi9"
kubectl logs job/case-service-db-migration-20260713-001
kubectl describe job/case-service-db-migration-20260713-001
```

```sql id="udkzp2"
SELECT * FROM flyway_schema_history ORDER BY installed_rank DESC LIMIT 20;
```

or:

```sql id="k9z18b"
SELECT * FROM databasechangelog ORDER BY orderexecuted DESC LIMIT 20;
```

---

## 11. Flyway Failure Handling

Flyway stores migration metadata in its schema history table. Redgate documentation says Flyway validation can fail when a versioned migration checksum differs from the schema history record, often because an already-applied migration was edited, or when a versioned migration recorded in history can no longer be found. ([documentation.red-gate.com](https://documentation.red-gate.com/fd/flyway-schema-history-table-273973417.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

### Common Flyway Failures

| Failure | Meaning | Recommended Response |
|---|---|---|
| Failed migration row | Migration started and failed. | Inspect DB object state; fix SQL; rerun if safe. |
| Checksum mismatch | Applied migration file changed. | Do not blindly repair; restore original file or create new migration. |
| Missing migration file | File applied before but absent now. | Restore file or formal metadata repair after approval. |
| Out-of-order migration | New migration version lower than current applied. | Rebase/renumber or explicitly allow if policy supports. |
| Non-transactional DDL failure | Object may partially exist. | Inspect object manually, clean/fix-forward. |

### `repair` Warning

Flyway `repair` modifies schema history metadata to make it consistent with migration files, and Redgate explains it is used after validation errors such as checksum mismatch or missing migrations. It should be treated as an incident recovery command, not routine deployment. ([Redgate](https://www.red-gate.com/hub/product-learning/flyway/flyways-repair-command-explained-simply/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Practical policy:

```text id="sifkzm"
flyway repair requires:
  - root cause identified
  - DB state inspected
  - service owner approval
  - DB owner approval
  - evidence archived
```

---

## 12. Flyway Recovery Decision Tree

```text id="s0kpsu"
Did migration fail before any SQL executed?
  ├─ Yes → fix script/config and rerun.
  └─ No
      │
      ├─ Was migration transactional and rolled back?
      │   ├─ Yes → fix script and rerun.
      │   └─ No / unsure
      │       ├─ Inspect created/modified objects.
      │       ├─ Write corrective migration or cleanup.
      │       └─ Rerun only after DB state known.
      │
      ├─ Is there checksum mismatch?
      │   ├─ Restore original migration file if possible.
      │   ├─ If intentional, create new migration instead.
      │   └─ Use repair only after formal approval.
      │
      └─ Is app already deployed?
          ├─ If DB compatible with old app → rollback app possible.
          └─ If DB not compatible → fix-forward.
```

---

## 13. Liquibase Failure Handling

Liquibase rollback can reverse changes based on tag, date/time, or number of changesets; when Liquibase rolls back a changeset, it removes the corresponding row from `DATABASECHANGELOG`. ([docs.liquibase.com](https://docs.liquibase.com/secure/user-guide-5-1-1/what-is-a-rollback?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

### Common Liquibase Failures

| Failure | Meaning | Response |
|---|---|---|
| Checksum validation failed | Deployed changeset changed after execution | Restore original or use approved checksum/update path. |
| Precondition failed | DB state not as expected | Decide `MARK_RAN`, fix data/schema, or stop. |
| Rollback missing | SQL changeset has no rollback | Prefer fix-forward unless rollback script exists. |
| Lock stuck | `DATABASECHANGELOGLOCK` held | Confirm no active Liquibase process before releasing. |
| Context/label mistake | Wrong changeset included/excluded | Stop and correct deployment command. |
| Partial DDL | Depends on transaction support and SQL | Inspect actual DB state. |

### Liquibase Rollback Warning

Liquibase rollback is powerful, but not automatically safe. The official rollback docs describe rollback to a prior state by tag/date/count, but production safety still depends on whether the changes are logically reversible and whether rollback SQL exists and was tested. ([docs.liquibase.com](https://docs.liquibase.com/secure/user-guide-5-1-1/what-is-a-rollback?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 14. Liquibase Recovery Decision Tree

```text id="z3dq1q"
Did update fail before changeset executed?
  ├─ Yes → fix config/changelog and rerun.
  └─ No
      │
      ├─ Was changeset transactional and rolled back?
      │   ├─ Yes → fix and rerun.
      │   └─ No / unsure → inspect DB state.
      │
      ├─ Is rollback defined and tested?
      │   ├─ Yes → rollback may be considered.
      │   └─ No → fix-forward usually safer.
      │
      ├─ Is DATABASECHANGELOGLOCK stuck?
      │   ├─ Confirm no running Liquibase process.
      │   └─ Release only with DB owner approval.
      │
      └─ Did context/label deploy wrong changes?
          ├─ Stop rollout.
          ├─ Identify applied changesets.
          └─ Roll-forward or tested rollback.
```

---

## 15. Metadata Table Safety

### Never casually edit these:

```text id="wstpqn"
flyway_schema_history
databasechangelog
databasechangeloglock
```

These tables are the migration tool’s memory.

Manual modification can cause:

- migrations skipped incorrectly;
- migrations rerun incorrectly;
- checksum drift hidden;
- audit evidence corrupted;
- different environments diverge.

### Metadata Change Policy

```text id="vbf2jy"
Manual metadata change requires:
  - incident ticket
  - DB owner approval
  - exact SQL reviewed
  - before/after snapshot
  - root cause documented
  - follow-up prevention
```

---

## 16. Lock Failure Handling

### Find Blockers

```sql id="l1vywc"
SELECT
    blocked.pid AS blocked_pid,
    blocked.query AS blocked_query,
    blocking.pid AS blocking_pid,
    blocking.query AS blocking_query,
    blocking.state AS blocking_state
FROM pg_stat_activity blocked
JOIN pg_locks blocked_locks
    ON blocked_locks.pid = blocked.pid
JOIN pg_locks blocking_locks
    ON blocking_locks.locktype = blocked_locks.locktype
   AND blocking_locks.database IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked_locks.database
   AND blocking_locks.relation IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked_locks.relation
   AND blocking_locks.page IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked_locks.page
   AND blocking_locks.tuple IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked_locks.tuple
   AND blocking_locks.virtualxid IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked_locks.virtualxid
   AND blocking_locks.transactionid IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked_locks.transactionid
   AND blocking_locks.classid IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked_locks.classid
   AND blocking_locks.objid IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked_locks.objid
   AND blocking_locks.objsubid IS NOT DISTINCT FROM blocked_locks.objsubid
   AND blocking_locks.pid <> blocked_locks.pid
JOIN pg_stat_activity blocking
    ON blocking.pid = blocking_locks.pid
WHERE NOT blocked_locks.granted
  AND blocking_locks.granted;
```

### Decision

| Situation | Action |
|---|---|
| Blocking query is normal user transaction | Wait if short; otherwise reschedule. |
| Blocking query is stuck/idle in transaction | Escalate to DBA/app owner. |
| Migration uses risky lock | Redesign migration. |
| Lock timeout intentional | Treat as safe abort and retry in better window. |

---

## 17. Constraint Failure Handling

Example failure:

```text id="jpe1k1"
ALTER TABLE ... VALIDATE CONSTRAINT failed
```

Response:

```text id="duab4m"
1. Do not drop or bypass constraint blindly.
2. Find invalid rows.
3. Repair data.
4. Rerun validation.
5. Capture evidence.
```

Find invalid risk score:

```sql id="m2sv3t"
SELECT id, risk_score
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
WHERE risk_score < 0
   OR risk_score > 100
   OR risk_score IS NULL
LIMIT 100;
```

Repair:

```sql id="zlwsf6"
UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
SET risk_score = 0
WHERE risk_score IS NULL;
```

Then validate again.

---

## 18. Failed Index Handling

### Normal Index

If `CREATE INDEX` fails transactionally, usually no index remains.

### Concurrent Index

`CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` is non-transactional and can leave invalid index if it fails.

Check:

```sql id="fmrs1t"
SELECT
    ns.nspname AS schema_name,
    cls.relname AS index_name,
    idx.indisvalid,
    idx.indisready,
    pg_get_indexdef(idx.indexrelid) AS index_def
FROM pg_index idx
JOIN pg_class cls
    ON cls.oid = idx.indexrelid
JOIN pg_namespace ns
    ON ns.oid = cls.relnamespace
WHERE ns.nspname = 'case_app'
ORDER BY cls.relname;
```

Recovery options:

```sql id="p4t39e"
DROP INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF EXISTS case_app.idx_failed_index;
```

or rebuild with reviewed migration.

---

## 19. Failed View / Function / Procedure Migration

If repeatable view/function/procedure migration fails:

```text id="tx7ho9"
- old definition may still exist if CREATE OR REPLACE failed before replacement
- dependent object may fail to compile/refresh
- mapper/API may still use old behavior
```

Response:

```text id="q3ee5l"
1. Check object definition.
2. Check dependent views/materialized views.
3. Run direct SQL smoke.
4. Run MyBatis mapper test.
5. Deploy corrected repeatable migration.
```

Definition check:

```sql id="5i8qmm"
SELECT pg_get_functiondef('case_app.fn_calculate_case_sla(uuid)'::regprocedure);
```

View check:

```sql id="zhgzma"
SELECT definition
FROM pg_views
WHERE schemaname = 'case_app'
  AND viewname = 'v_case_summary';
```

---

## 20. Failed Trigger Migration

Trigger migration failure is high-risk because it affects write behavior.

Response:

```text id="28a2xm"
1. Check whether trigger function exists.
2. Check whether trigger binding exists.
3. Check whether writes still behave as expected.
4. If trigger partially changed, run write-path smoke.
5. Avoid disabling trigger unless runbook-approved.
```

Check triggers:

```sql id="8b44ml"
SELECT
    event_object_schema,
    event_object_table,
    trigger_name,
    action_timing,
    event_manipulation,
    action_statement
FROM information_schema.triggers
WHERE event_object_schema = 'case_app'
ORDER BY event_object_table, trigger_name;
```

Write smoke:

```sql id="0kgxfp"
UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
SET status = status
WHERE id = '00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001';
```

Then verify side effects like `updated_at` or audit rows.

---

## 21. Failed Backfill / Data Repair

Backfill failure is usually not solved by transaction rollback if batches have committed.

Response:

```text id="jgaxcq"
1. Pause job.
2. Capture progress.
3. Identify last successful batch.
4. Identify failed row(s).
5. Decide skip, repair, or code fix.
6. Resume from idempotent condition.
7. Validate completion.
```

Progress:

```sql id="dzy4s9"
SELECT *
FROM case_app.migration_job_progress
WHERE job_name = 'backfill_enforcement_case_risk_score';
```

Remaining work:

```sql id="ttf64o"
SELECT COUNT(*)
FROM case_app.enforcement_case
WHERE risk_score IS NULL;
```

Failed audit:

```sql id="jfib4c"
SELECT *
FROM case_app.migration_job_batch_log
WHERE status = 'FAILED'
ORDER BY started_at DESC;
```

---

## 22. App Failure After Successful DB Migration

This is common.

### Situation

```text id="exgvsr"
DB migration succeeded.
New app deployment fails.
```

Decision:

| DB Change | Can Roll App Back? | Notes |
|---|---:|---|
| Add nullable column | Usually yes | Old app ignores it. |
| Add table/function/view unused by old app | Usually yes | Safe expand. |
| Add constraint affecting old writes | Maybe no | Old app may violate constraint. |
| Rename/drop column | No | Old app likely broken. |
| Change trigger behavior | Maybe no | Old app writes may fail. |
| Change status seed | Maybe no | Old app may not understand value. |

### Rule

```text id="pfq1qr"
App rollback is safe only if old app is compatible with new DB.
```

If not, fix-forward is usually safer.

---

## 23. Rollback vs Roll-Forward Decision

| Situation | Prefer |
|---|---|
| Function/view body bug | Roll-forward corrected definition. |
| Additive migration no app dependency | Roll-forward or leave as-is. |
| Bad seed label/sort order | Corrective seed. |
| Bad permission grant | Corrective revoke/deactivate with security approval. |
| Bad constraint added | Drop constraint if safe or fix data. |
| Bad backfill values | Corrective repair using audit/mapping. |
| Drop column/table executed | Restore/PITR or emergency reconstruction; rollback unlikely. |
| Metadata mismatch only | Metadata repair after investigation. |

Core rule:

```text id="teq8w4"
Rollback is a tool.
Roll-forward is often the production-safe strategy.
Restore is the last-resort data recovery strategy.
```

---

## 24. Liquibase Rollback Practical Guidance

Liquibase rollback by tag/date/count is useful only if rollback is actually defined and safe. Liquibase docs say rollback can target tag, date/time, or number of changesets; tag-based rollback returns DB to the state when the tag was applied. ([docs.liquibase.com](https://docs.liquibase.com/secure/user-guide-5-1-1/what-is-a-rollback?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

### Safe Liquibase Rollback Candidate

| Change | Rollback Candidate? |
|---|---:|
| Create small table unused | Yes |
| Create index | Yes |
| Add nullable column unused | Maybe |
| Add view/function | Yes, if previous definition known |
| Seed new reference value | Maybe deactivate instead |
| Drop column/table | Usually no |
| Backfill | Usually no |
| Rename/type change | Usually no unless explicitly staged |

### Command Example

```bash id="cm3ui3"
liquibase rollback --tag=before_case_service_20260713_001
```

But do not run this unless:

```text id="g5w4aj"
- tag exists
- rollback SQL was reviewed
- rollback was tested in staging
- app compatibility after rollback is known
```

---

## 25. Flyway Rollback Practical Guidance

Flyway Community-style versioned migrations are primarily forward migrations. In many teams, production rollback is implemented as a new corrective migration rather than undoing history.

Example:

```text id="d4i8ed"
Bad:
  V001200__add_bad_constraint.sql

Fix-forward:
  V001201__drop_bad_constraint.sql
```

### Why?

```text id="g8vlkr"
- schema history remains linear
- audit trail is preserved
- old migrations are not edited
- environments converge through same scripts
```

---

## 26. Restore / PITR Decision

Use restore/PITR when:

```text id="9fqbuf"
- destructive data loss happened
- wrong data repair affected too many rows
- corrective repair is impossible or unsafe
- legal/compliance requires exact recovery
- drop/truncate occurred accidentally
```

Do not treat restore as simple:

```text id="ewqi3u"
restore impacts downtime, data loss window,
external systems, event streams, Camunda state,
and app consistency.
```

Restore decision must involve:

- DBA/platform;
- service owner;
- incident commander;
- business/domain owner;
- security/compliance if relevant.

---

## 27. Audit Evidence Collection

For every production migration, archive:

| Evidence | Why |
|---|---|
| Git commit / PR link | Source of change. |
| Release version | Deployment traceability. |
| Migration job name | Kubernetes execution trace. |
| Migration logs | What happened. |
| Flyway info / Liquibase status | DB state before/after. |
| SQL preview / updateSQL | Review artifact. |
| Approval record | Governance. |
| Backup confirmation | Recovery readiness. |
| Smoke test results | Verification. |
| App deployment result | End-to-end release outcome. |
| Incident ticket if failed | Root cause and follow-up. |

---

## 28. Migration Audit Table

Optional but useful.

```sql id="xxiqi2"
CREATE TABLE case_app.deployment_migration_audit (
    id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    service_name TEXT NOT NULL,
    release_version TEXT NOT NULL,
    git_sha TEXT NOT NULL,
    migration_tool TEXT NOT NULL,
    migration_job_name TEXT NOT NULL,
    db_user TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT current_user,
    status TEXT NOT NULL,
    started_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    completed_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    error_message TEXT,
    evidence_url TEXT
);
```

Insert start:

```sql id="1uwk6s"
INSERT INTO case_app.deployment_migration_audit (
    service_name,
    release_version,
    git_sha,
    migration_tool,
    migration_job_name,
    status
)
VALUES (
    'case-service',
    '2026.07.13-001',
    'abc123',
    'flyway',
    'case-service-db-migration-20260713-001',
    'RUNNING'
);
```

Mark success:

```sql id="09e0wp"
UPDATE case_app.deployment_migration_audit
SET
    status = 'SUCCESS',
    completed_at = now()
WHERE migration_job_name = 'case-service-db-migration-20260713-001';
```

## 29. Post-Migration Verification

### Schema Verification

```sql id="fhip2u"
SELECT EXISTS (
    SELECT 1
    FROM information_schema.columns
    WHERE table_schema = 'case_app'
      AND table_name = 'enforcement_case'
      AND column_name = 'risk_score'
);
```

### View Verification

```sql id="ylc1mg"
SELECT *
FROM case_app.v_case_summary
LIMIT 1;
```

### Function Verification

```sql id="7lk36d"
SELECT case_app.fn_normalize_code(' open ') AS normalized_code;
```

### Trigger Verification

```sql id="74js7r"
SELECT trigger_name
FROM information_schema.triggers
WHERE event_object_schema = 'case_app'
  AND event_object_table = 'enforcement_case';
```

### Constraint Verification

```sql id="yrx12a"
SELECT
    conname,
    convalidated
FROM pg_constraint
WHERE connamespace = 'case_app'::regnamespace
ORDER BY conname;
```

---

## 30. App-Level Verification

### MyBatis Smoke

```text id="xuhni2"
- findCaseById
- insertCase
- updateCaseStatus
- findCaseSummary
- call function/procedure if used
```

### Jersey API Smoke

```text id="fpx19a"
GET /cases/{id}
POST /cases
PATCH /cases/{id}/status
GET /cases/{id}/summary
```

### Camunda Smoke

```text id="i2qcqy"
- worker can fetch and complete a job
- mapper query used by worker works
- status/reference values are recognized
- no new incidents caused by schema change
```

### Observability

```text id="jg6udc"
- DB error logs
- app error rate
- p95/p99 latency
- deadlocks
- lock wait
- replication lag
- Camunda incidents
- failed external tasks/jobs
```

---

## 31. Incident SOP

```text id="2k2ftl"
If migration incident occurs:

1. Declare incident.
2. Freeze further deploys.
3. Assign incident commander.
4. Capture facts, not assumptions.
5. Identify current DB/app state.
6. Decide containment:
   - stop app rollout
   - rollback app
   - pause backfill
   - disable feature flag
   - block traffic
7. Decide recovery:
   - fix-forward migration
   - app patch
   - data repair
   - tested rollback
   - restore/PITR
8. Verify recovery.
9. Write postmortem.
10. Add prevention gate/test.
```

---

## 32. Failure Communication Template

```text id="d6wt5g"
Status:
  Migration failed / partially applied / app deploy failed.

Impact:
  No user impact / degraded endpoint / write path affected / workflow affected.

Current State:
  DB schema version:
  App version:
  Migration job:
  Failed step:
  Error:

Decision:
  Stop rollout / rollback app / fix-forward DB / restore.

Owner:
  Incident commander:
  DB owner:
  Service owner:

Next Verification:
  Smoke test:
  Monitoring:
```

---

## 33. Postmortem Template

```md id="eklhil"
# DB Migration Incident Postmortem

## Summary
What happened?

## Timeline
- T0 migration started
- T1 failure observed
- T2 rollout stopped
- T3 recovery applied
- T4 service verified

## Impact
- User impact
- API impact
- Workflow/Camunda impact
- Data impact

## Root Cause
Technical and process cause.

## Detection
How was it detected?

## Resolution
What fixed it?

## What Went Well
...

## What Went Wrong
...

## Prevention
- New CI gate
- New integration test
- New runbook step
- New review checklist item

## Follow-Up Actions
| Action | Owner | Due |
|---|---|---|
```

---

## 34. Practical Recovery Examples

### Example A - View Bug

Problem:

```text id="ufo5zl"
v_case_summary missing status_label alias.
MyBatis mapper fails.
```

Fix-forward:

```sql id="3e59tw"
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW case_app.v_case_summary AS
SELECT
    c.id,
    c.case_number,
    c.status,
    sr.label AS status_label
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
JOIN case_app.case_status_reference sr
    ON sr.code = c.status;
```

Then rerun mapper smoke.

---

### Example B - Bad Seed

Problem:

```text id="2j3chs"
Permission CASE_DELETE accidentally granted to CASE_AGENT.
```

Fix-forward:

```sql id="d7kpxs"
DELETE FROM security.role_permission
WHERE role_code = 'CASE_AGENT'
  AND permission_code = 'CASE_DELETE';
```

Then audit access and archive approval.

---

### Example C - Constraint Validation Failed

Problem:

```text id="62hf4k"
FK validation failed because orphan status exists.
```

Find:

```sql id="ea4tvu"
SELECT c.status, COUNT(*)
FROM case_app.enforcement_case c
LEFT JOIN case_app.case_status_reference r
    ON r.code = c.status
WHERE r.code IS NULL
GROUP BY c.status;
```

Repair data or seed missing reference, then rerun validation.

---

### Example D - Backfill Wrong Values

Problem:

```text id="ebja4d"
risk_score backfill used wrong default.
```

Corrective repair:

```sql id="q6kws9"
UPDATE case_app.enforcement_case
SET risk_score = case_app.fn_calculate_risk_score(id)
WHERE risk_score = 0
  AND created_at < TIMESTAMPTZ '2026-07-13 00:00:00+07';
```

Only do this with audited criteria and validation.

---

## 35. Final Production Migration Checklist

| Phase | Checklist |
|---|---|
| Before | PR approved, staging passed, backup confirmed, runbook ready. |
| Start | Deployment lock acquired, migration job started, logs captured. |
| During | Monitor locks, errors, runtime, replication, DB CPU. |
| Success | Verify metadata, run schema smoke, deploy app, run API smoke. |
| Failure | Stop rollout, capture state, classify, decide recovery. |
| After | Archive evidence, monitor, close runbook, create follow-ups. |

---

## 36. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-Pattern | Why Dangerous | Better |
|---|---|---|
| “Migration failed, just rerun” | Can worsen partial state. | Inspect DB/tool metadata first. |
| Blind `flyway repair` | Can hide checksum/source drift. | Root-cause and approval first. |
| Manual edit metadata table | Corrupts audit/history. | Incident path only. |
| Rollback assumed safe | Many DB changes are irreversible. | Test rollback or prefer fix-forward. |
| No backup confirmation | Restore impossible when needed. | Confirm restore method before high-risk migration. |
| No app compatibility test | Old/new pods fail during rollout. | Old app/new schema smoke for risky changes. |
| No evidence collection | Incident/postmortem weak. | Archive logs/status/report. |
| App rollback after destructive DB change | Old app may not run. | Use expand/contract. |
| Backfill without progress | Cannot resume or explain state. | Progress table + batch log. |
| Constraint added without validation plan | Production writes fail. | Data repair then staged validation. |

---

## 37. Ringkasan Seri 18

| Area | Kesimpulan |
|---|---|
| Production migration | Harus punya runbook, owner, approval, evidence. |
| Backup | High-risk migration wajib punya restore/PITR/snapshot confirmation. |
| Failure handling | Stop rollout, capture state, classify failure, then decide recovery. |
| Flyway repair | Recovery command, bukan rutinitas. |
| Liquibase rollback | Berguna jika rollback defined, tested, dan safe. |
| Metadata tables | Jangan edit manual kecuali incident path approved. |
| Rollback vs roll-forward | Roll-forward sering lebih aman untuk production DB. |
| Backfill failure | Pause, inspect progress, resume idempotently. |
| App failure | App rollback hanya aman jika DB masih compatible. |
| Evidence | Logs, tool state, smoke result, approval, release version harus disimpan. |
| Post-migration | Verify DB, MyBatis, Jersey API, Camunda, observability. |
| Postmortem | Tambahkan gate/test/runbook agar incident tidak terulang. |
